--- PAGE 2 ---
Course File: 01_AWS_Cloud.md
Course: AWS Cloud
1. Beginner Level (10 Questions)
**Q1. A startup wants to deploy a web application without managing servers, but needs to run custom Docker containers.
Which AWS service should they choose?**
A. Amazon EC2
B. AWS Lambda
C. AWS Fargate with Amazon ECS
D. Amazon Lightsail
**Q2. You store 10 TB of financial reports that must be retained for 7 years for compliance, accessed less than once a year,
with retrieval in 12 hours acceptable. Which S3 storage class is MOST cost-effective?**
A. S3 Standard
B. S3 Glacier Flexible Retrieval
C. S3 Glacier Deep Archive
D. S3 Intelligent-Tiering
**Q3. A company wants to isolate its production VPC traffic from the internet while still allowing EC2 instances to download
security updates. What should they implement?**
A. Internet Gateway with restrictive Security Group
B. NAT Gateway in a public subnet and route private subnet traffic to it
C. VPN Gateway
D. Place all instances in public subnets and disable Elastic IPs
**Q4. An IAM user needs permission to start/stop EC2 instances tagged with Env=Dev but not production. What is the best
approach?**
A. Give AmazonEC2FullAccess managed policy
B. Create a custom IAM policy with ec2:StartInstances, ec2:StopInstances and Condition aws:ResourceTag/Env = Dev
C. Put Dev and Prod in separate AWS accounts and use SCPs
D. Use IAM role with AdministratorAccess and rely on MFA
**Q5. Your RDS MySQL instance is experiencing high read traffic. You need to scale reads without affecting the primary.
What should you do?**
A. Increase the instance class to db.r6g.4xlarge
B. Enable Multi-AZ
C. Create RDS Read Replicas
D. Move data to DynamoDB
**Q6. You have an Application Load Balancer (ALB) in front of 3 EC2 instances. A user reports that their shopping cart
empties on every request. What is the likely fix?**
A. Enable Sticky Sessions (session affinity) on the ALB target group, or better, externalize session to ElastiCache/DynamoDB
B. Enable Cross-Zone Load Balancing
C. Switch ALB to NLB
D. Attach a WAF to the ALB
**Q7. Which AWS service provides a managed, highly available DNS service with routing policies like latency-based and
geolocation routing?**
A. AWS Direct Connect
B. Amazon Route 53
C. AWS Global Accelerator

--- PAGE 3 ---
D. Amazon CloudFront
**Q8. You accidentally deleted an S3 bucket containing critical data, with no versioning enabled. How could you have
prevented permanent loss?**
A. Enable S3 Versioning and MFA Delete, and use S3 Object Lock for compliance
B. Enable S3 Transfer Acceleration
C. Enable S3 Access Points
D. Use S3 Replication alone
**Q9. A developer wants to run code in response to an S3 object upload without provisioning servers and only paying for
execution time. Which service fits?**
A. Amazon EC2 Auto Scaling
B. AWS Lambda triggered by S3 Event Notification
C. Amazon ECS on EC2
D. AWS Batch
**Q10. Your organization needs to centrally manage billing and apply guardrails to prevent use of certain regions across 20
AWS accounts. Which service combination should you use?**
A. AWS Organizations with Service Control Policies (SCPs) and consolidated billing
B. IAM Groups with resource policies
C. AWS Control Tower alone without Organizations
D. AWS Config alone
Beginner Answer Key & Explanations
**Q1 Answer: C - AWS Fargate with Amazon ECS**
Fargate is serverless compute for containers that eliminates EC2 management while allowing custom Docker images.
Lambda does not natively support long-running containers with arbitrary ports without extra constraints.
**Q2 Answer: C - S3 Glacier Deep Archive**
Deep Archive is the lowest-cost class for long-term retention (7-10 years) when retrieval within 12 hours is acceptable.
Flexible Retrieval is more expensive but faster, while Standard would cost ~10x more.
**Q3 Answer: B - NAT Gateway in a public subnet and route private subnet traffic to it**
A NAT Gateway allows outbound internet access for private subnets while blocking inbound initiation. An Internet Gateway
alone would make subnets public, violating isolation.
**Q4 Answer: B - Create a custom IAM policy with Condition on ResourceTag**
ABAC using aws:ResourceTag allows fine-grained control to limit actions to resources with specific tags. Giving FullAccess
violates least privilege.
**Q5 Answer: C - Create RDS Read Replicas**
Read Replicas offload read queries using asynchronous replication from the primary DB. Multi-AZ is for high
availability/failover, not read scaling.
**Q6 Answer: A - Enable Sticky Sessions or externalize session**
ALB by default distributes requests round-robin, breaking in-memory sessions. Sticky sessions fix it short-term, but
externalizing session state to ElastiCache is the resilient best practice.
**Q7 Answer: B - Amazon Route 53**
Route 53 is AWS's managed DNS with advanced routing policies including latency-based, geolocation, failover, and weighted
routing. CloudFront is CDN, Global Accelerator is anycast network acceleration.
**Q8 Answer: A - Enable S3 Versioning and MFA Delete, and use S3 Object Lock**
Versioning retains previous versions after deletion, MFA Delete requires MFA for permanent deletion, and Object Lock
prevents deletion via governance. Replication alone would replicate the delete.
**Q9 Answer: B - AWS Lambda triggered by S3 Event Notification**
Lambda is event-driven, serverless, and integrates natively with S3 events to process objects. EC2 and ECS require server
management and are not billed per 100ms execution.

--- PAGE 4 ---
**Q10 Answer: A - AWS Organizations with Service Control Policies (SCPs) and consolidated billing**
Organizations provides consolidated billing and OU hierarchies, while SCPs enforce permission guardrails like denying
regions across accounts. IAM Groups only work within a single account.
2. Intermediate Level (10 Questions)
**Q11. You have an S3 bucket receiving 5,000 PUTs/sec with key prefix logs/year=2024/month=11/day=15/hour=09/... where
all keys share same prefix. You see 503 SlowDown errors. What architectural change in 2024-2026 best solves this? (Note:
S3 now has no prefix partition limits since 2018, but contention still occurs)**
A. Add random hex prefix to keys
B. The bottleneck is no longer prefix partitioning but request rate to a single partition per prefix is still mitigated by using S3
Express One Zone with directory buckets for high TPS per prefix
C. Enable Transfer Acceleration
D. Switch to multipart upload
**Q12. An EC2 Auto Scaling Group behind ALB sees uneven CPU, new instances fail health checks during deployment. You
are using rolling updates with ELB health check type but instance-level 2-minute app warmup. What should you configure?**
A. Increase ALB health check interval to 5 minutes
B. Use Lifecycle Hook + warmup pool and increase ASG health check grace period to 180s
C. Disable ELB health checks
D. Use all-at-once deployment
**Q13. You need to connect a VPC to S3 without traversing the public internet and without using a NAT Gateway, with policy
limiting bucket access. Which option?**
A. Internet Gateway
B. NAT Gateway
C. Gateway VPC Endpoint for S3 with endpoint policy
D. Interface Endpoint for S3 using PrivateLink
**Q14. A Lambda function in VPC needs to access DynamoDB and Secrets Manager. It times out when trying to reach
DynamoDB. What is MOST likely and optimal fix?**
A. Attach Internet Gateway to Lambda VPC
B. Create Gateway VPC Endpoint for DynamoDB and Interface Endpoint for Secrets Manager, ensure Lambda subnet route
tables and SG allow it
C. Put Lambda in public subnet
D. Increase Lambda timeout to 15 minutes
**Q15. You are designing cross-region active-active serverless API. You have API Gateway + Lambda + DynamoDB Global
Tables in us-east-1 and eu-west-1. How should you route users to lowest latency and handle failover automatically?**
A. Use Route 53 latency-based routing with health checks to API Gateway regional endpoints
B. Use ALB with cross-region target groups
C. Use CloudFront with origin failover and latency metrics
D. Hardcode region in client
**Q16. Your EKS cluster's CoreDNS pods are crashing with OOMKilled. Node CPU is high due to many short-lived Jobs
spawning. You use Cluster Autoscaler. What is the best combination fix?**
A. Increase CoreDNS memory limits, use Fargate for CoreDNS, and implement Karpenter with consolidation + use proper
PodDisruptionBudgets and resource requests for Jobs
B. Delete CoreDNS
C. Switch to ECS
D. Overprovision nodes by 200%
**Q17. You need to enforce that all EBS volumes are encrypted and that all S3 buckets block public access across entire
organization. Where do you enforce preventatively BEFORE creation?**
A. AWS Config Rules only

--- PAGE 5 ---
B. SCPs with conditions ec2:Encrypted and s3:x-amz-acl + AWS Config for detection, plus enable EBS encryption by default
at account level
C. IAM policy alone
D. CloudTrail
**Q18. An RDS Postgres instance on gp3 runs out of IOPS during nightly batch. You see WriteLatency spikes. Storage is
1000 GiB with 3000 IOPS provisioned. Batch needs 12000 IOPS for 1 hour. Most cost-effective 2024-2025 solution?**
A. Change to io2 with 12000 IOPS permanently
B. Modify gp3 to provision 12000 IOPS and 500 MiB/s throughput before batch, then scale down after (gp3 allows
independent IOPS/throughput scaling)
C. Add Read Replica
D. Migrate to Aurora Serverless v1
**Q19. You have CloudFront distribution fronting S3 + ALB origins. Users request /api/* should go to ALB with caching
disabled, /static/* to S3 with caching. How to implement securely with Origin Access?**
A. One behavior only
B. Two cache behaviors: /api/* with Cache Policy CachingDisabled and Origin Request Policy AllViewer, ALB origin; /static/*
with CachingOptimized, S3 origin using Origin Access Control (OAC)
C. Use two CloudFront distributions
D. Use S3 static website hosting with public bucket
**Q20. A company needs to capture all VPC flow logs, DNS query logs (Route 53 Resolver), and CloudTrail events in central
security account with near-real-time detection. Architecture?**
A. Enable VPC Flow Logs to S3 in each account; manual review
B. Use AWS Organizations + enable organization trail for CloudTrail to central S3, centralize VPC Flow Logs to Kinesis Data
Firehose -> S3 + Athena, Route53 Resolver query logging to S3/OpenSearch, and Security Lake or OpenSearch +
GuardDuty
C. Use CloudWatch Logs only in each account
D. Disable logging to save cost
Intermediate Answer Key & Explanations
**Q11 Answer: B - Use S3 Express One Zone directory buckets for high TPS per prefix**
While classic S3 bucket prefix limits were removed, extremely high per-prefix TPS (like 5k PUTs/sec sequential) can still
cause throttling in general-purpose buckets. S3 Express One Zone directory buckets provide 10x higher performance and
single-digit ms latency for such workloads.
**Q12 Answer: B - Lifecycle Hook + warmup pool and increase ASG health check grace period**
ELB health checks start immediately, so short grace period causes new instances to be terminated before app starts. A
warmup pool and 180s+ grace period allow JIT compilation and cache warmup to succeed.
**Q13 Answer: C - Gateway VPC Endpoint for S3 with endpoint policy**
Gateway endpoints for S3/DynamoDB are free, route via prefix list, and support endpoint policies to restrict bucket access.
Interface endpoints for S3 exist but incur hourly cost and are for niche PrivateLink needs.
**Q14 Answer: B - Create Gateway Endpoint for DynamoDB and Interface Endpoint for Secrets Manager**
Lambda in VPC loses internet by default; it needs VPC endpoints to reach AWS services privately. Gateway for DynamoDB
(free) and Interface Endpoint (PrivateLink) for Secrets Manager avoids NAT cost and timeouts.
**Q15 Answer: A - Route 53 latency-based routing with health checks**
Latency-based routing directs users to lowest latency region with health checks for automatic failover at DNS layer. Global
Accelerator is also valid, but Route 53 + regional API Gateway custom domains is native and widely used.
**Q16 Answer: A - Increase CoreDNS limits, use Karpenter, set resource requests**
CoreDNS OOM due to unreserved burstable nodes and Job spikes is common; fixing requests/limits, using Karpenter for
faster consolidation/scaling, and PDBs ensures CoreDNS stability. Overprovisioning alone is wasteful.
**Q17 Answer: B - SCPs + EBS default encryption + Config**

--- PAGE 6 ---
SCPs can Deny ec2:CreateVolume when ec2:Encrypted:false and Deny S3 Put* with public ACLs, preventing non-compliant
creation at org level. AWS Config detects drift, and account-level EBS encryption by default ensures compliance.
**Q18 Answer: B - Modify gp3 to provision IOPS/throughput temporarily then scale down**
gp3 decouples IOPS and throughput from storage size (unlike gp2), allowing you to provision 12k IOPS for batch window and
revert. This is far cheaper than permanent io2 provisioning for a 1-hour workload.
**Q19 Answer: B - Two cache behaviors with CachingDisabled for /api and OAC for S3**
Separate behaviors allow path-based routing with different cache policies. OAC is the 2023+ replacement for OAI for secure
S3 origin access, and disabling caching for API avoids serving stale dynamic responses.
**Q20 Answer: B - Org Trail + centralized flow logs to Firehose + Resolver logs + Security Lake**
A multi-account security baseline uses Organization CloudTrail, centralized VPC Flow Logs via Kinesis Firehose to central
account (or S3 managed by Security Lake), and Resolver query logs aggregated for detection. Security Lake normalizes to
OCSF.
3. Advanced Level (10 Questions)
**Q21. You are designing a multi-tenant SaaS control plane on EKS that uses IAM Roles for Service Accounts (IRSA) for
tenant isolation. A tenant's pod is able to assume another tenant's IAM role. What is the most likely misconfiguration in trust
policy?**
A. Trust policy uses StringLike: aud: sts.amazonaws.com but missing StringEquals: sub = system:serviceaccount:tenant-a:sa
B. Using EKS Pod Identity instead of IRSA
C. Using instance profile
D. Using OIDC provider URL incorrectly
**Q22. You have DynamoDB table with 100k WCU, using on-demand bursts. Application does batch hot partition write to
same partition key for 10 minutes (e.g., tenantId=GLOBAL). Throttling persists even with DAX. What advanced pattern fixes
this without increasing WCU?**
A. Increase WCU to 200k
B. Use SQS buffer + randomized suffix (write-sharding) with aggregation via Streams + Lambda: write to
GLOBAL#rand(1-20) and aggregate reads
C. Use Strongly consistent reads
D. Switch to RDS
**Q23. Your organization uses AWS Direct Connect + Transit Gateway + Direct Connect Gateway. You have 50 VPCs and
on-premises ASN 65000. You advertise 200 on-prem routes over Direct Connect to AWS. You hit route quota on Transit
Gateway route table. Optimal fix?**
A. Increase TGW route quota via ticket to 10000 (still not enough scalable long-term)
B. Summarize on-prem routes to aggregate CIDRs before advertising via BGP, use route filtering, and leverage Transit
Gateway Connect or segments
C. Create more Transit Gateways
D. Move to VPN only
**Q24. A Step Functions Express workflow orchestrates API calls for financial transaction requiring exactly-once semantics
and idempotency across retries. Step Functions retries a Lambda that called third-party payment API, causing duplicate
charge. What design ensures idempotency?**
A. Rely on Express workflow exactly-once
B. Lambda generates idempotency key (transactionId) stored in DynamoDB with conditional write (attribute_not_exists) and
passes to payment API; check before calling external API; use DynamoDB Transactions
C. Increase retry backoff
D. Switch to Standard workflow alone
**Q25. You run Aurora PostgreSQL Global Database with primary in us-east-1, secondary in eu-west-1 with <1 sec lag. You
need RTO <1 min, RPO <5 sec for regional failure, and to prevent split-brain writes. What configuration?**
A. Use managed failover with Aurora Global Database Failover, enable Global Database Write Forwarding disabled for
secondary, use Route53 health checks + cluster endpoint, ensure application uses idempotent writes
B. Promote secondary manually after crash

--- PAGE 7 ---
C. Use snapshot copy
D. Use DMS replication
**Q26. An account's S3 data lake has 500M objects. Athena queries scanning entire bucket are expensive. You need to
provide sub-second dashboard queries for recent 30 days, with row-level security per team, while still keeping historical
Parquet. Which stack in 2025?**
A. Keep Athena only
B. Use S3 Tables (Apache Iceberg) with compaction + S3 Metadata + Lake Formation row filters + Athena v3, and put hot
data into CloudWatch Metrics
C. Use S3 Tables with automatic compaction, Athena + Lake Formation row-level security, and Amazon QuickSight SPICE or
Redshift Serverless or Athena with partition projection + result cache, plus materialized view via Glue ETL job aggregating
last 30 days
D. Move all to DynamoDB
**Q27. You use AWS KMS multi-Region keys for encryption across regions. After rotating primary key in us-east-1, data
encrypted in eu-west-1 can't be decrypted. What did you miss?**
A. Rotation of multi-Region primary does not automatically rotate replicas; you must manually rotate replica keys or use
primary to create new primary key material and sync; also need to ensure alias points and application fetched new key ID
B. KMS rotation breaks old ciphertext
C. Multi-Region keys don't support rotation
D. Need to re-encrypt all data immediately
**Q28. Your AWS Lambda function with 10GB RAM, 6 vCPU, Graviton2, processing video, cold start 8 seconds. Image is
5GB container. You need P99 latency <1 sec with sporadic 1000 concurrent invocations. Cost-optimized architecture?**
A. Increase to 12GB
B. Use Provisioned Concurrency + SnapStart? (SnapStart only Java) -> Use Lambda Provisioned Concurrency with
optimized 2GB image (multi-stage) using Lambda's new faster image pull + Tiered caching, plus Lambda Function URL with
response streaming, and consider AWS Fargate or Compute Savings
C. Correct answer: Use Lambda Provisioned Concurrency for baseline + optimize container multi-stage to <1GB, use
Lambda Arm64, enable Lambda SnapStart not applicable for container, so use Provisioned Concurrency + Elasticache, also
consider moving to Fargate for sustained workloads
D. Use EC2 only
**Q29. You must achieve 99.99% avail SLA for API across two regions, with no DNS propagation dependency for failover
(needs <30 sec). Traffic must be anycasted. Which AWS setup?**
A. Route 53 failover routing
B. AWS Global Accelerator with endpoint groups in two regions (ALB + health checks), client affinity, instant failover at edge
C. CloudFront alone
D. Transit Gateway peering
**Q30. A CI/CD pipeline builds container and deploys to EKS. Security requires: no long-lived AWS credentials in GitHub,
scanned images with no critical CVEs, signed images verified in cluster, and least-privilege IAM for deploy. Modern 2025
solution?**
A. Store AWS_ACCESS_KEY in GitHub Secrets
B. Use GitHub OIDC federation with IAM role (no keys), ECR with enhanced scanning (Inspector), AWS Signer + Notation,
and Kyverno/Policy Controller for signature verification, deploy via ArgoCD with IRSA Pod Identity
C. Use Jenkins with static credentials
D. Deploy directly from developer laptop
Advanced Answer Key & Explanations
**Q21 Answer: A - Trust policy missing sub condition**
IRSA relies on OIDC token's sub claim to bind SA to role. If trust policy only checks aud, any SA in cluster can assume role.
Fix requires StringEquals: sub = system:serviceaccount:<ns>:<sa> and aud: sts.amazonaws.com.
**Q22 Answer: B - Write-sharding with randomized suffix + Streams aggregation**

--- PAGE 8 ---
Hot partition on single PK exceeds 1k WCU per partition limit (and 3k RCU). Sharding write across 20 logical partitions
spreads throughput. Reads aggregate via DynamoDB Streams and eventual consistency, avoiding throttling without
over-provisioning.
**Q23 Answer: B - Summarize routes to aggregate CIDRs, use filtering**
Transit Gateway route tables default quota 10k routes; advertising 200 more-specific /24s is inefficient. Best practice is
summarize on-prem to supernets (e.g., /16), filter at DX GW, and segment via TGW routing domains/peering for scale.
**Q24 Answer: B - Lambda idempotency via DynamoDB conditional write**
Step Functions Express is at-least-once for external calls. Exactly-once must be implemented in business logic using
idempotency key with DynamoDB conditional write and storing payment gateway response to prevent duplicate charges on
retry.
**Q25 Answer: A - Managed failover with write forwarding disabled**
Aurora Global DB managed failover achieves <1 min RTO with data lag typically <1 sec for RPO. Write forwarding must be off
for secondary to avoid split-brain, and app must be aware of cluster endpoints and idempotent recovery after DNS.
**Q26 Answer: C - S3 Tables + compaction + Lake Formation + materialized view**
S3 Tables (Iceberg) provides ACID compaction and time travel, reducing small files pain at 500M objects. Combined with
Lake Formation row filters for multi-tenancy and SPICE/Redshift Serverless for sub-second BI is the modern lakehouse
pattern.
**Q27 Answer: A - Replica keys don't auto-rotate**
Multi-Region primary rotation only rotates primary key material; replicas must be rotated independently via ReplicateKey
update or manual rotation. Applications caching key ID will also fail if not refreshed to alias.
**Q28 Answer: C - Provisioned Concurrency + optimized image, or move to Fargate**
Large container image worsens cold start; Provisioned Concurrency keeps execution environments warm for P99 <1s. Real
fix is multi-stage build to shrink to <500MB and evaluate Fargate for sustained video workloads rather than Lambda.
**Q29 Answer: B - AWS Global Accelerator with endpoint groups**
Global Accelerator uses AWS global edge anycast IPs, independent of DNS TTL/caching, providing <30s failover via health
checks at edge. Route 53 failover depends on DNS TTL (60-300s) and client caching.
**Q30 Answer: B - GitHub OIDC + ECR Inspector + Signer + Policy Controller + ArgoCD with IRSA**
This is the 2025 secure software supply chain: OIDC federation eliminates long-lived keys, Inspector scans CVEs, Signer +
Notation signs OCI artifacts, admission controller verifies signature in cluster, and ArgoCD with short-lived IRSA reduces blast
radius.

Course File: 02_Azure.md
Course: Microsoft Azure
1. Beginner Level (10 Questions)
**Q1: What is Microsoft Azure primarily classified as?**
A) A cloud computing platform offering IaaS, PaaS, and SaaS services
B) A virtualization software for on-premises servers only
C) A database management system
D) An operating system for mobile devices
**Q2: In Azure Resource Manager (ARM), what is the primary purpose of a Resource Group?**
A) To group resources that share the same lifecycle, permissions, and policies for deployment and management
B) To provide physical isolation of servers in a datacenter
C) To automatically backup resources
D) To define network firewall rules
**Q3: You deploy a VM in Azure. Your company requires 99.99% SLA for VM uptime within a single region without using
Availability Sets. Which feature should you use?**
A) Deploy VMs across multiple Availability Zones within the same region
B) Deploy VMs in a single Availability Set with 2 fault domains
C) Deploy VMs in two different Azure regions
D) Enable Azure Backup on a single VM
**Q4: Which Azure storage service is best suited for storing unstructured object data like images, videos, and backups
accessible via REST API?**
A) Azure Blob Storage
B) Azure File Storage
C) Azure Queue Storage
D) Azure Disk Storage
**Q5: A developer wants to run small pieces of code in response to events (e.g., a file uploaded to Blob) without managing
any servers or paying for idle time. Which service should they use?**
A) Azure Functions (Consumption Plan)
B) Azure Virtual Machines
C) Azure Kubernetes Service (AKS)
D) Azure App Service Environment v3
**Q6: Your organization has 100 developers. You need to manage identities and provide SSO to thousands of SaaS apps like
M365 and Salesforce. What is the Azure service for this?**
A) Microsoft Entra ID (formerly Azure Active Directory)
B) Microsoft Entra Domain Services
C) Azure Key Vault
D) Azure Role-Based Access Control (RBAC) alone
**Q7: What does the concept of a Virtual Network (VNet) in Azure provide?**
A) An isolated, private network in the cloud to securely run VMs and services with control over IP ranges, subnets, and
routing
B) A public internet gateway with unlimited bandwidth
C) A content delivery network for caching
D) A DNS service for internet domains only

--- PAGE 10 ---
**Q8: Which pricing model allows you to pay only for what you use with no upfront commitment, ideal for dev/test?**
A) Pay-As-You-Go
B) Reserved Instances (1 or 3 years)
C) Spot VMs only
D) Enterprise Dev/Test license
**Q9: You need to host a simple web app with built-in CI/CD, scaling, and custom domains without managing OS patches.
Which PaaS option is most appropriate?**
A) Azure App Service
B) Azure Virtual Machine Scale Sets
C) Azure Bare Metal Infrastructure
D) Azure ExpressRoute
**Q10: What is the main difference between Azure Availability Set and Availability Zone?**
A) Availability Sets protect within a datacenter (fault/update domains), while Availability Zones are physically separate
datacenters in a region
B) Availability Sets are across regions, Zones are within a datacenter
C) They are identical, just different names
D) Availability Sets are for storage, Zones are for compute
Beginner Answer Key & Explanations
**Q1 Answer: A - A cloud computing platform offering IaaS, PaaS, and SaaS services**
Azure is Microsoft's public cloud platform providing over 200+ services across infrastructure, platform, and software as a
service. It is not just virtualization or a single product.
**Q2 Answer: A - To group resources that share the same lifecycle, permissions, and policies for deployment and
management**
Resource Groups are logical containers for resources that should be managed together, deployed, updated, or deleted as a
unit. They also scope RBAC and Azure Policy.
**Q3 Answer: A - Deploy VMs across multiple Availability Zones within the same region**
Availability Zones offer 99.99% VM SLA when using 2+ VMs in 2+ zones, as each zone is independent power, cooling, and
networking. Availability Sets only provide 99.95% SLA and are legacy within a single datacenter.
**Q4 Answer: A - Azure Blob Storage**
Blob Storage is optimized for massive amounts of unstructured data via REST, with tiers Hot/Cool/Cold/Archive. File Storage
provides SMB file shares, not REST object access.
**Q5 Answer: A - Azure Functions (Consumption Plan)**
Functions is serverless compute where you run event-driven code and pay per execution (100ms increments) with automatic
scale to zero. VMs and AKS require server/cluster management.
**Q6 Answer: A - Microsoft Entra ID (formerly Azure Active Directory)**
Microsoft Entra ID is the cloud identity and access management service providing SSO, MFA, and Conditional Access for
users. Key Vault is for secrets, not user identity.
**Q7 Answer: A - An isolated, private network in the cloud to securely run VMs and services with control over IP ranges,
subnets, and routing**
VNet is the fundamental building block for private networking in Azure, analogous to your on-prem network. It enables you to
segment, filter traffic, and connect to on-prem via VPN/ExpressRoute.
**Q8 Answer: A - Pay-As-You-Go**
Pay-As-You-Go has no upfront cost and per-second billing, perfect for unpredictable or short-term workloads. Reserved
Instances give discount but require 1-3 year commitment.
**Q9 Answer: A - Azure App Service**
App Service is a fully managed PaaS for web apps, APIs, and mobile backends handling OS patching, load balancing, and
autoscale. VM Scale Sets is IaaS requiring OS management.

--- PAGE 11 ---
**Q10 Answer: A - Availability Sets protect within a datacenter (fault/update domains), while Availability Zones are physically
separate datacenters in a region**
An Availability Zone is a unique physical location with independent infrastructure, providing higher resiliency than Availability
Sets. Availability Sets distribute VMs across isolated hardware within one datacenter.
2. Intermediate Level (10 Questions)
**Q11: You have a Hub-Spoke topology with Hub VNet 10.0.0.0/16 peered to Spoke1 and Spoke2. VMs in Spoke1 cannot
reach VMs in Spoke2. What is required?**
A) Enable "Allow forwarded traffic" and "Allow gateway transit" plus a Network Virtual Appliance or Azure Route Server in
Hub, or peer spokes directly
B) VNet peering is transitive by default, so just wait 10 minutes
C) Create NSG allowing all traffic
D) Enable Azure Bastion
**Q12: An app running on Azure VM needs to access Azure Key Vault without storing any credentials in code or config. What
is the secure, recommended approach?**
A) Enable System-Assigned Managed Identity on the VM and grant it Key Vault Secrets User role via RBAC
B) Store Client ID/Secret in appsettings.json
C) Use a Shared Access Signature (SAS) token hardcoded
D) Open Key Vault firewall to 0.0.0.0/0
**Q13: You need to enforce that all storage accounts in subscription "Prod" must use Private Endpoints and deny public
network access. How do you implement this at scale?**
A) Create Azure Policy with Deny effect auditing publicNetworkAccess and networkAcls.defaultAction, assign at subscription
scope with remediation
B) Manually configure each storage account firewall
C) Use Azure Blueprints to copy settings
D) Send an email to owners
**Q14: Your team uses Bicep for IaC. You have a module that should deploy only if environment is "prod". How do you
implement this?**
A) Use if (environment == 'prod') condition on the module/resource in Bicep
B) Use a separate subscription
C) Bicep does not support conditions, use ARM JSON copy loops
D) Delete the resource manually after deployment
**Q15: You have an Azure Load Balancer (Standard, Layer 4) and an Application Gateway. When should you choose
Application Gateway over Load Balancer?**
A) When you need Layer 7 routing, SSL termination, WAF, and path-based routing for HTTP/S
B) When you need ultra-low latency TCP/UDP load balancing
C) When you need to load balance across regions
D) When you need to balance non-HTTP traffic on port 22
**Q16: An AKS cluster needs to pull images from ACR securely without Kubernetes Secrets. The most secure, managed way
is:**
A) Attach ACR to AKS with az aks update --attach-acr which creates role assignment for kubelet Managed Identity with
AcrPull role
B) Create a service principal and store password in YAML
C) Make ACR public anonymously
D) Use imagePullSecrets with admin user enabled
**Q17: You need point-in-time restore for an Azure SQL Database up to 35 days and long-term retention for 10 years. How?**
A) PITR is built-in (7-35 days configurable) and configure Long-Term Retention (LTR) policy on the server for
weekly/monthly/yearly backups to Blob

--- PAGE 12 ---
B) Enable Azure Backup vault only
C) Use geo-restore only
D) Export BACPAC files daily
**Q18: What is the difference between Azure RBAC roles "Contributor" and "User Access Administrator"?**
A) Contributor can manage resources but not manage access; User Access Administrator can manage role assignments but
not resources
B) They are identical
C) Contributor can only read
D) User Access Administrator can delete subscriptions
**Q19: You deployed App Service with 2 deployment slots: Production and Staging. You want zero-downtime deployment
with warming up before swapping. Best practice?**
A) Deploy to Staging, validate, enable auto-swap or manually swap with preview, using
WEBSITE_SWAP_WARMUP_PING_PATH
B) Deploy directly to Production and restart
C) Stop Production slot before deploy
D) Use FTP only
**Q20: Your on-prem network 192.168.0.0/16 overlaps with Azure VNet 192.168.0.0/16 and you need hybrid connectivity via
VPN. What must you do?**
A) Re-address the Azure VNet to non-overlapping range (e.g., 10.0.0.0/16) before establishing Site-to-Site VPN, as
overlapping ranges cannot route
B) Enable NAT on VPN gateway to keep overlapping ranges
C) Use ExpressRoute to ignore IP conflicts
D) Just enable VNet peering
Intermediate Answer Key & Explanations
**Q11 Answer: A - Enable "Allow forwarded traffic" and "Allow gateway transit" plus a Network Virtual Appliance or Azure
Route Server in Hub, or peer spokes directly**
VNet peering is non-transitive: spoke-to-spoke communication doesn't flow through hub by default. You need transitive
routing via NVA/firewall in hub and user-defined routes, or create direct peerings between spokes.
**Q12 Answer: A - Enable System-Assigned Managed Identity on the VM and grant it Key Vault Secrets User role via RBAC**
Managed Identity provides an Entra ID identity for the Azure resource, eliminating credential storage. Azure AD authenticates
the VM automatically to Key Vault using RBAC or access policies.
**Q13 Answer: A - Create Azure Policy with Deny effect auditing publicNetworkAccess and networkAcls.defaultAction, assign
at subscription scope with remediation**
Azure Policy enforces governance at scale with Deny/AuditIfNotExists effects, and can auto-remediate non-compliant
resources. Manual configuration doesn't scale and doesn't prevent drift.
**Q14 Answer: A - Use if (environment == 'prod') condition on the module/resource in Bicep**
Bicep supports conditional deployment via if keyword at resource, module, and loop level, which translates to ARM condition.
This enables environment-aware infrastructure as code.
**Q15 Answer: A - When you need Layer 7 routing, SSL termination, WAF, and path-based routing for HTTP/S**
Application Gateway is L7 HTTP/S load balancer with WAF, cookie affinity, and URL routing. Azure Load Balancer is L4
TCP/UDP with high performance and low latency.
**Q16 Answer: A - Attach ACR to AKS with az aks update --attach-acr which creates role assignment for kubelet Managed
Identity with AcrPull role**
This command assigns AcrPull role to the kubelet's managed identity, avoiding secrets. Using admin user or service principal
secrets is legacy and insecure.
**Q17 Answer: A - PITR is built-in (7-35 days configurable) and configure Long-Term Retention (LTR) policy on the server for
weekly/monthly/yearly backups to Blob**

--- PAGE 13 ---
Azure SQL automatically handles PITR backups to RA-GRS storage with configurable retention. LTR uses separate backup
blobs with up to 10-year retention for compliance, distinct from short-term PITR.
**Q18 Answer: A - Contributor can manage resources but not manage access; User Access Administrator can manage role
assignments but not resources**
Contributor is data-plane/resource management without IAM rights, following least privilege. User Access Administrator can
grant roles (control-plane IAM) to implement separation of duties.
**Q19 Answer: A - Deploy to Staging, validate, enable auto-swap or manually swap with preview, using
WEBSITE_SWAP_WARMUP_PING_PATH**
Deployment slots enable blue-green deployments with warm-up to avoid cold start. Swap with preview ensures traffic routing
only after app initialization ping succeeds, providing true zero-downtime.
**Q20 Answer: A - Re-address the Azure VNet to non-overlapping range (e.g., 10.0.0.0/16) before establishing Site-to-Site
VPN, as overlapping ranges cannot route**
Overlapping IP ranges break routing as VPN gateway cannot disambiguate destinations. You must plan non-overlapping
RFC1918 space; Azure Virtual Network NAT can help for limited scenarios but re-addressing is standard.
3. Advanced Level (10 Questions)
**Q21: You are designing Azure Landing Zone for 500 subscriptions. App teams require Private Endpoints for PaaS services

**Q23: In AKS, you need pod-level network isolation and to assign VNet IPs directly to pods for NSG filtering (no overlay).

**Q25: A company runs 1000 VMs using Spot VMs for batch jobs. Jobs are evicted frequently causing failures. What resilient

**Q28: You are implementing Zero Trust for Entra ID. Requirement: Require compliant device OR Entra hybrid joined device
for accessing Azure Portal, block legacy auth, require phishing-resistant MFA for admins, and risk-based access. What
Conditional Access configuration?**
A) Multiple policies: 1) Block legacy auth client apps, 2) Require compliant/Hybrid Joined for all users on Azure Management,
3) Require phishing-resistant MFA (FIDO2/Windows Hello) for high-risk + admin roles with Authentication Strengths + Entra
ID Protection risk policy
B) Single policy allowing all
C) Disable MFA for convenience

**Q29: You need to audit and remediate 10,000 existing storage accounts that have allowBlobPublicAccess=true. You want
to set it to false and have continuous compliance without deployment failures. Implementation?**

B) Azure Policy Deny only

**Q30: Your organization uses Bicep + Azure DevOps. A PR triggers what-if but deployments fail intermittently with "Another
deployment in progress". You have 20 pipelines deploying to same Resource Group concurrently. Fix?**
A) Implement deployment concurrency control using deployment locks / pipeline dependsOn, serialize deployments to same
RG, use deployment stacking or distinct deployment names with incremental mode, and check
Microsoft.Resources/deployments locks
B) Increase agent count
C) Use Complete mode always
D) Delete resource group before each deployment
Advanced Answer Key & Explanations
**Q21 Answer: A - Deploy Azure Private DNS Zones in connectivity subscription hub, link to all spokes, with conditional
forwarding and Azure Policy to ensure auto-registration of A records from Private Endpoints**
Private Endpoints need Private DNS A record in privatelink zone to resolve to private IP. Enterprise pattern centralizes zones
in hub and links VNETs to avoid split-brain DNS, enforced by Policy that deploys Private DNS Zone Group.
**Q22 Answer: A - Azure Front Door Premium with custom domains, WAF policy, origin groups with health probes, and
Private Link to App Service / Load Balancer origins**

--- PAGE 15 ---
Front Door is Microsoft's global L7 reverse proxy with anycast edge, TLS termination, and Private Link integration to prevent
origin public exposure. Traffic Manager is DNS-only, no WAF or TLS termination, and fails for Private Link.
**Q23 Answer: A - Azure CNI (not overlay) gives VNet IPs to pods enabling NSG support, but requires large subnet planning
(/16) for IP exhaustion; use Azure CNI Overlay or Cilium for better scale**
Traditional Azure CNI allocates VNet IP per pod, allowing direct NSG filtering but consuming subnet IPs quickly. Overlay (with
Cilium) uses overlay network for pods, scaling to thousands without subnet exhaustion while still supporting Network Policy.
**Q24 Answer: A - Implement Azure Storage Resource Instance Rules + Service Endpoint Policies + Azure Firewall / Private
Link with Entra ID Conditional Access for workload identities and Trusted Access**
Defense in depth: Service Endpoint policies restrict to allowed storage accounts, Resource Instance Rules allow only specific
resources via RBAC, and Private Link removes public access. NSG alone cannot filter storage account FQDN.
**Q25 Answer: A - Use Spot with Eviction Policy Delete, mix with Standard VMs via Virtual Machine Scale Sets flexible
orchestration with priority mix (e.g., 80% Spot, 20% Standard), handle eviction notices via Scheduled Events and
checkpointing**
Flexible VMSS allows automated replenishment and priority mix balancing cost vs availability. Handling 30-sec eviction notice
via Azure Metadata Service (Scheduled Events) with checkpointing ensures batch progress isn't lost.
**Q26 Answer: A - Azure Virtual WAN Secured Hub with Azure Firewall Manager or Hub with Gateway Load Balancer +
third-party NVA farm + Azure Route Server for BGP**
Virtual WAN Secured Hub provides managed transitive routing, and Gateway Load Balancer chains NVA for East-West
inspection at scale. Basic peering without routing or firewall doesn't provide inspection or transitive control.
**Q27 Answer: A - Hyperscale uses page servers with log-based snapshots and separate compute replicas sharing same
storage layer, enabling instant backups via snapshots and up to 30 Named Replicas**
Hyperscale architecture separates compute and storage into page servers with log service, giving near-instant snapshot
backups regardless of size and highly scalable read replicas without data copy.
**Q28 Answer: A - Multiple policies: 1) Block legacy auth client apps, 2) Require compliant/Hybrid Joined for all users on
Azure Management, 3) Require phishing-resistant MFA (FIDO2/Windows Hello) for high-risk + admin roles with
Authentication Strengths + Entra ID Protection risk policy**
Zero Trust requires layered CA policies targeting conditions incrementally, not monolithic policy. Authentication Strengths
enforce phishing-resistant methods, and risk policies integrate with Entra ID Protection user/sign-in risk.
**Q29 Answer: A - Azure Policy with Modify effect using managed identity remediation task to set
allowBlobPublicAccess=false, with roleDefinitionIds for Storage Account Contributor, deployed via remediation task and
DeployIfNotExists**
Modify effect remediates existing non-compliant resources via managed identity. Deny only blocks future deployments, not
remediate existing. Portal manual edit doesn't scale to 10k accounts and lacks continuous enforcement.
**Q30 Answer: A - Implement deployment concurrency control using deployment locks / pipeline dependsOn, serialize
deployments to same RG, use deployment stacking or distinct deployment names with incremental mode, and check
Microsoft.Resources/deployments locks**
ARM allows only one deployment per RG at a time (per deployment name lock). Concurrent deployments cause 409 Conflict.
Serialization, using Deployment Stacks to manage lifecycle, and distinct names avoid collisions versus increasing agents.

--- PAGE 16 ---
Course File: 03_GCP.md
Course: Google Cloud Platform
1. Beginner Level (10 Questions)
**Q1.** What is the primary purpose of a GCP Project?
A) To store billing information only
B) To be a fundamental organizing entity that holds resources, enables APIs, and isolates billing and permissions
C) To manage physical data centers
D) To define network routing rules globally
**Q2.** Which of the following best describes a GCP Region and Zone?
A) A Region is a single data center, and a Zone is a continent
B) A Region is a specific geographical location composed of multiple Zones, where Zones are isolated deployment areas
within a Region
C) Regions and Zones are the same thing
D) A Zone contains multiple Regions
**Q3.** You want to store unstructured images for a website with high durability and low cost. Which storage class in Cloud
Storage is the default cost-effective choice for frequently accessed data?
A) Archive
B) Nearline
C) Standard
D) Coldline
**Q4.** Which IAM role structure follows Google's principle of least privilege?
A) Always grant Owner at organization level
B) Grant predefined roles with minimal permissions needed at the lowest resource level required
C) Grant Editor role to all users for simplicity
D) Use only custom roles for everything
**Q5.** What is the difference between Google Compute Engine and Cloud Run?
A) Compute Engine is for serverless containers, Cloud Run is for VMs
B) Compute Engine provides configurable virtual machines (IaaS), while Cloud Run is a fully managed serverless platform for
containerized applications
C) There is no difference
D) Compute Engine only runs Windows, Cloud Run only runs Linux
**Q6.** Which service is GCP's fully managed data warehouse for analytics?
A) Cloud SQL
B) BigQuery
C) Cloud Spanner
D) Firestore
**Q7.** What does VPC stand for and what does it provide?
A) Virtual Private Cloud - an isolated private network for your GCP resources
B) Virtual Public Cloud - a public internet gateway
C) Verified Private Connection - a VPN service
D) Virtual Partition Cluster - a Compute Engine cluster
**Q8.** Which command-line tool is used to manage GCP resources from terminal?
A) aws cli

--- PAGE 17 ---
B) gcloud cli
C) az cli
D) kubectl only
**Q9.** You deploy an application and want GCP to automatically manage patching, scaling, and availability of the underlying
infrastructure for a MySQL database. Which service should you choose?
A) Compute Engine with MySQL installed manually
B) Cloud SQL
C) Persistent Disk
D) Cloud Storage
**Q10.** What is Cloud Billing's resource hierarchy order from top to bottom?
A) Project > Folder > Organization > Resource
B) Organization > Folder > Project > Resource
C) Folder > Organization > Project > Resource
D) Resource > Project > Folder > Organization
Beginner Answer Key & Explanations
**Q1 Answer: B** - A GCP Project is the base entity for organizing resources, enabling APIs, managing permissions, and
billing isolation. Without a Project, no GCP resources can be created.
**Q2 Answer: B** - Regions are geographic locations (e.g., us-central1) with 3+ Zones for high availability. Zones are isolated
failure domains within a region, allowing fault-tolerant deployments.
**Q3 Answer: C** - Standard Storage is ideal for hot, frequently accessed data like website assets. It offers high performance
and 99.999999999% durability while other classes are for infrequently accessed data.
**Q4 Answer: B** - Least privilege means granting only necessary permissions at the narrowest scope. Predefined roles are
maintained by Google and should be preferred over primitive roles like Owner/Editor/Viewer.
**Q5 Answer: B** - Compute Engine is IaaS requiring you to manage VMs, OS, and scaling. Cloud Run abstracts
infrastructure, auto-scales to zero, and runs stateless containers on request.
**Q6 Answer: B** - BigQuery is serverless, petabyte-scale data warehouse with SQL interface for analytics. Cloud SQL is
OLTP, Firestore is NoSQL document DB, Spanner is globally distributed relational DB.
**Q7 Answer: A** - A VPC is a global, private, isolated software-defined network. It lets you define subnets, firewalls, routes,
and connects resources securely within GCP and to on-premises via VPN/Interconnect.
**Q8 Answer: B** - gcloud is GCP's official CLI for creating, managing, and interacting with GCP resources. It also includes
gsutil for storage and bq for BigQuery.
**Q9 Answer: B** - Cloud SQL is fully managed relational DB (MySQL, PostgreSQL, SQL Server) handling backups,
replication, patching, and scaling. Self-managing on GCE adds operational overhead.
**Q10 Answer: B** - Organization is top node (your company), Folders group Projects (e.g., by department), Projects contain
actual Resources (VMs, buckets). IAM policies inherit top-down.
2. Intermediate Level (10 Questions)
**Q11.** Your team runs GKE Autopilot and needs to securely pull private images from Artifact Registry. What is the

A) Create a service account key and mount it as a Kubernetes secret with storage.objectViewer

B) Enable Pub/Sub ordered delivery with ordering keys, and make your Cloud Function idempotent because Pub/Sub
guarantees at-least-once delivery

**Q18.** A Dataflow streaming job reading from Pub/Sub shows high system lag and hot keys. What optimizations should you
apply?
A) Increase number of zones and use default windowing
B) Re-key data with salted keys, enable Dataflow Shuffle, use appropriate windowing/triggers, and check for fusion
optimization breaks
C) Switch to batch mode
D) Delete the job and recreate
**Q19.** You need to export BigQuery audit logs and cost data to analyze who queried what and the slot consumption by
team. What solution?
A) Enable INFORMATION_SCHEMA queries and export Audit Logs via Log Sink to BigQuery dataset, then join with
INFORMATION_SCHEMA.JOBS and label billing export
B) Only use Cloud Logging console manually

--- PAGE 19 ---
C) Use Cloud Trace
D) Enable Dataflow
**Q20.** Your organization uses Cloud Interconnect and you need to encrypt traffic in transit from on-prem to GCP with
line-rate 100Gbps without managing your own VPN devices. Which option?
A) Use Cloud VPN over Interconnect with IPSec
B) Use MACsec for Cloud Interconnect (MACsec for Cloud Interconnect provides line-rate L2 encryption)
C) Use Cloud NAT
D) Use TLS only at application layer
Intermediate Answer Key & Explanations
**Q11 Answer: B** - Workload Identity Federation for GKE avoids long-lived keys by federating K8s SA to Google SA.
Granting artifactregistry.reader is minimal permission vs. storage legacy roles or Owner.
**Q12 Answer: B** - Pub/Sub ordered delivery ensures order when using ordering keys, but still only offers at-least-once
delivery. Your consumer must be idempotent and handle duplicates using message deduplication IDs.
**Q13 Answer: B** - Cloud Spanner is horizontally scalable, strongly consistent, external-consistency relational DB for global
workloads. Cloud SQL is regional with async replicas, Bigtable is wide-column NoSQL, Firestore is not ideal for relational
low-latency multi-region OLTP.
**Q14 Answer: B** - Binary Authorization enforces deploy-time attestations (from Cloud Build/Binauthz attestors) ensuring
only verified images run. VPC SC is perimeter security, Org Policy prevents resource types but doesn't attest images.
**Q15 Answer: B** - Since 2023, Cloud Run has native Cloud SQL integration (no sidecar proxy container needed). Combine
with connection pooling and Unix sockets to reuse connections across warm instances, reducing cold start overhead
significantly.
**Q16 Answer: B** - Organization Policy Service enforces guardrails across org/folder/project hierarchy, e.g.,
constraints/compute.vmExternalIpAccess and constraints/iam.disableAuditLoggingExemption. IAM manages who can do
what, Org Policy manages what can be done.
**Q17 Answer: B** - Hub-spoke with Shared VPC is current enterprise landing zone best practice. Centralized egress via hub
with Cloud NAT/next-gen firewall VM/third-party NVA and Private Service Connect avoids peering sprawl and allows
inspection and hierarchical firewall policies.
**Q18 Answer: B** - Hot keys cause worker skew; salting distributes load. Dataflow Shuffle moves shuffle off workers.
Windowing tuning and avoiding fusion breaks (e.g., unnecessary GroupByKey) reduces lag. System lag metric indicates
backlog.
**Q19 Answer: A** - Log Router sinks can export Admin Data Access logs to BigQuery.
INFORMATION_SCHEMA.JOBS_BY_ORGANIZATION provides job stats, bytes, slots, labels. Joining with billing export
labeled by team gives cost attribution per query/user.
**Q20 Answer: B** - MACsec for Cloud Interconnect (GA 2023) provides Layer 2 encryption at line rate (10G/100G) without
IPsec overhead. Cloud VPN over Interconnect adds encryption but caps at ~3Gbps per tunnel and adds complexity.

**Q28.** Explain the nuance of Private Google Access vs Private Service Connect vs Private Services Access (PSA) vs
Serverless VPC Access.
A) They are all identical

--- PAGE 21 ---
B) Private Google Access: VM in private subnet accesses Google APIs via private IP; PSA: allocates IP range in your VPC for
managed services like Cloud SQL/Redis via VPC peering; Private Service Connect: consumer endpoint to producer services
(service-oriented, no peering, supports consumer control); Serverless VPC Access Connector: legacy way for serverless
(Cloud Run/Functions/App Engine) to connect to VPC, now largely replaced by direct VPC egress
C) PSA is same as PSC
D) Private Google Access is for on-prem
**Q29.** Your Terraform deployment via Cloud Build fails with 403 on service account impersonation when deploying to prod

B) Check: 1) Cloud Build SA has iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator on deploy SA, 2) deploy SA has VPC SC ingress/egress
rules allowing Cloud Build project access, 3) Org Policy exemption for service account impersonation, 4) access level for
Cloud Build worker pool (private pool inside perimeter), 5) Use workload identity federation if external
C) Disable VPC SC
D) Use gcloud from laptop
**Q30.** You need to optimize Cloud Run + BigQuery + Cloud Storage cost by 40% while meeting SRE SLOs of 99.9%
availability. Which SRE-driven FinOps strategy?
A) Randomly delete resources
B) Implement SLO-based rightsizing: use Cloud Monitoring SLOs, set error budget burn rate alerts, rightsizing: Cloud Run
min instances = 0 for non-critical + CPU always allocated = false + concurrency tuning via load test; BigQuery: slot
autoscaling baseline tuned via INFORMATION_SCHEMA + materialized views + clustering; Storage: Autoclass + lifecycle +
Object Versioning off; use Recommender + Active Assist + carbon footprint; implement hierarchical budget alerts and label
enforcement via Org Policy constraints/requireLabels
C) Set everything to max
D) Use only on-demand pricing
Advanced Answer Key & Explanations
**Q21 Answer: A** - At scale, cost allocation requires labels on namespaces and billing export joined with GKE cost
allocation. Dataplane V2 Cilium provides eBPF-based network policy and better performance; Anthos Service Mesh gives
mTLS zero-trust. Policy Controller enforces Pod Security Standards preventing privileged escalation.
**Q22 Answer: B** - BigQuery Editions (now called BigQuery Reservations with baseline/autoscaling) provides predictable
slot cost. Materialized views pre-aggregate, partitioning/clustering prunes 10TB scan, BI Engine accelerates sub-second
dashboards, caching avoids re-scan.
**Q23 Answer: B** - CMEK implementation requires granting Google service agents encrypter/decrypter roles on KMS keys.
Org policies enforce CMEK mandatory. Rotation is native in KMS, revocation via IAM or key destruction makes data
crypto-shredded. CSEK is manual and not applicable to all services.
**Q24 Answer: B** - Eventarc Advanced (2024) separates bus/pipeline model for fan-in scale (100M events). filtering at
pipeline reduces Cloud Run invocations, Pub/Sub transport buffers, DLQ handling prevents event loss. Classic Eventarc
standard (1000s buckets) doesn't scale.
**Q25 Answer: B** - Compute Engine PD async replication now supports cross-region replication but async = RPO >0. True
RPO=0 requires application-level synchronous replication (e.g., Spanner, or DB cluster replication). RTO <5min requires GLB
+ MIG autohealing + automated failover; block storage alone cannot achieve RPO=0 cross-region.
**Q26 Answer: A** - SCC Premium is centralized risk management: Asset Inventory gives resource snapshot, SHA finds
misconfigurations (public bucket, open firewall), ETD detects threats via logs, Policy Analyzer finds who can access what,
remediation via Eventarc triggers. VPC SC adds data exfiltration protection.
**Q27 Answer: B** - BigLake decouples storage and compute with governance over GCS open formats including Apache
Iceberg (GA 2024). Dataplex provides auto-discover, policy tags, quality. BigQuery acceleration and caching gives fast query
without moving data, plus fine-grained RLS/masking.
**Q28 Answer: B** - This is classic confusion: PGA is for private subnet VMs -> Google APIs; PSA is peering-based private
connectivity for managed services allocating your IP range; PSC is more modern service-attachment model without peering
exhaustion; Serverless VPC Access connector is legacy L2 connector now superseded by direct VPC egress for Cloud
Run/Functions.

--- PAGE 22 ---
**Q29 Answer: B** - 403 impersonation + VPC SC is common. Systematic fix: token creator role, VPC SC perimeter bridging
or ingress rule for Cloud Build prod perimeter, private worker pool inside perimeter to avoid internet egress, Org Policy
exceptions for SA creation. This avoids Owner permission anti-pattern.
**Q30 Answer: B** - FinOps with SRE: error budgets allow cost optimization when SLOs healthy. Rightsizing: Cloud Run
concurrency/CPU allocation tuning saves 30%+, Autoclass moves objects to cheaper classes automatically, slot baseline
tuning prevents over-provisioning, label enforcement ensures chargeback. Active Assist Recommender automates idle
resource detection.

1. Beginner Level (10 Questions)
**Q1. What does the docker pull nginx:alpine command actually do?**

A. ENTRYPOINT
B. RUN
C. CMD

**Q3. You run docker ps. What do you see?**
A. All containers including stopped ones
B. Only running containers
C. All images on the host
D. All Docker networks and volumes
**Q4. What is the purpose of .dockerignore file?**

B. To exclude files and directories from the build context sent to the Docker daemon
C. To prevent images from being pushed to Docker Hub
D. To ignore vulnerabilities during docker scout scans
**Q5. How do you map host port 8080 to container port 80 when running a container?**

B. docker run --link 8080:80 nginx
C. docker run -v 8080:80 nginx

**Q6. What does docker build -t myapp:1.0 . do?**

B. Builds an image from Dockerfile in current directory and tags it as myapp:1.0
C. Pulls myapp:1.0 from registry and tests it
D. Exports the current directory as a tarball named myapp:1.0
**Q7. Which command removes a stopped container named web?**
A. docker rmi web
B. docker rm web
C. docker delete web
D. docker image prune web
**Q8. What happens to data written inside a container's writable layer when the container is removed with docker rm?**
A. It is automatically persisted in a volume
B. It is committed to the underlying image

--- PAGE 24 ---
C. It is permanently deleted unless stored in a volume or bind mount
D. It is moved to /var/lib/docker/backup
**Q9. What is the difference between COPY and ADD in Dockerfile for most use cases?**
A. No difference, they are aliases
B. COPY only copies local files, ADD can also handle remote URLs and auto-extract tar archives
C. ADD only works for binaries, COPY only for text files
D. COPY requires root privileges, ADD does not
**Q10. Which command shows the logs of a running container named api?**
A. docker logs api
B. docker inspect api
C. docker history api
D. docker events api
Beginner Answer Key & Explanations
**Q1. Answer: B** - docker pull fetches image layers from a registry to local cache without creating or starting a container.
The :alpine is a tag variant built on Alpine Linux for smaller size.
**Q2. Answer: C** - CMD provides default executable/args that are replaced if user supplies args to docker run.
ENTRYPOINT is harder to override and is meant for fixed main command.
**Q3. Answer: B** - docker ps lists only running containers by default. Use docker ps -a to see all containers including exited
ones.
**Q4. Answer: B** - .dockerignore reduces build context size and avoids sending secrets/node_modules/.git to daemon,
speeding up builds. It works like .gitignore for the build.
**Q5. Answer: A** - -p host:container publishes container port to host. 8080:80 means localhost:8080 forwards to container's
port 80.
**Q6. Answer: B** - -t tags the built image, . sets build context to current dir where Dockerfile is located. Daemon executes
Dockerfile steps to create layers.
**Q7. Answer: B** - docker rm removes containers, docker rmi removes images. You must stop container first unless using -f.
**Q8. Answer: C** - Container writable layer is ephemeral and tied to container lifecycle. For persistence, you must use
volumes (docker volume) or bind mounts.
**Q9. Answer: B** - Docker best practices recommend using COPY for straightforward file copying. ADD's URL and tar
auto-extraction can cause unexpected behavior.
**Q10. Answer: A** - docker logs streams stdout/stderr of main process (PID 1). Useful for debugging even after container
exited, unless using different logging driver.

**Q18. You run docker exec -it mycontainer /bin/sh. What happens if the container's main process was started with USER

A. It always works because exec runs as root by default and sh exists in all images
B. It runs as root by default (unless --user specified) but will fail if /bin/sh binary not present in image (e.g., distroless, scratch)
C. It automatically installs sh
D. It restarts container with root user
**Q19. What does docker build --no-cache and BuildKit's --mount=type=cache achieve?**
A. Both disable caching entirely
B. --no-cache disables layer cache for full rebuild, while --mount=type=cache provides persistent cache mount for package
managers across builds without bloating layers
C. They are identical
D. --mount=type=cache is used to cache container logs
**Q20. In Docker Compose v2, you have two services needing to share the same network alias and communicate via
http://backend:8000. How do they reach each other by default?**
A. They cannot, must use host network
B. Compose creates default network where services are DNS-discoverable by service name automatically
C. Must manually create external: true network
D. Only possible with --link

--- PAGE 26 ---
Intermediate Answer Key & Explanations
**Q11. Answer: B** - Multi-stage builds let you use heavy builder image then COPY only artifacts to minimal runtime like
distroless/alpine/scratch. This shrinks image from ~1GB to ~20MB and removes shell and package managers.
**Q12. Answer: C** - Named volumes are Docker-managed, portable, work on all OSes, and support volume drivers/backups.
Bind mounts tie to host path and have host permission issues, while writable layer is ephemeral.
**Q13. Answer: B** - Without proper init, PID 1 doesn't reap zombies and may not forward SIGTERM, causing graceful
shutdown issues in Node/JVM apps. --init uses tini, now built-in, critical for production.
**Q14. Answer: A** - Since Compose spec, depends_on: condition: service_healthy waits for healthcheck to be passing.
Without condition, it only waits for container start, leading to race conditions.
**Q15. Answer: A** - --memory=1g (or -m) sets cgroup memory limit, --cpus=1 limits CPU time across cores. Docker uses
cgroups v2 on modern hosts; always set limits to prevent noisy neighbor.
**Q16. Answer: B** - Combining update+install+caching cleanup in single RUN avoids stale layer and reduces size.
--no-install-recommends and removing apt lists is official best practice since 2023 Docker docs.
**Q17. Answer: A** - Bridge (default) gives container its own veth + iptables NAT. Host mode (--network host) sacrifices
isolation for performance/lower latency, but loses port mapping and is Linux-only.
**Q18. Answer: B** - docker exec defaults to root user inside container namespace, but filesystem is container's. Minimal
images lack shell; you need docker debug or include busybox for troubleshooting.
**Q19. Answer: B** - --no-cache forces all layers to rebuild, useful but slow. BuildKit cache mounts (RUN
--mount=type=cache,target=/root/.cache/pip pip install) persist outside layers, speeding rebuilds without increasing final size.
**Q20. Answer: B** - Compose automatically creates a custom bridge network for project, with embedded DNS server.
Service names resolve to container IPs, no links needed. Custom network names improve isolation vs default bridge.
3. Advanced Level (10 Questions)
**Q21. You've enabled Docker Content Trust and BuildKit. Your CI builds image myreg.io/app:prod with cosign signature.
What is the most secure way to ensure deployment pulls only signed image in production?**
A. DOCKER_CONTENT_TRUST=1 docker pull myreg.io/app:prod
B. Rely on tag mutability and pull by tag
C. Use digest pinning myreg.io/app@sha256:<digest> plus signature verification with cosign verification policy / admission
controller (e.g., OPA, Kyverno, Sigstore policy controller)
D. Use docker pull --verify=false
**Q22. Your container app OOMKilled intermittently despite --memory=2g. docker stats shows MEM usage 1.2g/2g. What is
likely root cause?**
A. Docker stats is wrong
B. Kernel memory, tmpfs mounts, or memory used by processes inside container counted differently; also Go/Java memory
limits not aware of cgroup unless configured, causing heap to exceed limit
C. Need to increase CPUs
D. This cannot happen
**Q23. How does BuildKit's --mount=type=secret differ from ARG or COPY .env for handling private NPM_TOKEN / SSH
keys during build?**
A. No difference
B. ARG and COPY leave secrets in image layers/history, visible via docker history. --mount=type=secret mounts secret only
during RUN step, never persisted to layer, and not cached
C. Secret mount is slower
D. ARG is more secure
**Q24. For a Docker Swarm / Compose production stack, you need config reload without restart and secrets rotation for DB
password. How should you handle it?**
A. Use ENV variables and docker restart

--- PAGE 27 ---
B. Use Docker configs/secrets (mounted as /run/secrets/, in-memory tmpfs) + support for file-based watch, or with Swarm use
docker service update --secret-rm/add with rotation, app watches file change via inotify
C. Bake password into image
D. Use bind mount of host /etc/passwd
**Q25. You see docker system df shows huge build cache and you run docker builder prune -a. A container using overlay2
driver suddenly fails to start with no space left. Why?**
A. builder prune deletes volumes
B. overlay2 driver shares space with /var/lib/docker/buildkit; but more likely disk pressure from volumes/log files filling /var
partition causing overlay to fail; pruning build cache alone doesn't free volume/logs, need docker system prune --volumes and
log rotation with json-file max-size
C. This never happens
D. You must reboot
**Q26. In rootless Docker (2024 default for many distros), which limitation is true?**
A. Rootless can use privileged containers and modify host network without restriction exactly like rootful
B. Rootless cannot bind to privileged ports <1024 by default unless sysctl set, AppArmor may restrict, overlay over
fuse-overlayfs performance penalty, and --network host doesn't give real host network due to user namespace
C. Rootless is faster
D. Rootless supports cgroups v1 only
**Q27. You need to debug a distroless image running in Kubernetes that has no shell. What is valid 2024+ approach?**
A. docker exec with bash
B. Use kubectl debug -it pod --image=busybox --target=app --share-processes (ephemeral debug container) and
docker:24-dind context, or cdebug/docker debug with docker build --platform debug / Nixery debug variants, attaching via
process namespace
C. Rebuild with ubuntu base in production
D. Impossible
**Q28. CVE-2024-21626 (runc) is exploited via WORKDIR inside container being /proc/self/fd/7. How do you mitigate before
patching Docker?**
A. Nothing
B. Ensure base images don't contain /proc symlink tricks, don't run untrusted Dockerfiles with attacker-controlled WORKDIR,
enforce policy with image scanning, and update runc >=1.1.12 / Docker >=25.0.2 immediately; use user namespaces and
drop capability
C. Use docker pull only
D. Disable overlay2
**Q29. You are optimizing CI/CD: building 10 microservices each with identical npm ci layer. Build takes 10m. Best BuildKit
optimization?**
A. Keep separate builds
B. Use shared BuildKit cache backend: --cache-from type=registry,ref=myreg/cache:service --cache-to
type=registry,ref=myreg/cache:service,mode=max plus type=gha for GitHub Actions, and common layer for dependencies via
COPY package*.json before source, with mode=max cross-repo mounts
C. Use docker commit
D. Disable BuildKit
**Q30. When using docker run --read-only --tmpfs /tmp --tmpfs /run -p 3000:3000 myapp, app writes to /app/logs which fails.
Correct secure fix?**
A. Remove --read-only
B. Keep read-only root filesystem, mount writable tmpfs or volume only where needed: --tmpfs /app/logs:uid=1000,mode=755
or -v app-logs:/app/logs, keeping rest immutable to prevent binary tampering and comply with CIS benchmarks
C. Run privileged
D. chmod 777 in Dockerfile

--- PAGE 28 ---
Advanced Answer Key & Explanations
**Q21. Answer: C** - Tags are mutable. Production should pin by digest and verify Sigstore/cosign signature via policy
controller. DCT is deprecated in favor of Notation/Cosign + OPA/Kyverno admission.
**Q22. Answer: B** - OOM can be from cgroup memory includes page cache, kernel mem, or Java default heap sizing 1/4
host RAM not container. Configure -XX:MaxRAMPercentage or GOMEMLIMIT for Go to respect cgroup limits.
**Q23. Answer: B** - docker history and layer export reveals ARG and ENV. Secret mounts (RUN
--mount=type=secret,id=npm_token npm ci) are BuildKit-only, tmpfs-mounted during build, never committed, safe for CI.
**Q24. Answer: B** - Docker secrets are tmpfs mounted at /run/secrets, 400 permissions, not in env ps. Apps should watch
file change or receive SIGHUP. Swarm supports rolling update with secret rotation without full downtime.
**Q25. Answer: B** - docker system df counts build cache, images, volumes. builder prune doesn't touch volumes or
container logs. Disk full on /var/lib/docker affects overlay2 copy-up causing ENOSPC. Implement log rotation and monitor
volumes.
**Q26. Answer: B** - Rootless runs daemon in user namespace, cannot use privileged ports without
net.ipv4.ip_unprivileged_port_start, host network is actually namespaced slirp. Supports cgroups v2 with systemd, good for
security but with networking/perf nuances.
**Q27. Answer: B** - Modern debugging uses ephemeral containers sharing PID namespace. kubectl debug, Docker 25+
docker debug (beta), or ko/cdebug allow inspecting distroless without shipping shell to prod, preserving minimal attack
surface.
**Q28. Answer: B** - CVE-2024-21626 allows container escape via leaked file descriptor due to WORKDIR inside proc.
Mitigation is patch runc ASAP and enforce policy via image linter (e.g., forbidding WORKDIR starting with /proc), plus user
namespace remapping.
**Q29. Answer: B** - BuildKit cache backends (registry, gha, s3) with mode=max cache all layers not just final, shared across
parallel builds. Combined with proper layer ordering (deps first) cuts 10m to ~1-2m.
**Q30. Answer: B** - --read-only sets rootfs immutable per CIS Docker Benchmark 4.1. It prevents runtime
modification/exploits. Provide writable tmpfs/volumes only for required paths, preserving immutability and least privilege, not
full privileged or 777.

--- PAGE 29 ---

Course: Kubernetes
1. Beginner Level (10 Questions)
**Q1: Which component is responsible for maintaining the desired state of the cluster by managing Pods via ReplicaSets?**

**Q2: What is the smallest deployable unit in Kubernetes?**

A) On control plane nodes, scheduling Pods to worker nodes
B) On each worker node, ensuring containers in PodSpecs are running and healthy
C) Inside etcd, storing cluster state
D) As a DaemonSet, providing network proxying
**Q6: What happens when you run kubectl apply -f deployment.yaml if the Deployment already exists?**
A) It fails with "already exists" error
B) It deletes and recreates the Deployment
C) It performs a declarative three-way merge patch to update to desired state
D) It only scales the Deployment
**Q7: Which resource is used to persist data beyond Pod lifecycle and must be bound to a PersistentVolumeClaim?**
A) ConfigMap
B) PersistentVolume (PV)
C) EmptyDir
D) Secret
**Q8: What does the restartPolicy: Never mean in a Pod spec?**
A) The kubelet will never restart containers in that Pod if they fail
B) The Deployment will never create new Pods
C) The Pod is evicted and never rescheduled

--- PAGE 30 ---
D) The livenessProbe is disabled
**Q9: Which namespace holds control plane components like kube-system components in a standard cluster?**
A) default
B) kube-system
C) kube-public
D) kube-node-lease
**Q10: How do you get detailed information about a failing Pod including events?**
A) kubectl logs pod-name
B) kubectl get pods --watch
C) kubectl describe pod pod-name
D) kubectl explain pod
Beginner Answer Key & Explanations
**Q1 Answer: C) Deployment Controller** - The Deployment controller, running in kube-controller-manager, reconciles
desired vs current state via ReplicaSets. It handles rolling updates and rollbacks.
**Q2 Answer: B) Pod** - A Pod wraps one or more containers with shared network and storage. You never deploy containers
directly, only via Pods.
**Q3 Answer: B) kubectl create deployment nginx --image=nginx:1.25** - create deployment is the imperative way to create a
Deployment. Option A creates a Pod, not a Deployment.
**Q4 Answer: C) Exposes the Service on an internal IP reachable only within the cluster** - ClusterIP is the default Service
type, allocating a virtual IP for internal service discovery via kube-proxy and CoreDNS.
**Q5 Answer: B) On each worker node, ensuring containers in PodSpecs are running and healthy** - kubelet is the node
agent that watches API server PodSpecs bound to its node and manages the CRI to run them.
**Q6 Answer: C) It performs a declarative three-way merge patch to update to desired state** - apply uses
last-applied-configuration annotation to patch, enabling declarative management and gitops workflows.
**Q7 Answer: B) PersistentVolume (PV)** - PVs are cluster storage provisioned by admins, and PVCs claim them. This
decouples Pod from storage implementation.
**Q8 Answer: A) The kubelet will never restart containers in that Pod if they fail** - This policy is primarily used for Jobs.
Always and OnFailure are the other options; Deployments require Always.
**Q9 Answer: B) kube-system** - This namespace contains CoreDNS, kube-proxy, and control plane Pods in some
distributions. User workloads should use default or custom namespaces.
**Q10 Answer: C) kubectl describe pod pod-name** - Describe shows conditions, container statuses, and crucial Events like
FailedScheduling or ImagePullBackOff. Logs only show stdout.

**Q20: Explain the difference between ConfigMap and Secret in etcd storage and usage:**

Intermediate Answer Key & Explanations

--- PAGE 32 ---
**Q11 Answer: C) After tolerationSeconds of 300s for not-ready/unreachable taints, they are evicted and rescheduled** -
Default tolerations allow 5 minutes before eviction. After eviction, ReplicaSet creates new Pods elsewhere if resources allow.
**Q12 Answer: B) Pods are deleted but PVCs are retained by default to prevent data loss** - StatefulSet's PVC retention is
intentional for stateful data safety. You must manually delete PVCs or use spec.persistentVolumeClaimRetentionPolicy (K8s
1.27+).
**Q13 Answer: B) readinessProbe** - Readiness failing removes Pod from Endpoints, so no Service traffic. Liveness failure
causes restart. StartupProbe gates liveness/readiness during slow start.
**Q14 Answer: A) Create a ServiceAccount, bind it to Role/ClusterRole via RoleBinding, and set serviceAccountName in
Pod** - This follows least privilege. Pods get projected token via TokenRequest API mounted at
/var/run/secrets/kubernetes.io/serviceaccount.
**Q15 Answer: A) Requests used for scheduling, limits enforced via CFS quota throttling** - Scheduler sums requests.
Kubelet sets cpu.shares from requests and cpu.cfs_quota_us from limits. Memory limits trigger OOMKill if exceeded.
**Q16 Answer: A) They share network namespace (same IP, can use localhost) and can share volumes, but have isolated
filesystem otherwise** - Pod is the network boundary. Containers can communicate via localhost:port and share
EmptyDir/Volumes explicitly configured.
**Q17 Answer: A) An Ingress Controller (e.g., ingress-nginx, traefik) that implements Ingress logic** - Ingress is just API spec;
controller watches it and configures reverse proxy. Without controller, resource does nothing.
**Q18 Answer: A) At least 2 Pods must remain available during drain/upgrade operations** - PDB is respected by kubectl
drain and voluntary eviction API. It does not affect involuntary failures like Node crash.
**Q19 Answer: B) It queries metrics APIs (resource, custom, external) and calculates desiredReplicas = ceil[currentReplicas *
(currentMetric/desiredMetric)]** - HPA control loop fetches metrics every 15s by default and stabilizes scaling with windows to
avoid flapping.
**Q20 Answer: B) Secrets are base64-encoded and can be encrypted at rest via EncryptionConfiguration, intended for
sensitive data, while ConfigMaps are plaintext for non-confidential config** - Secrets have 1MB limit, enable encryption at
rest, and have slightly more protection in logs and etcd. Neither is secure without RBAC and encryption.
3. Advanced Level (10 Questions)
**Q21: You see CrashLoopBackOff with OOMKilled (exit 137). Memory request=256Mi, limit=512Mi, node has 4Gi free.
Application heap uses 700Mi. What is correct fix?**
A) Increase Node memory
B) Increase memory limit to >700Mi (e.g., 1Gi) and set request appropriately, plus fix heap/mem leak; consider VPA
C) Decrease memory request to 128Mi to allow better binpacking
D) Add CPU limit to prevent OOM

B) Cilium requires CiliumNetworkPolicy or needs NetworkPolicy enforcement enabled and endpoint selected by label

C) NetworkPolicy only works with Calico
D) ClusterIP Services bypass NetworkPolicy by design

B) Use CSI driver: Mountpoint for Amazon S3 CSI driver or GCS Fuse CSI driver with appropriate cache and permission

C) Use PersistentVolume with NFS and copy data from S3

**Q25: etcd shows high DB size and etcdserver: mvcc: database space exceeded. What happened and how to fix without

A) OPA Gatekeeper / Kyverno via ValidatingAdmissionPolicy (1.28+ ValidatingAdmissionPolicy) and Pod Security Standards
enforced via namespace labels
B) Use NetworkPolicy
C) PodSecurityPolicy still works in 1.29
D) Use RBAC alone
**Q27: A Job with 100 parallel Pods doing batch processing suddenly gets many FailedScheduling due to insufficient CPU.

A) Disable autoscaler
B) Use Karpenter with consolidation, Pod resource rightsizing, set Job parallelism with work queue (e.g., Kueue + JobSet),

C) Manually create Nodes with kubectl
D) Use DaemonSet instead of Job
**Q28: Debugging: kubectl exec -it pod -- /bin/sh hangs. API server logs show timeout. Pod is Running, kubelet is healthy.
What is likely root cause chain?**
A) Corrupt etcd
B) CNI overlay failure, kubelet <-> API server network issue, or container runtime exec not responding; check kubelet logs,
