C) ConfigMap too large
D) ServiceAccount missing
**Q29: For multi-tenant cluster with strict isolation, you need per-tenant network encryption, node isolation, and API audit.
Complete solution?**
A) Install Cilium with WireGuard encryption + mutual TLS via CiliumNetworkPolicy L7, use Kata Containers/gVisor
runtimeClass for node isolation, taint nodes per tenant, and enable API Server Audit logging with structured webhooks
B) Use only ClusterIP Services
C) Use hostNetwork: true for all Pods
D) Rely on default Docker bridge
**Q30: You need to upgrade Kubernetes from 1.28 to 1.29 in production with zero control plane downtime. Correct kubeadm
procedure?**
A) Upgrade all Nodes at once with kubeadm upgrade apply
B) Upgrade control plane one by one: kubeadm upgrade plan, kubeadm upgrade apply v1.29.x on first control plane, then
kubeadm upgrade node on others, then drain & upgrade kubelet/kubectl on worker nodes one by one respecting skew policy
(kubelet may be up to 2 minor older than apiserver)
C) Delete cluster and create new one
D) Upgrade kubelet before apiserver
Advanced Answer Key & Explanations

--- PAGE 34 ---
**Q21 Answer: B) Increase memory limit to >700Mi (e.g., 1Gi) and set request appropriately, plus fix heap/mem leak;
consider VPA** - OOMKill occurs when container exceeds cgroup memory limit enforced by kernel. Node free memory is
irrelevant. Setting limit too low kills process; need to profile and set request=usage, limit=peak+bump.
**Q22 Answer: B) Cilium requires CiliumNetworkPolicy or needs NetworkPolicy enforcement enabled and endpoint selected
by label correctly; also check if policy allows DNS egress which may block resolution** - Cilium supports standard
NetworkPolicy but enforcement can be disabled. Common pitfall: Ingress policy denies DNS (port 53) breaking name
resolution, and label selector mismatch.
**Q23 Answer: B) Use CSI driver: Mountpoint for Amazon S3 CSI driver or GCS Fuse CSI driver with appropriate cache and
permission settings** - CSI drivers provide native bucket mounts with caching, mount options, and lifecycle management.
Mountpoint S3 is AWS GA since 2024 and optimizes read throughput for ML.
**Q24 Answer: B) Configure readinessProbe + startupProbe to delay traffic, add minReadySeconds,
terminationGracePeriodSeconds > 0, and handle SIGTERM with preStop hook for connection draining** - 502s during rollout
happen due to race between endpoint removal and connection close. Proper lifecycle hooks + probes + draining solves;
maxSurge 0 ensures capacity.
**Q25 Answer: A) Compact revision and defrag, increase quota-backend-bytes (default 2GB -> 8GB max), check for runaway
Lease/Events creating spam (e.g., misbehaving controller spamming events)** - etcd keeps MVCC history; without
compaction DB grows. Defrag reclaims space. Quota prevents runaway growth. Find source of spam via etcd metrics.
**Q26 Answer: A) OPA Gatekeeper / Kyverno via ValidatingAdmissionPolicy (1.28+ ValidatingAdmissionPolicy) and Pod
Security Standards enforced via namespace labels** - PSP removed. Replacement is PSS (privileged/baseline/restricted via
labels pod-security.kubernetes.io/enforce) + policy engines for custom rules.
**Q27 Answer: B) Use Karpenter with consolidation, Pod resource rightsizing, set Job parallelism with work queue (e.g.,
Kueue + JobSet), overprovisioning pause pods, and node affinity to mix spot/on-demand** - This pattern handles bursty batch
efficiently. Karpenter scales faster than CAS, Kueue manages quota, JobSet groups jobs, pause pods reduce scale-up
latency.
**Q28 Answer: B) CNI overlay failure, kubelet <-> API server network issue, or container runtime exec not responding; check
kubelet logs, CRI socket, CNI plugin health, and whether kubectl port-forward also fails due to network path** - Exec uses
SPDY/websocket via apiserver->kubelet->CRI. Hang indicates network path broken or runtime deadlock. CNI outage breaks
pod network but not host network.
**Q29 Answer: A) Install Cilium with WireGuard encryption + mutual TLS via CiliumNetworkPolicy L7, use Kata
Containers/gVisor runtimeClass for node isolation, taint nodes per tenant, and enable API Server Audit logging with
structured webhooks** - Full multi-tenant security requires network encryption, runtime sandboxing, scheduling isolation, and
audit. This stack provides all layers.
**Q30 Answer: B) Upgrade control plane one by one: kubeadm upgrade plan, kubeadm upgrade apply v1.29.x on first control
plane, then kubeadm upgrade node on others, then drain & upgrade kubelet/kubectl on worker nodes one by one respecting
skew policy (kubelet may be up to 2 minor older than apiserver)** - This is the supported kubeadm HA upgrade flow ensuring
rolling upgrade and version skew compliance. Apiserver must be upgraded before kubelet.

Course File: 06_Terraform.md
Course: Terraform
1. Beginner Level (10 Questions)
**Q1. What is the primary purpose of terraform init?**
A. It applies the execution plan to create infrastructure
B. It initializes a working directory by downloading provider plugins, modules, and initializing the backend
C. It validates the syntax of Terraform configuration files
D. It destroys all managed infrastructure
**Q2. Which file extension is used for Terraform configuration files, and what is the default state file name?**
A. .tf and terraform.tfstate
B. .terraform and state.json
C. .hcl and terraform.state
D. .tf.json only and terraform.tfstate.d
**Q3. What does terraform plan do?**
A. It immediately creates resources without showing changes
B. It generates an execution plan showing what actions Terraform will take to achieve the desired state, without making
changes
C. It imports existing infrastructure into state
D. It formats Terraform code according to canonical style
**Q4. In HCL, how do you define an input variable with a default value and type?**
A. variable "instance_type" { default = "t3.micro" type = string }
B. variable "instance_type" { type = string default = "t3.micro" description = "EC2 instance type" }
C. input "instance_type" = "t3.micro"
D. var.instance_type = { value = "t3.micro" }
**Q5. What is the purpose of terraform.tfvars or *.auto.tfvars?**
A. To store provider credentials securely encrypted
B. To define backend configuration
C. To assign values to input variables automatically without CLI flags
D. To define output values
**Q6. Consider: resource "aws_instance" "web" { ami = "ami-12345" instance_type = "t3.micro" }. How do you reference its
public IP in another resource?**
A. aws_instance.web.public_ip
B. ${aws_instance.web.public_ip}
C. aws_instance.web.public_ip.value
D. The syntax is invalid, public_ip cannot be referenced
**Q7. Which command is used to change formatting of Terraform files to canonical style?**
A. terraform validate
B. terraform fmt
C. terraform lint
D. terraform format
**Q8. What does an output block do in Terraform?**
A. It prints debug logs during apply
B. It defines values that are exposed to the user after apply, and can be consumed by other modules or workspaces

--- PAGE 36 ---
C. It writes data directly to the backend
D. It creates a file output on disk
**Q9. What happens if you manually delete a resource in the cloud console that is managed by Terraform, then run terraform
plan?**
A. Nothing, Terraform ignores manual changes
B. Terraform detects drift and proposes to recreate the resource to match desired state
C. Terraform automatically deletes it from state
D. Terraform will error and require terraform refresh only
**Q10. Which block is used to configure a Terraform provider version constraint?**
A. provider "aws" { version = "~> 5.0" }
B. terraform { required_providers { aws = { source = "hashicorp/aws" version = "~> 5.0" } } }
C. versions { aws = "~> 5.0" }
D. dependency "aws" { version = "~> 5.0" }
Beginner Answer Key & Explanations
**Q1. Answer: B** - terraform init initializes the working directory, downloading providers, modules, and configuring the
backend. It must be run before plan/apply in a new directory.
**Q2. Answer: A** - Terraform configs use .tf (or .tf.json) extension, and default local state file is terraform.tfstate. This is
fundamental working knowledge.
**Q3. Answer: B** - terraform plan is a dry-run that shows creates, updates, deletes to align real infrastructure with desired
configuration. It never modifies infrastructure.
**Q4. Answer: B** - Correct syntax requires type = string and default = "t3.micro" inside a variable block. Terraform 0.12+
requires type constraints without interpolation.
**Q5. Answer: C** - .tfvars files provide variable values; terraform.tfvars and *.auto.tfvars are loaded automatically. They
simplify variable injection without -var flags.
**Q6. Answer: A** - Terraform reference syntax is resource_type.name.attribute, e.g., aws_instance.web.public_ip. Legacy
interpolation syntax B is still valid but unnecessary since Terraform 0.12.
**Q7. Answer: B** - terraform fmt rewrites configs to canonical HCL style (spacing, alignment). It's used in CI to enforce
consistent formatting.
**Q8. Answer: B** - Outputs expose values after apply, useful for parent modules, CLI display, and remote state references.
They are essential for module composition.
**Q9. Answer: B** - Terraform compares desired config vs state vs real infra; drift detection in plan will show the resource
needs to be recreated. This is core to declarative IaC.
**Q10. Answer: B** - Since Terraform 0.13+, version constraints belong in required_providers inside terraform block with
source address. Option A was deprecated in 0.13.
2. Intermediate Level (10 Questions)
**Q11. What is the purpose of terraform state mv vs terraform import?**
A. Both do the same thing
B. state mv moves/renames resources within state or between states without changing real infrastructure; import brings
existing real infrastructure under Terraform management
C. state mv imports from cloud; import moves inside state
D. state mv deletes state entries; import creates infrastructure
**Q12. You have count = 3 on aws_instance.web. How do you reference the second instance's ID, and what is a key
limitation of count?**
A. aws_instance.web[1].id; count creates a list and removing an item in the middle causes re-indexing and unwanted
recreation
B. aws_instance.web.1.id; count has no limitations

--- PAGE 37 ---
C. aws_instance.web["1"].id; count cannot be used with instances
D. element(aws_instance.web.*.id, 1) only
**Q13. What does for_each require as input, and how does it improve over count?**
A. It requires a list; it is identical to count
B. It requires a map or set of strings, and creates a map of resources keyed by stable identifiers, avoiding reordering issues
when items are removed
C. It requires an integer; it automatically handles dependencies
D. It requires a boolean; it enables parallel execution
**Q14. Consider this backend config: terraform { backend "s3" { bucket = "my-tf-state" key = "prod/terraform.tfstate" region =
"us-east-1" dynamodb_table = "tf-lock" encrypt = true } }. What is the role of dynamodb_table?**
A. It stores the state file
B. It provides state locking and consistency checking to prevent concurrent writes
C. It stores provider plugins
D. It is deprecated and unused
**Q15. What is the difference between depends_on and implicit dependencies?**
A. There is no difference
B. Implicit dependencies are created via attribute references (Terraform infers order); depends_on is explicit for hidden
dependencies where no attribute is referenced, e.g., IAM role must exist before instance that assumes it without direct
reference
C. depends_on is required for all dependencies
D. Implicit dependencies only work for data sources
**Q16. In Terraform modules, how do you pass data from a child module to root?**
A. Using export block in child module
B. Child module defines output blocks, root accesses via module.<name>.<output_name>
C. Using global variables
D. Modules cannot return data
**Q17. What does terraform taint (legacy) or -replace target do, and when would you use it?**
A. It deletes the resource from state permanently
B. It marks a resource for recreation on next apply, useful when resource is degraded but Terraform doesn't detect drift (e.g.,
corrupted VM)
C. It locks the resource to prevent changes
D. It imports the resource
**Q18. What is the behavior of lifecycle { create_before_destroy = true }?**
A. It prevents any destruction
B. Terraform creates the replacement resource first, then destroys the old one, critical for zero-downtime resources like
launch templates or certificates requiring name uniqueness
C. It deletes first then creates
D. It ignores changes to the resource
**Q19. How does terraform workspace work and when should you avoid it?**
A. Workspaces create completely separate configs
B. Workspaces are named isolated state files within same backend/config, useful for simple env separation but should be
avoided for strong isolation (use separate directories/backends and variable files) due to shared code and risk of misapplied
changes
C. Workspaces delete other environments
D. Workspaces are same as modules
**Q20. What does dynamic block do in Terraform?**
A. Dynamically creates providers

--- PAGE 38 ---
B. Generates nested blocks dynamically inside a resource/data source based on a collection, replacing repetitive code; e.g.,
dynamic ingress blocks for security groups from a list of ports
C. Allows dynamic backend configuration
D. Enables runtime evaluation of count
Intermediate Answer Key & Explanations
**Q11. Answer: B** - state mv is refactoring tool for state structure without touching real infra; import maps existing real infra
to a resource address. Confusing them leads to accidental deletions or duplication.
**Q12. Answer: A** - With count, resources are indexed 0..n-1; referenced as resource[1]. Removing index 0 shifts all others,
causing recreate. This is why for_each is preferred.
**Q13. Answer: B** - for_each requires map or set(string) with stable keys, e.g., for_each = toset(["a","b"]). It maps keys to
instances, so removal doesn't affect others, solving count's weakness.
**Q14. Answer: B** - DynamoDB table provides state locking for S3 backend. Before S3 native conditional writes (2024),
DynamoDB prevented concurrent applies from corrupting state. Now optional but still common.
**Q15. Answer: B** - Implicit via aws_instance.example.id creates DAG edge automatically. Explicit depends_on =
[aws_iam_role.role] needed when dependency isn't captured by reference, like provisioners or side-effects.
**Q16. Answer: B** - Child outputs become attributes on module.<NAME> object. Root cannot access child resources
directly, only via declared outputs, enforcing encapsulation.
**Q17. Answer: B** - terraform apply -replace="aws_instance.web[0]" forces recreate next apply. Useful for immutable infra
pattern or when instance is unhealthy but Terraform sees no diff.
**Q18. Answer: B** - create_before_destroy inverts default order to avoid downtime and handle name conflicts. Essential for
aws_launch_configuration, ACM certificates, etc., often needs allow_overlapping considerations.
**Q19. Answer: B** - Workspaces share backend config and code but separate state via key prefix. For prod/non-prod strong
isolation, separate root modules/backends + code reuse via modules is safer and more explicit than workspaces.
**Q20. Answer: B** - dynamic with for_each + content generates repeatable nested blocks. It keeps DRY for security groups,
lifecycle rules, etc., where Terraform doesn't support count/for_each directly on nested blocks.
3. Advanced Level (10 Questions)
**Q21. You are using Terraform Cloud remote execution with import blocks (Terraform 1.5+). Which statement is accurate
about declarative import?**
A. import { to = aws_instance.web id = "i-12345" } only works with terraform import CLI
B. import { to = aws_instance.web id = "i-12345" } defines import in config; on next plan/apply Terraform merges existing
resource into state and then shows diff vs config, enabling codified, reviewable imports and chaining with generation via
-generate-config-out
C. Import blocks automatically generate correct resource configuration
D. Import blocks delete existing state
**Q22. How do you handle provider version upgrades that introduce breaking state schema changes for resources with
moved blocks?**
A. Delete the state
B. Use moved { from = aws_instance.old_name to = aws_instance.new_name } block to refactor without destroying;
Terraform treats it as moved in state during plan. For provider schema upgrades, run terraform apply -refresh-only first, check
changelog for state migration, and pin providers with ~> constraints
C. Moved blocks are not needed
D. Always taint and recreate
**Q23. You have a module with for_each = var.env_map where var.env_map contains sensitive values. terraform plan leaks
sensitive values in output. How do you properly mitigate?**
A. Use sensitive = true on output and variable, but note for_each keys/values exposure: keys are never sensitive, so don't put
secrets in map keys; values marked sensitive are redacted, but for_each itself cannot be sensitive - restructure to use
non-sensitive keys and handle sensitive data inside resources

--- PAGE 39 ---
B. Set TF_CLI_CONFIG_FILE
C. There is no mitigation
D. Use count instead
**Q24. Consider performance at scale (5000 resources). Which patterns optimize plan time?**
A. Always use -refresh=false
B. Use -parallelism=n tuning (default 10), minimize data sources with dependencies, use moved blocks carefully, leverage
-target only for emergencies, enable provider-level skip_* flags where available, use terraform_data instead of null_resource,
and consider breaking into smaller root modules with dependencies via remote state or Stacks
C. Increase instance size
D. Use count = 5000 on one resource
**Q25. Explain check blocks (Terraform 1.5+) vs postcondition/precondition (Terraform 1.2+). When would you use each?**
A. They are identical
B. check blocks are independent data-source style validations that don't block apply but show warnings/errors for assertions
on infra health (e.g., endpoint responds); precondition/postcondition are lifecycle validations that block apply if violated (e.g.,
postcondition on aws_instance ensuring instance_type meets policy, or data source precondition ensuring VPC exists)
C. Checks replace providers
D. Preconditions only work for outputs
**Q26. You manage AWS with Terraform Stacks (TF Stacks, GA 2024-2025) and want to handle multi-region deployment
with shared components. How should you structure it?**
A. Use one big root module with provider alias per region
B. Define components in .tfcomponent.hcl, use component blocks with for_each per region, stack variables, and deployment
orchestration with deployment blocks; providers configured per component, dependencies via component outputs, enabling
orchestrated multi-region/component lifecycle
C. Stacks are same as workspaces
D. You must use Terragrunt
**Q27. What is the implication of using ignore_changes with a map attribute, and how does it interact with jsonencode drift?**
A. It has no effect
B. lifecycle { ignore_changes = [tags, ami] } ignores all future changes to those attributes (useful for externally-managed tags
or ASG AMI); but if you ignore tags_all or entire map, replacement detection breaks; for jsonencode fields (e.g.,
aws_iam_policy), Terraform compares normalized JSON - ignoring it prevents drift fixes but necessary if policy managed
outside Terraform
C. ignore_changes deletes resources
D. It only works for id
**Q28. You have a provider that hits API rate limits during refresh. How do you design around it in Terraform 1.7+?**
A. There is no solution
B. Strategies: use terraform_data with triggers_replace + provisioners replaced by API calls, set provider retry config (e.g.,
max_retries for AWS), implement -parallelism=5 or lower, use removed {} block for resources no longer needed without
refresh, use ephemeral resources (ephemeral block in TF 1.10+) for transient creds, and split state or use -refresh-only +
cached plan
C. Always use count to reduce API calls
D. Delete provider
**Q29. How do Terraform test framework (.tftest.hcl, GA 1.6+) tests work vs legacy Terratest?**
A. They are identical
B. Native tests: define run blocks with command = plan or apply, variables, and assert conditions on outputs/resources;
executes in isolated state, supports mocking providers with mock_provider "aws" {}, unlike Terratest which uses Go wrapper
executing real CLI and needs real creds; native tests are faster for unit-style validation
C. Native tests cannot assert
D. They require Python

--- PAGE 40 ---
**Q30. You need to perform a zero-downtime migration from aws_s3_bucket legacy arguments (acl, versioning {} inline) to
new standalone resources (aws_s3_bucket_acl, aws_s3_bucket_versioning). Describe the safe path.**
A. Just delete and recreate bucket
B. Safe path: Import legacy bucket then upgrade AWS provider to v4+, then remove inline
acl/versioning/server_side_encryption_configuration from aws_s3_bucket config (now deprecated), create new standalone
resources referencing bucket ID, add lifecycle { } if needed, run terraform plan - plan should show no bucket recreation, only
creation of new versioning/acl resources adopting existing settings; if state mismatch, terraform state mv is not needed; key is
provider upgrade preserves state schema
C. Use terraform taint
D. Buckets cannot be migrated
Advanced Answer Key & Explanations
**Q21. Answer: B** - Import blocks made imports declarative and reviewable in VCS since TF 1.5. Combined with terraform
plan -generate-config-out they generate stub configs. This is crucial for brownfield adoption at scale.
**Q22. Answer: B** - moved blocks enable refactoring addresses without destroy. For provider schema upgrades, check for
state upgrade path and test with terraform plan. Provider pinning prevents unexpected major upgrades causing state
incompatibility.
**Q23. Answer: A** - for_each keys cannot be sensitive (they appear in resource addresses and plan). If secrets are needed
as map values, structure map with non-sensitive keys. Outputs/variables with sensitive = true redact UI but still store in state -
consider remote state encryption.
**Q24. Answer: B** - Large states cause slow plans due to refresh parallelism. Tuning -parallelism, reducing data sources,
splitting into micro-stacks (or TF Stacks), and using provider performance features drastically improves time. -target and
-refresh=false are dangerous long-term.
**Q25. Answer: B** - check blocks decoupled health checks from lifecycle (non-blocking, continue on error).
precondition/postcondition are blocking assertions evaluated during plan/apply for contract enforcement, essential for
policy-as-code without Sentinel/OPA.
**Q26. Answer: B** - Terraform Stacks (.tfstack.hcl / .tfcomponent.hcl) provide orchestration layer for components across
deployments. Unlike workspaces, they manage dependencies, order, and multi-region matrix deployments natively in HCP
Terraform.
**Q27. Answer: B** - ignore_changes is powerful for externally mutated fields but hides drift. For JSON fields, Terraform
normalizes ordering, so use jsonencode() consistently. Ignoring all tags with provider default_tags interaction requires
ignoring tags not tags_all.
**Q28. Answer: B** - Provider retry + parallelism tuning are primary defenses against rate limits. Ephemeral resources (TF
1.10+) avoid persisting sensitive short-lived data in state. removed block gracefully deletes without requiring refresh of
already-removed infra.
**Q29. Answer: B** - Native TF test framework (1.6+) runs terraform test, supports mocks and assertions without external
tooling, enabling fast unit testing. Terratest remains for integration/e2e testing with real infra, but native tests are preferred for
module contracts.
**Q30. Answer: B** - AWS provider v4 split S3 bucket inline args into standalone resources to fix perpetual diffs. Safe
migration requires provider upgrade plus config refactoring without bucket recreation. Plan should show only new resources
being added; if it shows bucket recreation, inline args not fully removed.

**Q2: Which file is checked FIRST for ansible configuration when running ansible-playbook?**

B) /etc/ansible/ansible.cfg
C) ANSIBLE_CONFIG environment variable
D) ./ansible.cfg in current directory
**Q3: What does an Ansible ad-hoc command do compared to a playbook?**
A) It runs a single task/module on hosts without writing a YAML file
B) It installs Ansible collections permanently
C) It encrypts inventory files
D) It only works with localhost
**Q4: In Ansible, what is idempotency?**
A) The ability to run a task multiple times with the same result and no unintended changes on subsequent runs
B) The ability to run playbooks in parallel across 1000s of hosts
C) The ability to rollback automatically on failure
D) The ability to encrypt variables at rest
**Q5: What is the default connection method Ansible uses for Linux hosts?**
A) WinRM
B) Paramiko
C) SSH
D) Local exec
**Q6: You run ansible all -m ping. What does the ping module actually test?**
A) ICMP network connectivity like the Linux ping command
B) If the host can reach internet
C) If Ansible can log in and execute Python code on the target
D) If the Ansible controller is reachable
**Q7: What is the correct YAML syntax for a basic playbook targeting webservers?**
A) - hosts: webservers / tasks: - ping
- name: Test connectivity
hosts: webservers
tasks:
- name: Ping hosts
ansible.builtin.ping:
C) hosts=webservers task=ping
D) [webservers] ping

--- PAGE 42 ---
**Q8: Where are Ansible facts gathered from, by default?**
A) From group_vars/all.yml
B) From the Ansible controller's environment variables
C) From the target host at the start of a play via the setup module
D) From Ansible Galaxy
**Q9: Which command installs a role from Ansible Galaxy?**
A) ansible-galaxy role install <role_name>
B) ansible install role <role_name>
C) ansible-playbook --install-role <role_name>
D) pip install ansible-role-<role_name>
**Q10: What does become: yes do in a play or task?**
A) Enables privilege escalation (e.g., sudo to root)
B) Becomes the inventory file
C) Enables async mode
D) Forces Ansible to become idempotent
Beginner Answer Key & Explanations
**Q1 Answer: B** - Inventory is the source of truth for managed nodes, defining hostnames/IPs and group hierarchies.
Without an inventory, Ansible doesn't know where to run tasks.
**Q2 Answer: C** - Precedence order is: ANSIBLE_CONFIG env var > ./ansible.cfg (cwd) > ~/.ansible.cfg >
/etc/ansible/ansible.cfg. Since ansible-core 2.12, this is strictly enforced.
**Q3 Answer: A** - Ad-hoc commands use ansible -m <module> -a args pattern for quick one-off operations. Playbooks are
for repeatable, multi-task automation in YAML.
**Q4 Answer: A** - Most modules are designed to be idempotent, checking state before acting. This makes automation safe
to rerun and critical for configuration management.
**Q5 Answer: C** - The default connection plugin is ssh (now ansible.builtin.ssh). WinRM is used for Windows, and paramiko
is a fallback option.
**Q6 Answer: C** - The ping module does NOT use ICMP; it logs in over SSH/WinRM and runs a small Python snippet
returning pong. Failure indicates authentication, Python, or connection issues.
**Q7 Answer: B** - A playbook is a list of plays with name, hosts, and tasks keys. Option B follows ansible-lint and
ansible.builtin FQCN best practices for 2024+.
**Q8 Answer: C** - By default gather_facts: true runs ansible.builtin.setup at play start, collecting OS, IP, memory facts into
ansible_facts. Disable for faster execution on known targets.
**Q9 Answer: A** - ansible-galaxy role install and ansible-galaxy collection install are the legacy/standard commands. In
2024+ with ansible-core 2.14+, you can also use ansible-galaxy install -r requirements.yml.
**Q10 Answer: A** - become abstracts sudo/su/runas/doas via become_method. It is the modern replacement for the old
sudo: yes which was removed years ago.
2. Intermediate Level (10 Questions)
**Q11: What is the correct variable precedence for group_vars vs host_vars vs play vars?**
A) host_vars > group_vars/all > play vars: > extra vars -e
B) extra vars -e > host_vars > group_vars > role defaults
C) role defaults > group_vars > host_vars > extra vars
D) Inventory vars never override playbook vars
**Q12: How do you safely handle a task that may fail but should not stop the play?**
- command: /bin/false
ignore_errors: yes

--- PAGE 43 ---
B) failed_when: false
C) any_errors_fatal: false
D) Both A and B are valid depending on intent, but B still marks as changed vs failed
**Q13: What does a Handler do in Ansible?**
A) Catches Python exceptions from modules
B) A task that runs only when notified via notify and only once at the end of the play (by default)
C) Handles inventory connections
D) Manages Ansible Vault passwords
**Q14: You need to loop over a list of users and create them. What is the modern (2024+) preferred loop syntax?**
A) with_items: "{{ users }}"
B) loop: "{{ users }}" with loop_control if needed
C) for_each: users
D) with_list:
**Q15: What is Ansible Vault used for?**
A) To speed up playbook execution by caching facts
B) To encrypt sensitive data like passwords and keys at rest in YAML files
C) To store SSH keys for the controller
D) To lock inventory files from editing
**Q16: Consider this task:**
- template:
src: nginx.conf.j2
dest: /etc/nginx/nginx.conf
notify: Reload nginx
When will Reload nginx handler run if the task runs on 3 hosts but only 1 host changed?
A) On all 3 hosts at end of play
B) Only on the 1 host where file changed, once at end of play
C) Immediately after the task on changed host
D) Never, unless --force-handlers is used
**Q17: What's the difference between ansible.builtin.include_tasks and ansible.builtin.import_tasks?**
A) No difference, they are aliases
B) import_tasks is static/pre-processed at playbook parse time, include_tasks is dynamic at runtime and can be
looped/conditional
C) include_tasks is static, import_tasks is dynamic
D) import_tasks was removed in ansible-core 2.17
**Q18: How do you install collections in an air-gapped or CI/CD reproducible way (Ansible 2.16+)?**
A) ansible-galaxy collection install -r requirements.yml checked into repo with pinned versions
B) Always use ansible-galaxy collection install community.general on controller without requirements
C) Copy collections to /usr/share/ansible
D) Use pip install for collections
**Q19: In a role, what is the execution order of defaults/main.yml, vars/main.yml, tasks/main.yml, and meta/main.yml?**
A) meta dependencies run first, then defaults (lowest precedence), vars override many, and tasks use the final merged vars
B) tasks > defaults > vars > meta
C) All run simultaneously
D) vars is lowest, defaults is highest
**Q20: What does ansible-inventory --graph and ansible-inventory -i inv --host <hostname> help with?**

--- PAGE 44 ---
A) First shows group hierarchy visually, second shows composed variables for a specific host after all
group_vars/host_vars/plugins resolution
B) Both install inventory plugins
C) First encrypts inventory, second decrypts
D) First is deprecated in favor of ansible --graph
Intermediate Answer Key & Explanations
**Q11 Answer: B** - Extra vars (-e) always win (precedence 21). Host_vars beats group_vars, which beats play vars, which
beats role defaults (lowest). This is crucial for environment overrides like dev vs prod.
**Q12 Answer: D** - ignore_errors: yes ignores failure but shows error, failed_when: false never marks failed. Use
failed_when with conditions for semantic failure detection, e.g., failed_when: rc not in [0,2].
**Q13 Answer: B** - Handlers are idempotent action coalescers, notified only on changed status. Use listen: topic to notify
multiple handlers, and meta: flush_handlers to run mid-play.
**Q14 Answer: B** - loop replaced with_* lookups since 2.5. with_items still works but is not recommended. loop supports
loop_control: {label, pause, extended} and filters better.
**Q15 Answer: B** - Vault encrypts YAML/files via AES256: ansible-vault encrypt_string, encrypt, view. In 2024+, use multiple
vault-ids with vault-id labels for team workflows.
**Q16 Answer: B** - Handlers run once per host if that host triggered notify via a changed task, batched at end of play. Use
flush_handlers or --force-handlers changes behavior on failure.
**Q17 Answer: B** - Imports are static: cannot be looped, condition applies to all imported tasks, tags are inherited differently.
Includes are dynamic, support loops, and are evaluated at runtime. Critical for large playbooks.
**Q18 Answer: A** - Pinning in requirements.yml with name: community.general version: 9.4.0 + ansible-galaxy install -r
requirements.yml ensures reproducible builds. Execution Environments also bundle them.
**Q19 Answer: A** - Role dependency resolution: meta/main.yml dependencies execute before parent role. defaults has
lowest precedence (meant to be overridden), vars/main.yml has high precedence (hardcoded role intent).
**Q20 Answer: A** - Inventory variable composition is complex with constructed, group_vars, host_vars plugins. These two
commands are the primary debugging tools before you actually run a playbook.
3. Advanced Level (10 Questions)
**Q21: You are on ansible-core 2.17+ where ansible-test dropped Python 3.8. In an Execution Environment (EE), what is the
role of execution-environment.yml and ansible-builder?**
A) It defines the base image, Python dependencies, collections, system packages, and bindeps to build a container image

**Q22: What is the difference between strategy: free vs strategy: linear vs strategy: host_pinned?**
A) No difference
B) linear (default): all hosts execute task N before any host starts task N+1; free: each host runs through tasks as fast as
possible without batch lockstep; host_pinned: like free but sticky to same worker in AWX/serial batches for fact cache
optimization
C) free runs tasks in random order
D) host_pinned was removed in 2023
**Q23: You need to patch 200 hosts with zero downtime, 20 at a time, and abort if >25% fail in a batch. Correct pattern?**
- hosts: web
serial: 20
max_fail_percentage: 25
tasks: [...]

--- PAGE 45 ---
B) serial: 20% max_fail_percentage: 25%
C) throttle: 20
D) forks: 20
**Q24: How do you correctly filter/sanitize untrusted input from ansible_facts in a role consumed by others (avoid injection via
Jinja2 templating)?**
A) Use {{ user_input }} directly
B) Use {{ user_input | string }} and avoid eval style via | ansible.builtin.quote for shell or rely on module argument escaping,
not string concatenation in shell/command; enable jinja2_native and avoid unsafe promotion
C) Ansible automatically sanitizes all facts
D) Disable facts gathering
**Q25: You write a custom filter plugin filter_plugins/custom.py. In ansible-core 2.17, after adding
ANSIBLE_JINJA2_NATIVE_WARNING=False, you see performance degradation when returning dicts. Why?**
A) Filter plugins should return native Python types and in 2.14+ jinja2_native=True is default in config, causing type
preservation but templating overhead; returning complex types is fine, but you must ensure FilterModule.filters dict mapping is
correct and avoid re-templating large dicts via | to_json unnecessarily
B) Filter plugins cannot return dicts
C) Python version is too old
D) You must use query not filter
**Q26: Consider using ansible.builtin.reboot module in a play with serial: 10. Reboots hang after kernel updates on RHEL 9.
What is the robust best practice fix in 2024-2026?**
A) Use reboot_timeout: 600, connect_timeout: 20, test_command: whoami, and post_reboot_delay: 30 plus reboot -t now
verification
B) Just increase forks
C) Use command: reboot
D) Disable gather_facts
**Q27: How does ansible-core handle delegate_to and run_once interaction with hostvars and delegate_facts?**
A) delegate_to: otherhost runs task on other host but hostvars[inventory_hostname] still refers to original host;
delegate_facts: true assigns facts to delegated host not original; run_once: true runs on first host in batch but applies to all
hosts unless delegate_to and loop are used carefully
B) delegate_to changes inventory_hostname
C) run_once runs on all hosts
D) delegate_facts is ignored
**Q28: You are migrating from with_items: "{{ some_registered_var.results }}" to loop. After register: result on a looped task,
what structure does result.results have and how do you correctly loop over it in next task?**
A) result.results is list of dicts each with item, ansible_facts, changed, failed; iterate with loop: "{{ result.results }}" and access
item.item for original item
B) result.results is a dict
C) result no longer contains results
D) Use with_items: result
**Q29: In Molecule 6.x (2024+) for testing Ansible roles, what driver and provisioner combo is recommended for minimal CI
without Docker?**
A) driver: podman + provisioner: ansible is common, or driver: delegated with ephemeral VMs; Molecule 6 dropped docker
driver as built-in, requiring molecule-plugins and recommends Podman for rootless; Use molecule test --destroy=never is

B) Use driver: docker only
C) Molecule is deprecated
D) Use driver: ec2 only
**Q30: You need to implement dynamic inventory from AWS with filtering, composition, and caching (amazon.aws collection
8.x). What's correct?**

--- PAGE 46 ---
A) Use aws_ec2 inventory plugin YAML file demo.aws_ec2.yml with plugin: amazon.aws.aws_ec2, filters: {tag:Env: prod},
keyed_groups:, compose:, cache: true, cache_plugin: jsonfile, and run with ansible-inventory -i demo.aws_ec2.yml --graph
B) Use static inventory only

Advanced Answer Key & Explanations
**Q21 Answer: A** - Execution Environments provide immutable controller images replacing pip venvs. ansible-builder build
uses execution-environment.yml with dependencies: {python: requirements.txt, system: bindeps.txt, galaxy:
requirements.yml}. Required for Automation Platform 2.4+ (2024).
**Q22 Answer: B** - linear ensures ordered steps for rolling deployments needing synchronization. free maximizes speed at
cost of inter-host ordering. host_pinned (from 2.12) improves performance for large inventories by pinning hosts to workers,
crucial for AWX/AAP.
**Q23 Answer: A** - serial allows rolling/batch updates (number or percentage). Combined with max_fail_percentage
(allowed failure percent in batch before aborting whole play) it's the standard SRE pattern for safe patching. throttle limits
concurrent tasks within a batch, forks is global parallelism.
**Q24 Answer: B** - Ansible's command/shell with string interpolation is injection-prone. Always use args list via argv, or
quote filter for shell. Module args are escaped, but shell: "echo {{ user_var }}" is unsafe. jinja2_native changes typing behavior
important for security.
**Q25 Answer: A** - Since ansible-core 2.14, jinja2_native = True default preserves native types reducing string round-trips
but requires filter plugins to return proper Python types. Performance issues usually come from unnecessary serialization or
bad plugin caching, not dict returns themselves.
**Q26 Answer: A** - RHEL 9/cloud boots can take >300s after kernel updates. reboot_timeout is total wait, connect_timeout
is per attempt, test_command validates readiness (default whoami may fail before cloud-init), use uptime | awk '{print $3}'
alternative checks. Never use raw reboot command.
**Q27 Answer: A** - This triad is a frequent bug source. inventory_hostname never changes with delegation. delegate_facts
controls fact assignment target. run_once executes on batch's first host context, so hostvars and when must be explicit to
avoid applying first-host vars everywhere.
**Q28 Answer: A** - Registered loop results store 1 entry per loop iteration in .results. Next task needs nested item.item
access. Common pitfall: forgetting results is list, not dict, and using item directly instead of item.item or item.ansible_facts.
**Q29 Answer: A** - Molecule 6.0 (2024) refactored drivers out to molecule-plugins. Docker driver requires external plugin,
Podman is preferred rootless for GitHub Actions. delegated driver points at Terraform-provisioned infrastructure for realistic
tests.
**Q30 Answer: A** - Current amazon.aws.aws_ec2 plugin (replacing aws_ec2 short name) supports filters, keyed_groups:
[{prefix: env, key: tags.Env}], compose: {ansible_host: public_ip_address}, and caching to avoid API rate limits. The legacy
ec2.py script was removed in Dec 2023.

Course File: 08_Jenkins.md
Course: Jenkins
1. Beginner Level (10 Questions)
**Q1: What is Jenkins primarily used for in software development?**
A) Container orchestration
B) Continuous Integration and Continuous Delivery (CI/CD) automation
C) Database management
D) Machine learning model training
**Q2: What is the default language for Jenkins pipelines defined via Jenkinsfile?**
A) YAML
B) Groovy-based DSL
C) Python
D) Shell Script
**Q3: In a Declarative Pipeline, which block is mandatory and defines the execution environment?**
A) tools
B) stages
C) agent
D) environment
**Q4: Which directory convention does Jenkins use to store job configurations and builds by default?**
A) JENKINS_HOME
B) /etc/jenkins
C) /var/lib/jenkins_config
D) ~/.jenkins
**Q5: What is the purpose of the post section in a Declarative Jenkinsfile?**
A) To publish artifacts to Nexus
B) To define actions that run after pipeline execution completes (e.g., always, success, failure)
C) To trigger post-commit hooks in Git
D) To send HTTP POST requests
**Q6: Which plugin is required to run Docker containers as build agents in Jenkins?**
A) Docker Pipeline plugin
B) Kubernetes plugin
C) Docker plugin / Docker Agent templates
D) Containerd plugin
**Q7: What does the checkout scm step do in a Jenkinsfile?**
A) Checks out the source code defined in the job's SCM configuration
B) Validates SCM credentials
C) Creates a new SCM repository
D) Merges branches automatically
**Q8: Which of the following is TRUE about Freestyle vs Pipeline jobs?**
A) Freestyle jobs support Jenkinsfile
B) Pipeline jobs are legacy and deprecated since 2024
C) Freestyle jobs are configured via UI forms, while Pipeline jobs are defined as code and versioned

--- PAGE 48 ---
D) Pipeline jobs cannot use plugins
**Q9: How do you manually trigger a parameterized build with a STRING parameter named VERSION in Jenkins UI?**
A) Click "Build Now"
B) Click "Build with Parameters" and enter VERSION value
C) Run jenkins-cli build VERSION
D) Edit config.xml directly
**Q10: What is Jenkins Blue Ocean?**
A) A security hardening tool for Jenkins
B) A modern, visual user interface for creating and visualizing pipelines
C) A cloud hosting service for Jenkins
D) A backup and restore plugin
Beginner Answer Key & Explanations
**Q1 Answer: B**
Jenkins is an open-source automation server focused on CI/CD. It automates building, testing, and deploying code, enabling
rapid feedback loops.
**Q2 Answer: B**
Jenkins pipelines use a Groovy-based Domain Specific Language (DSL) in Jenkinsfile. Declarative and Scripted syntaxes are
both built on top of Groovy.
**Q3 Answer: C**
The agent directive is required in Declarative Pipeline and specifies where the pipeline or stage executes (any, none, label,
docker, kubernetes). Without it, the pipeline has no executor.
**Q4 Answer: A**
JENKINS_HOME (default /var/jenkins_home or ~/.jenkins) contains all jobs, builds, plugins, and configs. Backing up this
directory is standard for Jenkins backup.
**Q5 Answer: B**
The post block runs conditionally after pipeline completion, e.g., always { junit ... }, failure { slackSend ... }. It is ideal for
cleanup, notifications, and test reporting.
**Q6 Answer: C**
The Docker plugin allows defining Docker Agent templates, while Docker Pipeline plugin provides docker DSL. For ephemeral
agents via Docker, Docker plugin/cloud config is the core requirement.
**Q7 Answer: B**
Actually Answer: A - checkout scm clones the repository using the SCM configuration from the Multibranch or Pipeline job
definition. It ensures Jenkinsfile and sources use same branch/commit.
**Q8 Answer: C**
Freestyle is UI-driven and older, hard to version. Pipeline-as-code via Jenkinsfile enables code review, versioning, durability,
and complex workflows — the standard since 2020+.
**Q9 Answer: B**
Parameterized jobs expose "Build with Parameters" in UI, allowing inputs before run. "Build Now" uses defaults without
prompting.
**Q10 Answer: B**
Blue Ocean provides a pipeline-centric UX with visual editor and stage visualization. While maintenance mode since 2023, it
remains widely recognized for pipeline visualization.
2. Intermediate Level (10 Questions)
**Q11: In Declarative Pipeline, what is the difference between agent any at top-level vs agent none with per-stage agents?**
A) No difference

--- PAGE 49 ---
B) agent any allocates one executor for entire pipeline; agent none defers allocation allowing each stage to use different
agents and saving executors when idle
C) agent none is invalid syntax
D) agent any only works on master
**Q12: You need credentials with ID aws-prod in a pipeline without exposing them in logs. Which is the secure pattern?**
A) env.AWS_KEY = credentials('aws-prod')
B) withCredentials([string(credentialsId: 'aws-prod', variable: 'AWS_KEY')]) { sh 'echo $AWS_KEY' }
C) withCredentials([usernamePassword...]) always masks values, but you must use double-quoted Groovy interpolation
carefully and avoid echo in plain sh
D) Store in Jenkinsfile as plaintext
**Q13: How do you achieve parallel execution of tests in Declarative Pipeline in 2024+ syntax?**
A) Using parallel directive inside a stage with nested stages
B) Using parallel { } step directly — only available in Scripted
C) Parallel is not supported in Declarative
D) Using throttle plugin only
**Q14: What does options { skipDefaultCheckout() } do, and when would you use it?**
A) Disables automatic checkout scm at start of Declarative Pipeline to manually control checkout timing for efficiency or
custom workspace handling
B) Skips all future checkouts
C) Deletes workspace
D) Disables SCM polling
**Q15: How does Jenkins handle shared logic across pipelines in enterprise setups?**
A) Copy-paste Jenkinsfiles
B) Shared Libraries loaded via @Library('my-lib') _ with vars/ and src/ structure
C) Symlinking Jenkinsfiles
D) Shared Libraries only work for Freestyle jobs
**Q16: Consider: when { branch 'main'; changeset 'src/**' }. What does this do?**
A) Runs stage only if building main branch AND changes occurred in src/**
B) Runs stage if either condition true
C) Invalid syntax — when cannot have multiple conditions
D) Runs only if files were deleted in src/**
**Q17: You have flaky integration tests. Which Declarative option best handles automatic retry?**
A) options { retry(3) } at stage level
B) stage { options { retry(3) } } or retry(3) { } wrapped in script step
C) Use loop plugin
D) post { retry}
**Q18: How do you archive artifacts and fingerprint them for traceability between builds?**
A) archiveArtifacts artifacts: 'target/*.jar', fingerprint: true, onlyIfSuccessful: true
B) archive 'target/*.jar'
C) stash '*.jar'
D) publishArtifacts
**Q19: In Jenkins distributed builds, what is the difference between Master/Agent (formerly Master/Slave) with JNLP vs SSH
agents launched in Kubernetes?**
A) No difference
B) JNLP agents have controller initiate connection; SSH agents have agent initiate; Kubernetes plugin dynamically provisions
ephemeral pods as agents using JNLP in pod template
C) Kubernetes agents cannot run pipelines

--- PAGE 50 ---
D) SSH launcher is deprecated in 2025
**Q20: How do webhooks trigger Jenkins Multibranch Pipeline efficiently vs SCM polling?**
A) Polling is faster than webhooks
B) Webhooks (e.g., GitHub webhook to /github-webhook/) trigger immediate indexing and build for changed branches/PRs,
reducing load; polling scans repo periodically wasting resources
C) Webhooks only work for GitLab
D) Multibranch cannot use webhooks
Intermediate Answer Key & Explanations
**Q11 Answer: B**
agent any locks an executor for whole run, inefficient if pipeline waits. agent none + per-stage agent { label '...' } allocates only
when needed, enabling different labels/containers per stage.
**Q12 Answer: C**
withCredentials binds credentials to env vars and Jenkins masks them in logs (with credential masking). However, using
Groovy double quotes "$TOKEN" can cause interpolation before masking; best to use single quotes in sh and let shell
expand, e.g., sh 'printenv | grep ...' should avoid printing.
**Q13 Answer: A**
Declarative Pipeline since 2019 supports stage('Test') { parallel { stage('Unit') { steps {...} } stage('Integration') {...} } }. This is
the standards-compliant way; Scripted uses parallel mapOfBranches.
**Q14 Answer: A**
By default Declarative checks out SCM automatically. skipDefaultCheckout() disables it so you can e.g., checkout in specific
stage, do sparse checkout, or avoid double checkout when using agent docker that would otherwise checkout twice.
**Q15 Answer: B**
Shared Libraries (@Library) define reusable steps in vars/*.groovy and classes in src/. Loaded implicitly via global config or
explicitly via annotation, they enable DRY, governance, and versioning (e.g., @Library('my-lib@1.2')).
**Q16 Answer: A**
Multiple conditions inside same when are ANDed by default; use anyOf, allOf, not for logic. Here stage runs only on main
branch when changeset includes files under src/, useful for monorepo optimization.
**Q17 Answer: B**
Stage-level options { retry(n) } is valid for retrying whole stage. Alternatively retry(n) { sh '...' } works in scripted context. Post
does not support retry logic directly.
**Q18 Answer: A**
archiveArtifacts stores files on controller for download; fingerprint: true tracks which build produced which artifact for
dependency tracing. stash/unstash is for inter-stage transfer within same build, not long-term.
**Q19 Answer: B**
Traditional JNLP: agent connects to controller (firewall friendly). SSH: controller connects to agent. Kubernetes plugin
launches pod with jnlp container that connects back, creating ephemeral, scalable, isolated builds — standard in 2024-2026
cloud-native setups.
**Q20 Answer: B**
Webhook-driven triggers push model: Git provider POSTs to Jenkins on push/PR, instant. SCM polling (e.g., * * * * *) pulls
periodically, high load and delay. For Multibranch, GitHub Branch Source plugin handles webhooks optimally.
3. Advanced Level (10 Questions)
**Q21: You use Jenkinsfile with agent { kubernetes { yaml ''' apiVersion: v1 kind: Pod spec: containers: - name: golang image:
golang:1.22 command: [cat] tty: true ''' } }. The container keeps exiting. Root cause and fix?**
A) Need args for image
B) Command must keep container alive; [cat] is correct but you must disable default tty override — issue is missing yamlFile
parameter

--- PAGE 51 ---
C) Kubernetes plugin requires containers to stay running; command: [cat] + tty: true is actually correct pattern, but failure
likely due to resourceQuota or missing jnlp container — ensure pod template includes implicit jnlp sidecar or specify
defaultContainer
D) Use sleep infinity
**Q22: How do you prevent Script Security sandbox violations when using Shared Library src/ classes that call Jenkins
pipeline steps?**
A) Disable sandbox
B) Steps are not accessible in src/ — need to pass Script context (this or steps) to class, or make class implement
Serializable and use vars/ wrapper; or use @NonCPS appropriately
C) Annotate all methods with @Trusted
D) Shared libraries bypass sandbox entirely
**Q23: Jenkins controller shows high GC pressure and java.lang.OutOfMemoryError after migrating to Jenkins 2.479+ (Java
21, Jakarta). Most likely mitigation?**
A) Jenkinsfile has memory leak
B) Since Jenkins 2.479+ requires Java 17/21 and Jakarta EE 9, plugins must be updated; old plugins cause metaspace leaks;
increase -Xmx and -XX:MaxMetaspaceSize, update Pipeline: Groovy plugin to 2.114+ and remove deprecated Unsafe
usages; also use jenkins.model.Jenkins.crumbIssuer optimization
C) Downgrade to Java 8
D) Increase executors on controller to 0 is already required — OOM unrelated
**Q24: You need to implement a Jenkins Pipeline that builds Docker images without Docker-in-Docker (DinD) security risk, in
Kubernetes. Best 2024-2026 practice?**
A) Mount docker.sock
B) Use Kaniko, Buildah, or docker buildx with podman rootless, or AWS CodeBuild style; e.g., container
gcr.io/kaniko-project/executor:debug with args: --dockerfile=--context=--destination=
C) Always use DinD, it's secure now
D) Use docker { } DSL with privileged true
**Q25: What is the purpose and risk of org.jenkinsci.plugins.scriptsecurity.sandbox.groovy.SecureGroovyScript approval in
"In-process Script Approval"?**
A) Approves entire pipeline
B) Admin must approve Groovy methods/signatures that escape sandbox; over-approving method java.lang.Runtime exec
creates RCE; should minimize approvals and prefer Shared Library trusted code
C) Automatically approves all plugins
D) No risk
**Q26: You have a large monorepo with 50 microservices. You want Jenkins to build only affected services using changeset
AND to avoid rebuilding if Jenkinsfile didn't change. Optimal strategy?**
A) Use when { changeset pattern comparator } per service stage + not { changeset Jenkinsfile}? Better: use Generic
Webhook Trigger parsing commit diff, or use pipeline logic with git diff --name-only in script block and set env flags; combine
with Shared Library that computes build matrix dynamically and use parallel dynamic stages created in script block
B) Build all always
C) Separate repo per service only solution
D) Use Multibranch for each service folder impossible
**Q27: How does Jenkins achieve Configuration as Code (JCasC) and ephemeral controllers in production (2024-2026)?**
A) JCasC YAML + Job DSL + Helm chart with EFS; controller bootstraps from CASC_JENKINS_CONFIG env, zero manual
UI config, secrets via Vault plugin or Kubernetes secrets; jobs recreated via Seed job
B) Manual UI only
C) JCasC deprecated in 2025
D) Groovy init scripts only
**Q28: Explain CPS (Continuation Passing Style) transformation issue causing NotSerializableException or
org.jenkinsci.plugins.workflow.cps.CpsCallableInvocation error in pipeline.**

--- PAGE 52 ---
A) Jenkins pipeline must checkpoint; all variables in pipeline scope must be Serializable; local non-serializable objects inside
script should be scoped with def inside method or annotated @NonCPS; avoid iterating non-serializable iterators across sh
steps
B) Bug in Jenkins
C) Means you need more memory
D) Use serializable true
**Q29: You need to implement strict compliance: audit trail, RBAC per folder, build provenance (SLSA) and prevent sh 'curl |
bash'. Architecture?**
A) Impossible in Jenkins
B) Use Role-based Authorization Strategy + Folders Plus, Audit Trail plugin, Pipeline: Groovy library restrictions (reject
arbitrary sh?), Warnings NG, use OPA/Conftest in Shared Library validator to block untrusted steps, generate SLSA

**Q30: A Declarative Pipeline uses post { always { junit 'build/test-results/**/*.xml' } } but flaky test upload fails silently causing
build UNSTABLE misclassification. How to make robust and collect Prometheus metrics for pipeline duration per stage?**
A) Use junit testResults:..., allowEmptyResults: true, skipPublishingChecks: false and wrap in script { try { } catch { unstable()
} }; for metrics use OpenTelemetry plugin + Prometheus plugin exposing jenkins_pipeline_stage_duration_seconds and

B) Ignore
C) post cannot use junit
D) Use HTML publisher only
Advanced Answer Key & Explanations
**Q21 Answer: C**
Kubernetes plugin auto injects jnlp container unless yaml overrides spec completely; if you provide full yaml, you must still
ensure JNLP connectivity and resource limits. Pattern command: [cat] tty: true keeps container alive, but pod failure often due
to OOMKilled or missing serviceAccount. Debug via kubectl get pod -o yaml and Jenkins podTemplate logs.
**Q22 Answer: B**
src/ code runs as pure Groovy classes, not CPS-transformed pipeline DSL, so sh, echo etc. are not directly available. Pass
script context: new MyClass(this).doThing() where this is pipeline script, then steps.sh. Also classes must implement
Serializable for checkpointing. vars/ are implicitly trusted and CPS-transformed.
**Q23 Answer: B**
Jenkins 2.479.1 switched to Spring Security 6 + Jakarta Servlet 6 + Java 17 minimum (Java 21 recommended). Outdated
plugins using javax.* cause ClassNotFound and memory leaks. Fix: upgrade all plugins, use latest workflow-cps, increase
heap to 4-8GB for large controllers, enable G1GC -XX:+UseG1GC.
**Q24 Answer: B**
Mounting docker.sock or privileged DinD gives root on node — security anti-pattern. Kaniko builds in userspace without
daemon, Buildah rootless, or docker buildx with remote builder. In Kubernetes, use Kaniko container template with volume for
Docker config secret, push without privileges.
**Q25 Answer: B**
Sandbox protects controller from malicious pipeline. Admin page Script Approvals lists pending signatures. Approving
dangerous signatures like System.exit, Runtime.exec, File methods opens remote code execution for any writer. Best
practice: keep approvals minimal, use Shared Libraries marked as trusted which bypass sandbox but are code-reviewed.
**Q26 Answer: A**
Declarative when { changeset } works for simple cases but limited to git comparison against previous build. For monorepo
scale, compute affected modules via git diff origin/main...HEAD --name-only in script, build dynamic map of parallel stages.
Use changeset as optimization but combine with Shared Library build-matrix generator. Consider Bazel/gradle detection for
true incremental.

--- PAGE 53 ---
**Q27 Answer: A**
Modern Jenkins on Kubernetes uses Helm + JCasC YAML (jenkins: systemMessage: credentials: etc.) mounted via
ConfigMap, seeds jobs via Job DSL seed job that scans repo for Jenkinsfiles. Controllers ephemeral: stateful part only
JENKINS_HOME/jobs optionally backed by PVC or externalized via S3 artifacts + CloudBees backup, secrets from Vault/K8s
Secrets.
**Q28 Answer: A**
Jenkins pipeline serializes execution state after each step (CPS). Non-serializable objects (e.g., Matcher, JsonSlurper result
without def, DB connections) held across steps cause failure. Fix: confine them to method with @NonCPS or local scope, or
make variable non-persistent, e.g., wrap in script { def data = readJSON ... }. Cannot use pipeline steps inside @NonCPS.
**Q29 Answer: B**
Enterprise compliance via: RBAC Folder-based Authorization, Audit Trail + JobConfigHistory plugins for audit, Script Security
+ Approved Shared Library only, Policy enforcement in Shared Library global wrapper (e.g., reject sh containing curl .. | sh),
plus supply-chain security: generate SBOM with cyclonedx plugin, attest via Sigstore Cosign and SLSA provenance for
artifact traceability.
**Q30 Answer: A**
allowEmptyResults prevents pipeline failure if no XML found, but you should catchError or unstable conditionally. For
observability, OpenTelemetry plugin (since 2023) emits traces/metrics to OTLP/Prometheus, providing per-stage duration,
queue time. Prometheus plugin exposes endpoint /prometheus. Create Grafana dashboard for MTTR and flake tracking.

1. Beginner Level (10 Questions)
**Q1: Which command displays the current working directory's absolute path?**

**Q2: In the Linux Filesystem Hierarchy Standard (FHS), where are essential user binaries (like ls, cp) typically located in
modern systemd-based distros (2024+)?**

B) /sbin
C) /opt/bin
D) /usr/local/bin
**Q3: What does chmod 754 myfile.sh mean in terms of permissions?**
A) Owner: rwx, Group: r-x, Others: r--
B) Owner: rwx, Group: r-x, Others: r--
C) Owner: rwx, Group: r--, Others: r-x (Note: 754 = rwxr-xr--)
D) Owner: rwx, Group: r-x, Others: r-- Wait - let's be precise: 7=rwx, 5=r-x, 4=r--
A) Owner: rwx, Group: r-x, Others: r--
B) Owner: rwx, Group: r--, Others: --x
C) Owner: rwxr-xr-- (Owner rwx, Group r-x, Others r--)
D) Owner: r--, Group: rwx, Others: r-x
**Q4: Which command correctly appends the line "hello" to file.txt without overwriting it?**

**Q8: You try to delete a file but get "Permission denied". You check with ls -l and see -r--r--r-- 1 root root. As a regular user,
what is fundamentally preventing deletion?**
A) The file's read-only permissions prevent deletion; you need to chmod it
B) Deletion depends on the parent directory's write and execute permissions, not the file's permissions
C) Only root can ever delete files owned by root
D) The file has the immutable attribute set
**Q9: What does the command sudo apt update && sudo apt upgrade -y do on Ubuntu/Debian (2024)?**
A) Updates all installed packages to latest versions after refreshing package index
B) Upgrades the distribution to the next Ubuntu release
C) Updates only the apt tool itself
D) Cleans apt cache and reinstalls all packages
**Q10: How can you view the contents of a file named large.log page by page, with forward/backward scrolling and search?**
A) cat large.log
B) less large.log
C) more large.log
D) open large.log
Beginner Answer Key & Explanations
**Q1 Answer: B) pwd - print working directory**
Explanation: pwd is a POSIX-standard builtin that prints the absolute canonical path of the current directory. It is essential for
scripting and navigation to confirm location.
**Q2 Answer: A) /bin -> /usr/bin**
Explanation: Since the usr-merge (completed in Debian 12, Ubuntu 20.04+, Fedora, RHEL 9), /bin is a symlink to /usr/bin for
compatibility. All essential binaries actually live in /usr/bin.
**Q3 Answer: C) Owner rwx (7), Group r-x (5), Others r-- (4) -> rwxr-xr--**
Explanation: The octal 754 translates as 7=4+2+1=rwx, 5=4+1=r-x, 4=r--. This is a common permission for a script that owner
can edit/execute while others can read/execute or only read.
**Q4 Answer: B) echo "hello" >> file.txt**
Explanation: >> is the append redirection operator while > truncates and overwrites. Appending is critical for logging and
building files safely without data loss.
**Q5 Answer: B) ls -la or ls -l -a**
Explanation: -a shows all files including dotfiles, and -l uses long listing format with permissions, owner, size, and timestamp.
Combined as -la it is the standard way to audit a directory.
**Q6 Answer: B) Recursively searches for the string "error" in all files under /var/log/**
Explanation: -r makes grep recursive, searching subdirectories. This command is a fundamental log troubleshooting pattern
for sysadmins diagnosing system issues.
**Q7 Answer: B) man ls**
Explanation: man displays the manual page from the system's man database, the canonical documentation for commands,
system calls, and config files. It's the primary help system since UNIX V7.
**Q8 Answer: B) Deletion depends on the parent directory's write and execute permissions, not the file's permissions**
Explanation: In Linux, unlinking a file modifies the directory, so you need wx on the directory. The file's own permissions
control reading/writing content, not deletion, unless sticky bit or immutable flag applies.
**Q9 Answer: A) Updates all installed packages to latest versions after refreshing package index**
Explanation: apt update refreshes package lists from repositories without installing anything, while apt upgrade installs newer
versions of installed packages. The && chains them safely and -y auto-confirms.
**Q10 Answer: B) less large.log**

--- PAGE 56 ---
Explanation: less is a pager that loads files efficiently on demand, supports backward scrolling, forward search with /, and
doesn't load the entire file into memory like cat. It's standard on all 2024+ distros.

**Q11: What is the difference between systemctl restart nginx and systemctl reload nginx?**

B) restart stops and starts the process (new PID, drops connections), reload re-reads config without terminating process if

B) It's a zombie process - its parent hasn't called wait() to read exit status. Fix by fixing/killing parent, not the defunct itself

C) Duplicates process ID 2 to process ID 1
D) Merges stdout with stdin
**Q14: What does the SUID bit do, and what is a security risk of chmod u+s /usr/bin/myapp?**

B) When set on executable, it runs with file owner privileges, not executor. Risk: if myapp is vulnerable, privilege escalation to
owner (often root)
C) It makes file hidden; risk is obscurity
D) It forces file into swap; risk is memory exhaustion

--- PAGE 57 ---
**Q19: How do you persistently add a static route on a system using NetworkManager (nmcli) in RHEL 9?**
A) route add -net 10.0.0.0/24 gw 192.168.1.1
B) nmcli connection modify eth0 +ipv4.routes "10.0.0.0/24 192.168.1.1" and nmcli con up eth0
C) Edit /etc/gateway.conf
D) ip route add alone is persistent across reboots
**Q20: In bash, what is the difference between source script.sh (or . script.sh) and ./script.sh?**
A) No difference
B) source runs in current shell environment (variables/functions persist), ./script.sh runs in a new subshell
C) ./script.sh is faster and sources env
D) source is only for Python scripts
Intermediate Answer Key & Explanations
**Q11 Answer: B) restart stops and starts the process (new PID, drops connections), reload re-reads config without
terminating process if supported**
Explanation: Restart causes downtime and breaks active connections as PID changes. Reload sends SIGHUP or equivalent
for zero-downtime config reload, critical for production web servers.
**Q12 Answer: B) It's a zombie process - its parent hasn't called wait() to read exit status. Fix by fixing/killing parent, not the
defunct itself**
Explanation: Defunct entries remain in process table until parent reaps them. Killing the zombie with kill -9 does nothing; you
must kill the parent so init/systemd re-parents and reaps it.
**Q13 Answer: A) Redirects FD 2 (stderr) to the same location as FD 1 (stdout), so both go to output.log**
Explanation: File descriptors 0=stdin,1=stdout,2=stderr. Order matters: > output.log 2>&1 must have stdout redirect first so
stderr copies its destination. Reversing order behaves differently.
**Q14 Answer: B) When set on executable, it runs with file owner privileges, not executor. Risk: if myapp is vulnerable,
privilege escalation to owner (often root)**
Explanation: SUID allows controlled privilege elevation like passwd or sudo. If set on arbitrary binaries or writable interpreters,
any local user can get root. Auditing SUID binaries with find / -perm -4000 is a core hardening step.
**Q15 Answer: A) find /home -size +500M -mtime -7 -type f**
Explanation: find predicates: +500M means greater than 500 MiB, -mtime -7 means modified less than 7*24h ago, -type f
regular files. It's efficient because it filters at filesystem level.
**Q16 Answer: B) Hard link still contains data as long as one link count >0; symlink becomes dangling/broken**
Explanation: Hard link shares same inode and data blocks; data persists while link count >=1. Symlink stores path string, so if
target is deleted it breaks. Hard links cannot cross filesystems.
**Q17 Answer: B) nftables is the default backend; iptables command is often a compatibility shim via iptables-nft using
nftables kernel API**
Explanation: Since Debian 10, RHEL 8+, Ubuntu 20.10+, kernel packet filtering uses nf_tables. The iptables binary uses
xtables2nft translation layer, seen via iptables -V and update-alternatives.
**Q18 Answer: B) Queries systemd journal for sshd unit logs in last hour with priority err and higher**
Explanation: journalctl is the systemd log query tool replacing /var/log/messages. Filtering by unit -u, time --since, and priority
-p err (0 emerg to 3 err) is essential for modern troubleshooting.
**Q19 Answer: B) nmcli connection modify eth0 +ipv4.routes "10.0.0.0/24 192.168.1.1" and nmcli con up eth0**
Explanation: ip route add is ephemeral until reboot. NetworkManager stores persistent config in
/etc/NetworkManager/system-connections/. Using nmcli with +ipv4.routes ensures survival after reboot and integration with
NM dispatcher.
**Q20 Answer: B) source runs in current shell environment (variables/functions persist), ./script.sh runs in a new subshell**
Explanation: Sourcing is required for environment setup scripts like venv activate, /etc/profile changes. Executing creates
child process that cannot modify parent shell's environment variables.

--- PAGE 58 ---

**Q21: Explain the Linux OOM Killer behavior with oom_score_adj. A container with cgroup v2 and high memory usage is
getting killed despite oom_score_adj = -1000 on its main process. Why?**
A) oom_score_adj is ignored in cgroup v2
B) cgroup-level OOM kills at cgroup level bypass per-process oom_score_adj; memory.max enforcement kills entire cgroup;
oom_score_adj only affects system-wide OOM selection, not cgroup limit enforcement
C) -1000 means kill first
D) OOM killer is disabled in containers
**Q22: You debug production latency spikes. perf top shows high overhead in native_queued_spin_lock_slowpath. cat
/proc/softirqs and /proc/interrupts look normal. Which is most likely root cause and next tool in 2024+?**

B) Severe lock contention on kernel spinlocks / qspinlock, often due to many vCPUs contending on same lock (e.g., mm,
scheduler). Next: perf c2c, bpftrace or lock_stat, check schedstat, reduce vCPU or sharding
C) Network bug; use tcpdump
D) Normal behavior, ignore
**Q23: Difference between PID namespace and cgroup in container isolation (Docker/Podman/K8s) and why you need
both?**
A) They are same
B) PID namespace provides visibility/isolation of PIDs and prevents seeing/killing host processes; cgroup provides resource
limiting/accounting (CPU, memory, io). Need both for security + resource fairness
C) PID namespace limits memory; cgroup limits PIDs
D) cgroup is deprecated in favor of PID namespace in kernel 6.x
**Q24: Your pod shows nsenter -t <PID> -n ip a has no interface, but nsenter -t <PID> -m shows files. What state? Also how

B) Network namespace is isolated/empty due to CNI failure to attach veth; mount namespace still has containerFS. Debug
with eBPF via bpftool, cilium monitor, tc programs to trace netlink CNI calls and packet drops without restarting pod

**Q25: Systemd user says systemd-run --scope --user -p MemoryMax=1G stress --vm 1 --vm-bytes 2G fails to limit. On

B) Must enable delegation via systemd Delegate=yes and enable controllers in

**Q26: Secure Boot + kexec + kernel lockdown: On a kernel in lockdown=confidentiality mode (2024+), what fails and why?**

B) kexec of unsigned kernel, /dev/mem access, BPF that reads kernel memory, and unsigned module loads are blocked
because lockdown prevents bypassing Secure Boot-verified integrity boundary by user still with root

**Q27: You tune a high-throughput NUMA server (2 sockets, NVMe, 100G NIC) Linux 6.6+. ethtool -G, sysctl
net.core.busy_poll, irqbalance disabled with manual affinity, but PPS still low and cat /proc/net/softnet_stat shows many
drops in column 2 and netstat -s shows RPS? Actually column 2 is time_squeezed. What combination fixes it?**
A) Increase ring buffer only
B) Enable RPS/RFS/XPS tuning, adjust netdev_budget and netdev_max_backlog, use XDP or AF_XDP, pin application to

C) Disable NUMA

**Q28: A process opens file then unlinks it while still holding FD. What happens to disk space and how do you recover content
on ext4/xfs (2024 kernel) without restarting?**
A) Space freed immediately
B) Space not freed until FD closed; inode stays allocated with link count 0. Recover via /proc/<pid>/fd/<fd> (readlink or cp), or
lsof +L1 shows, and /proc path provides access to inode data

**Q29: Compare OverlayFS (used in Docker/Container) performance vs native - why might find with many stat() be slow and
what workaround in 2024+?**
A) OverlayFS always fast
B) Overlay requires lookup in all lower layers for each file (metacopy + redirect) causing dentry chain walk and possible
metacopy check; use volatile mount for scratch, enable metacopy=on and index=on, or use fscache, or avoid excessive stat
by using st_dev check and caching, or use overlayfs-fast or bind mount for build dirs
C) Disable SELinux
D) No workaround
**Q30: You need to trace why execve() for a binary intermittently fails with EACCES despite rwx and no SELinux/AppArmor
denial. Auditd shows nothing. Which advanced Linux feature should you check? (Hint: 2024+ container/sandbox hardening)**
A) chmod
B) Check Landlock, seccomp-bpf filters, NoNewPrivs, BPF LSM, and Integrity (IMA/EVM) or mount noexec, or
fs.protected_regular and capability checks (FCaps). Use bpftool prog, grep Seccomp /proc/<pid>/status, cat
/proc/<pid>/attr/current, check landlock via audit or strace of landlock rules, and dmesg for AppArmor implicit deny
C) Reinstall OS
D) Disk failure
Advanced Answer Key & Explanations
**Q21 Answer: B) cgroup-level OOM kills at cgroup level bypass per-process oom_score_adj; memory.max enforcement kills
entire cgroup; oom_score_adj only affects system-wide OOM selection, not cgroup limit enforcement**
Explanation: In cgroup v2, hitting memory.max triggers cgroup allocator OOM, not system-wide OOM Killer. The cgroup
controller kills processes inside regardless of oom_score_adj=-1000 which only protects against global OOM. Fix is
increasing memory.max/memory.high or tuning memory.oom.group.
**Q22 Answer: B) Severe lock contention on kernel spinlocks / qspinlock, often due to many vCPUs contending on same lock
(e.g., mm, scheduler). Next: perf c2c, bpftrace or lock_stat, check schedstat, reduce vCPU or sharding**
Explanation: native_queued_spin_lock_slowpath in perf top is classic sign of highly contended spinlocks, especially on
overcommitted VMs or large SMP. Tools like bpftrace lock tracing, perf lock, or /proc/lock_stat (if enabled) pinpoint holders.
**Q23 Answer: B) PID namespace provides visibility/isolation of PIDs and prevents seeing/killing host processes; cgroup
provides resource limiting/accounting (CPU, memory, io). Need both for security + resource fairness**
Explanation: Namespaces (PID, NET, MNT, UTS, IPC, USER, Cgroup, Time) give illusion of isolated system. Cgroups don't
isolate visibility, only limit/account resources. Containers need both plus seccomp/SELinux/caps for full isolation.
**Q24 Answer: B) Network namespace is isolated/empty due to CNI failure to attach veth; mount namespace still has
containerFS. Debug with eBPF via bpftool, cilium monitor, tc programs to trace netlink CNI calls and packet drops without
restarting pod**
Explanation: Pod sandbox creation failure leaves netns empty. Using nsenter -n isolates network view. eBPF-based CNI
(Cilium, Calico eBPF) allows introspection with zero downtime eBPF programs on tc hook to trace why veth attach or routing
failed.
**Q25 Answer: B) Must enable delegation via systemd Delegate=yes and enable controllers in
/sys/fs/cgroup/user.slice/.../cgroup.subtree_control, ensure user has cgroup controller delegation; by default user slices may
not have memory controller enabled without systemctl set-property or logind EnableControllers**
Explanation: Cgroup v2 strict delegation model: parent must enable controllers for child via cgroup.subtree_control. For user
slice, systemd only delegates cpu by default; memory/io need explicit enable via systemd property Delegate=cpu cpuset io

--- PAGE 60 ---
memory pids and +memory echo into subtree_control.
**Q26 Answer: B) kexec of unsigned kernel, /dev/mem access, BPF that reads kernel memory, and unsigned module loads
are blocked because lockdown prevents bypassing Secure Boot-verified integrity boundary by user still with root**
Explanation: Kernel lockdown in confidentiality/integrity mode, auto-enabled when Secure Boot is on since kernel 5.4+,
restricts even root from violating trust model. It blocks kexec unsigned kernels, debugfs, /dev/mem/kcore, and BPF helpers
that leak kernel addresses.
**Q27 Answer: B) Enable RPS/RFS/XPS tuning, adjust netdev_budget and netdev_max_backlog, use XDP or AF_XDP, pin
application to same NUMA node as NIC/IRQs, check ksoftirqd saturation and use busy_poll or thread-ized NAPI**
Explanation: Column 2 in /proc/net/softnet_stat is time_squeezed, meaning ksoftirqd couldn't process all budget in time, due
to CPU saturation on IRQ core. Proper fix is distribute processing via RPS, align affinity, increase net.core.netdev_budget, or
use XDP for kernel-bypass, all critical for 100G tuning in 2024+ kernels.
**Q28 Answer: B) Space not freed until FD closed; inode stays allocated with link count 0. Recover via /proc/<pid>/fd/<fd>
(readlink or cp), or lsof +L1 shows, and /proc path provides access to inode data**
Explanation: Unlink removes directory entry but inode remains while open FD holds refcount. df still shows space used.
/proc/<pid>/fd entries point to deleted inode ( (deleted) in ls -l), allowing copy recovery until process closes FD or exits.
**Q29 Answer: B) Overlay requires lookup in all lower layers for each file (metacopy + redirect) causing dentry chain walk and
possible metacopy check; use volatile mount for scratch, enable metacopy=on and index=on, or use fscache, or avoid
excessive stat by using st_dev check and caching, or use overlayfs-fast or bind mount for build dirs**
Explanation: OverlayFS whiteouts and metacopy mean stat must merge upper and lower. On deep layers (common in Docker
with many RUN), lookup cost O(n_layers). Modern optimizations: metacopy reduces copy_up of data, volatile disables sync,
and using cache=bind for builds improves drastically.
**Q30 Answer: B) Check Landlock, seccomp-bpf filters, NoNewPrivs, BPF LSM, and Integrity (IMA/EVM) or mount noexec, or
fs.protected_regular and capability checks (FCaps). Use bpftool prog, grep Seccomp /proc/<pid>/status, cat
/proc/<pid>/attr/current, check landlock via audit or strace of landlock rules, and dmesg for AppArmor implicit deny**
Explanation: Modern sandbox (Flatpak, Chrome, systemd ProtectSystem, containerd) uses Landlock unprivileged LSM and
seccomp-bpf that can deny execve without audit log. Seccomp shows in /proc/pid/status, Landlock in kernel 5.13+, and BPF
LSM allows custom policies invisible to traditional auditd. Need specialized tooling to diagnose.
