Course File: 10_Networking.md
Course: Networking
1. Beginner Level (10 Questions)
**Q1. In the OSI model, which layer is responsible for logical addressing (IP) and routing?**
A) Layer 2 - Data Link
B) Layer 3 - Network
C) Layer 4 - Transport
D) Layer 7 - Application
**Q2. What does a /24 subnet mask (255.255.255.0) indicate?**
A) 24 usable host addresses
B) 254 usable host addresses in the network
C) 24 networks in the subnet
D) The network supports IPv6
**Q3. Which protocol automatically assigns IP addresses to devices on a LAN?**
A) DNS
B) DHCP
C) ARP
D) NAT
**Q4. You run ping 8.8.8.8 and get replies, but ping google.com fails. What is the most likely issue?**
A) Default gateway misconfigured
B) DNS resolution failure
C) Physical cable disconnected
D) DHCP server down
**Q5. What is the primary purpose of NAT (Network Address Translation) in a home router?**
A) To encrypt Wi-Fi traffic
B) To allow multiple private IP devices to share a single public IP to access the internet
C) To block malware
D) To increase internet speed
**Q6. Which device operates at Layer 2 and forwards frames based on MAC addresses?**
A) Router
B) Hub
C) Managed Switch
D) Modem
**Q7. TCP vs UDP: Which statement is TRUE?**
A) UDP provides guaranteed delivery and ordering, TCP does not
B) TCP is connection-oriented with retransmissions; UDP is connectionless with low overhead
C) Both use three-way handshake
D) DNS always uses TCP only
**Q8. What is the function of ARP?**
A) To translate domain names to IP addresses
B) To map an IP address to a MAC address on a local network
C) To assign IP addresses dynamically

--- PAGE 62 ---
D) To route packets between VLANs
**Q9. Which Wi-Fi standard from the Wi-Fi 6/6E family operates in the 6 GHz band to reduce congestion?**
A) 802.11n
B) 802.11ac (Wi-Fi 5)
C) 802.11ax (Wi-Fi 6E)
D) 802.11b
**Q10. What does 1000BASE-T mean for an Ethernet cable?**
A) 1000 Mbps over fiber optic
B) 100 Mbps over twisted pair
C) 1000 Mbps (1 Gbps) over twisted pair copper
D) 1000 Mbps over wireless
Beginner Answer Key & Explanations
**Q1 Answer: B) Layer 3 - Network**
The Network layer handles logical addressing (IPv4/IPv6) and routing across networks. Routers operate here to forward
packets based on destination IP.
**Q2 Answer: B) 254 usable host addresses in the network**
A /24 leaves 8 host bits (32-24), giving 256 total addresses minus network and broadcast = 254 usable hosts. This is the most
common subnet for small LANs.
**Q3 Answer: B) DHCP**
DHCP (Dynamic Host Configuration Protocol) automatically leases IP, subnet mask, gateway, and DNS. Without it, devices
need static configuration.
**Q4 Answer: B) DNS resolution failure**
Since ICMP to a public IP works, L1-L3 connectivity is fine. Failure by domain name points to DNS not resolving names to
IPs.
**Q5 Answer: B) To allow multiple private IP devices to share a single public IP to access the internet**
NAT translates private RFC1918 addresses (e.g., 192.168.1.x) to the router's public IP. This conserves IPv4 addresses and
provides basic isolation.
**Q6 Answer: C) Managed Switch**
Switches build MAC address tables and forward frames only to the destination port, unlike hubs which flood. Routers operate
at L3 with IP.
**Q7 Answer: B) TCP is connection-oriented with retransmissions; UDP is connectionless with low overhead**
TCP provides reliability, ordering, and flow control (used for HTTP, SMTP). UDP is fast with no guarantees, ideal for DNS,
VoIP, and gaming.
**Q8 Answer: B) To map an IP address to a MAC address on a local network**
ARP broadcasts "who has 10.0.0.1?" and caches the MAC reply. Without ARP, Layer 3 packets cannot be encapsulated into
Layer 2 frames.
**Q9 Answer: C) 802.11ax (Wi-Fi 6E)**
Wi-Fi 6E extends Wi-Fi 6 (802.11ax) into the 6 GHz band, adding up to 1200 MHz of spectrum and 59 non-overlapping
channels to avoid 2.4/5 GHz congestion.
**Q10 Answer: C) 1000 Mbps (1 Gbps) over twisted pair copper**
1000 = speed in Mbps, BASE = baseband, T = twisted-pair copper. It's the standard for Gigabit Ethernet on Cat5e/Cat6.
2. Intermediate Level (10 Questions)
**Q11. A network engineer configures two VLANs (VLAN10 and VLAN20) on a single L2 switch, but devices in different
VLANs cannot communicate. What is required for inter-VLAN communication?**
A) Enable STP

--- PAGE 63 ---
B) A Layer 3 device (router or L3 switch) with SVIs or router-on-a-stick
C) Increase MTU to 9000
D) Configure LACP
**Q12. In OSPF, what is the purpose of Area 0 (backbone area)?**
A) To provide NAT translation
B) All other areas must connect to Area 0 to prevent routing loops and ensure inter-area reachability
C) To filter BGP routes
D) To elect the root bridge
**Q13. You see high retransmissions and poor 2.4 GHz Wi-Fi throughput in a dense apartment building. Analyzer shows
overlapping channels. Best fix?**
A) Set all APs to channel 6 at max power
B) Use 20 MHz channels 1, 6, 11 with proper power tuning and enable WPA3, plus migrate capable clients to 5 GHz / 6 GHz
C) Disable encryption
D) Use 40 MHz width on 2.4 GHz
**Q14. TCP 3-way handshake is SYN -> SYN-ACK -> ACK. What security attack exploits this by sending many SYNs with
spoofed IPs and never completing?**
A) ARP poisoning
B) SYN flood (DoS)
C) DNS cache poisoning
D) DHCP starvation
**Q15. An enterprise uses 10.0.0.0/8 internally. When a user accesses the internet, what happens to the source IP in the
packet at the edge firewall?**
A) It remains 10.x.x.x on the internet
B) It is translated via NAT/PAT to a public IP
C) It is encrypted via IPsec
D) It is dropped by default
**Q16. You configure spanning-tree mode rapid-pvst. What does RSTP improve over original STP (802.1D)?**
A) Increases bandwidth
B) Reduces convergence from ~30-50s to <5s using proposal/agreement and alternate/backup port roles
C) Adds VLAN tagging
D) Enables 100 Gbps links
**Q17. What is the key difference between TCP BBR and CUBIC congestion control (relevant for 2024-2026 cloud
networks)?**
A) BBR is loss-based, CUBIC is delay-based
B) CUBIC uses packet loss as congestion signal; BBR models bottleneck bandwidth and RTT to avoid bufferbloat and
improves throughput on lossy/high-RTT paths
C) BBR only works for UDP
D) No difference
**Q18. A branch office connects to HQ via MPLS, but you want encrypted overlay with dynamic path selection over internet
broadband as backup. Which modern WAN technology achieves this?**
A) Frame Relay
B) SD-WAN with IPsec tunnels and application-aware routing
C) Hub with repeaters
D) ISDN
**Q19. IPv6 address 2001:db8::1 is being pinged. What protocol replaces ARP in IPv6 for neighbor discovery?**
A) ARP still
B) NDP using ICMPv6 (Neighbor Solicitation/Advertisement) multicast to ff02::1:ff00:0/104

--- PAGE 64 ---
C) DHCPv6 only
D) RARP
**Q20. You configure LACP between two switches with 2x 10G links. What is the result?**
A) Only one link active, other blocked by STP
B) Both links aggregated into a single logical EtherChannel with ~20 Gbps and redundancy / load balancing via hashing
C) Creates a routing loop
D) Requires BGP
Intermediate Answer Key & Explanations
**Q11 Answer: B) A Layer 3 device (router or L3 switch) with SVIs or router-on-a-stick**
VLANs isolate broadcast domains at L2. Inter-VLAN routing requires an L3 interface with an IP in each VLAN to route
between them.
**Q12 Answer: B) All other areas must connect to Area 0 to prevent routing loops and ensure inter-area reachability**
OSPF's hierarchical design mandates Area 0 as transit. Inter-area routes must go through Area 0, preventing loops via LSAs.
**Q13 Answer: B) Use 20 MHz channels 1, 6, 11 with proper power tuning and enable WPA3, plus migrate capable clients to
5 GHz / 6 GHz**
Channels 1, 6, 11 are the only non-overlapping 2.4 GHz channels. 20 MHz reduces interference, power tuning reduces CCI,
and 5/6 GHz offload is standard 2024+ best practice.
**Q14 Answer: B) SYN flood (DoS)**
The attacker exhausts the target's TCP backlog with half-open connections. Mitigations include SYN cookies, rate limiting,
and modern firewall defenses.
**Q15 Answer: B) It is translated via NAT/PAT to a public IP**
RFC1918 private addresses are not routable on the public internet. Edge NAT/PAT translates source IP/port to a public
routable IP.
**Q16 Answer: B) Reduces convergence from ~30-50s to <5s using proposal/agreement and alternate/backup port roles**
RSTP (802.1w) defines P/A handshake, faster topology change propagation, and port roles that pre-alternate loop-free paths.
**Q17 Answer: B) CUBIC uses packet loss as congestion signal; BBR models bottleneck bandwidth and RTT to avoid
bufferbloat and improves throughput on lossy/high-RTT paths**
CUBIC fills buffers until loss. BBRv2/v3 (deployed widely by Google/CLOUDFLARE in 2024-2026) measures BtlBw and
RTprop, yielding lower latency and better performance on modern WANs.
**Q18 Answer: B) SD-WAN with IPsec tunnels and application-aware routing**
SD-WAN builds encrypted overlays (IPsec/DTLS) over any transport (MPLS, broadband, LTE), measures SLA, and
dynamically steers traffic per application policy.
**Q19 Answer: B) NDP using ICMPv6 (Neighbor Solicitation/Advertisement) multicast to ff02::1:ff00:0/104**
IPv6 has no broadcast ARP. NDP uses ICMPv6 types 135/136 to solicited-node multicast, plus DAD and router discovery.
**Q20 Answer: B) Both links aggregated into a single logical EtherChannel with ~20 Gbps and redundancy / load balancing
via hashing**
LACP (802.1AX) negotiates bundling. Traffic is load-shared via src-dst IP/MAC hash, and failure of one link causes
sub-second failover, not STP blocking.

**Q29. You enable ECN (Explicit Congestion Notification) and DCTCP in a data center with RoCEv2. Why is this critical for
2024-2026 AI/ML fabrics with 400GbE?**
A) To increase packet loss
B) ECN marks packets with CE when queue builds (before drop) allowing sender to reduce rate earlier; DCTCP reacts
proportionally to marking fraction, preventing PFC storms and tail latency for RDMA over Converged Ethernet
C) To disable congestion control
D) Only for Wi-Fi
**Q30. Zero Trust Network Access (ZTNA 2.0) vs traditional VPN concentrator model in 2025. What architectural shift matters
most?**
A) ZTNA assigns full /16 to client; VPN does not
B) VPN provides broad network access (L3 tunnel); ZTNA provides identity-aware, per-application least-privilege access with
continuous trust verification, device posture, and no listening port exposure (brokered via cloud edge, e.g., SDP/ZTNA)
C) Both are identical
D) ZTNA uses only IPv6
Advanced Answer Key & Explanations
**Q21 Answer: B) Path1 because shorter AS_PATH is evaluated before MED**
BGP selection order: Weight (Cisco), LocalPref, locally originated, AS_PATH length (shortest wins), Origin, MED (compared
only if same neighboring AS), etc. AS_PATH is evaluated before MED.
**Q22 Answer: B) As EVPN Route Type 2 (MAC/IP advertisement) in MP-BGP EVPN address family, encapsulating L2 over
VXLAN with VNI**
EVPN Type 2 carries MAC + IP, VNI, and next-hop VTEP IP. Type 3 is inclusive multicast for BUM, Type 5 for IP prefix. This
decouples L2 learning from data-plane flooding.
**Q23 Answer: B) Throughput capped at ~6.5 Mbps per flow (64KB / RTT); enable TCP window scaling (RFC 1323) and
increase buffers, enable jumbo MTU, tune BBR**
Max throughput = window / RTT. 65535 / 0.08 = ~819KB/s ~6.5Mbps, far below 10 Gbps. Requires window scaling (up to
1GB), larger socket buffers, and modern CC.
**Q24 Answer: B) DoH encapsulates DNS in HTTPS on port 443, making firewall-based DNS filtering and visibility harder
without TLS inspection or endpoint policy; DoT uses port 853**
Traditional port-53 inspection fails for DoH. Enterprises must enforce managed DNS via endpoint config, DNS filtering
policies, or TLS interception/EDR to retain visibility.
**Q25 Answer: B) Clients are routed to the topologically nearest PoP per BGP best-path (lowest AS_PATH, MED, IGP cost),
providing low latency and DDoS absorption**
Anycast leverages BGP's decision process to route to nearest instance. If one PoP fails, its route is withdrawn and traffic
reconverges elsewhere.
**Q26 Answer: B) eBPF provides O(1) service load balancing via eBPF maps, reduced conntrack overhead, support for direct
server return (DSR), and network policy enforcement without linear iptables traversal**
iptables scales O(n) rules. Cilium eBPF replaces kube-proxy with maps, supports magneta-based load balancing, socket LB,
and eBPF host-routing that bypasses overlay overhead when possible.
**Q27 Answer: B) IDS performing full TLS inspection causing bufferbloat, TCP segmentation offload mismatch and
asymmetric routing; remediate by bypassing trusted flows, enabling TSO/GRO consistency, fixing MTU/MSS clamping for
IPsec/VXLAN (e.g., MSS 1380), and ensuring symmetric path**
Inline IDS/NGFW can cause latency spikes, MSS/MTU blackhole after encapsulation, and stateful drop on asymmetry. Fix
with proper MSS clamping, path MTU discovery, symmetric routing, and hardware offload alignment.
**Q28 Answer: B) Inner label = VPN label (identifies VRF/BGP next-hop) allocated by egress PE via MP-BGP; Outer label =
transport/LDP/RSVP-TE/SR label to reach egress PE**
Egress PE advertises VPN label via MP-BGP. Ingress PE pushes VPN + transport labels; P routers switch on outer only
(penultimate hop pop may pop outer).
**Q29 Answer: B) ECN marks packets with CE when queue builds (before drop) allowing sender to reduce rate earlier;
DCTCP reacts proportionally to marking fraction, preventing PFC storms and tail latency for RDMA over Converged

--- PAGE 67 ---
Ethernet**
At 400G, packet loss/PFC pause causes head-of-line blocking. ECN+DCTCP provides early feedback, maintaining low
queues and high throughput crucial for distributed training/collectives.
**Q30 Answer: B) VPN provides broad network access (L3 tunnel); ZTNA provides identity-aware, per-application
least-privilege access with continuous trust verification, device posture, and no listening port exposure (brokered via cloud
edge, e.g., SDP/ZTNA)**
ZTNA 2.0 implements never-trust, always-verify, micro-segmented app access vs network-wide VPN. It removes inbound
exposure and continuously validates user/device/context per session.

Course File: 11_Bash.md
Course: Bash & Shell Scripting
1. Beginner Level (10 Questions)
**Q1.** What does the shebang #!/usr/bin/env bash do when you execute ./script.sh?
- A) It is a comment ignored by the shell
- B) It tells the kernel to locate bash via PATH and use it as the interpreter
- C) It sets the SHELL environment variable to bash
- D) It enables bash strict mode automatically
**Q2.** What is the difference between $* and $@ inside double quotes?
- A) Both are identical in all contexts
- B) "$*" expands to a single word with positional params joined by first char of IFS, "$@" expands to separate words for each
positional param
- C) "$@" joins with spaces, "$*" splits into separate args
- D) $* includes $0, $@ does not
**Q3.** Given file="my document.txt", which correctly handles spaces?
- A) cat $file
- B) cat "$file"
- C) cat ${file}
- D) cat <$file>
**Q4.** What does chmod +x script.sh do?
- A) Adds execute permission for owner, group, others based on umask
- B) Sets SUID bit on the script
- C) Makes the file executable only for root
- D) Converts DOS line endings to Unix
**Q5.** Which command will show the exit status of the last executed command?
- A) echo $?
- B) echo $!
- C) echo $$
- D) echo $#
**Q6.** What does 2>&1 do in ls /nonexistent >out.log 2>&1?
- A) Redirects stdout to stderr
- B) Redirects stderr (fd 2) to the same destination as stdout (fd 1)
- C) Duplicates stdout to two files
- D) Suppresses both stdout and stderr
**Q7.** How do you define a local variable inside a bash function that doesn't leak to global scope?
- A) var=value
- B) local var=value or declare var=value inside function
- C) export var=value
- D) let var=value
**Q8.** What does set -e do?
- A) Enables echoing of commands before execution
- B) Causes the shell to exit immediately if a simple command fails (non-zero status), with exceptions for conditional contexts

--- PAGE 69 ---
- C) Disables globbing
- D) Exports all variables automatically
**Q9.** Which is the correct way to test if file data.csv exists and is a regular file?
- A) [ -d data.csv ]
- B) [ -f data.csv ]
- C) [ -x data.csv ]
- D) [ -e data.csv ] is enough to prove it's regular file
**Q10.** What does #!/bin/bash script do with echo $(( 5 + 3 * 2 ))?
- A) Prints 16
- B) Prints 11
- C) Prints 5 + 3 * 2
- D) Syntax error
Beginner Answer Key & Explanations
**Q1. Answer: B** – The kernel reads the shebang line on execve. Using /usr/bin/env searches PATH for bash, making it
portable across systems where bash isn't in /bin.
**Q2. Answer: B** – This is a classic distinction. "$@" preserves argument boundaries, critical for scripts handling filenames
with spaces, while "$*" concatenates into one string.
**Q3. Answer: B** – Double quoting prevents word splitting and globbing. Without quotes, my document.txt becomes two
arguments to cat.
**Q4. Answer: A** – +x adds execute bits. Final permissions depend on umask and existing modes, and apply to
user/group/other by default.
**Q5. Answer: A** – $? is last exit code, $! is PID of last background job, $$ is current shell PID, $# is number of positional
args.
**Q6. Answer: B** – Order matters: >out.log first sets fd1 to file, then 2>&1 makes fd2 duplicate fd1's current target, so both
go to out.log.
**Q7. Answer: B** – local is bash keyword to scope variables to function. Without it, variables in functions are global by
default in bash.
**Q8. Answer: B** – set -e / set -o errexit exits on failure. It doesn't trigger inside if, while, until, &&/|| lists, or when the failing
command is part of a pipeline except last.
**Q9. Answer: B** – -f tests regular file existence. -d is directory, -x executable, -e exists but could be any type (dir, symlink,
fifo).
**Q10. Answer: B** – $(( )) does arithmetic expansion with standard precedence. Multiplication precedes addition, so
3*2=6+5=11.
2. Intermediate Level (10 Questions)
**Q11.** What is the output of: x=5; echo ${x:-10}; echo ${x:=10}; echo $x ?
- A) 5, 5, 5
- B) 5, 10, 10
- C) 5, 5, 10
- D) 10, 10, 5
**Q12.** Consider set -o pipefail. What does false | true; echo $? output?
- A) 0 (without pipefail) and 0 (with pipefail)
- B) 0 without pipefail, 1 with pipefail
- C) 1 without pipefail, 1 with pipefail
- D) 1 without pipefail, 0 with pipefail
**Q13.** What does [[ $str =~ ^[0-9]{3}-[0-9]{4}$ ]] do differently from [ "$str" = pattern ]?
- A) They are identical

--- PAGE 70 ---
- B) [[ =~ ]] does POSIX ERE regex matching and populates BASH_REMATCH; [ = ] does string equality/glob, not regex
- C) [ ] does regex, [[ ]] does glob
- D) =~ only works with #!/bin/sh
**Q14.** What is the purpose of IFS= read -r line when reading a file line by line?
- A) Disables backslash escape interpretation (-r) and prevents leading/trailing whitespace trimming by setting IFS empty
- B) Makes read faster
- C) Splits line by colon
- D) Reads binary files
**Q15.** In bash, what does trap 'rm -f /tmp/tmp.$$' EXIT achieve?
- A) Deletes temp file when shell receives SIGKILL
- B) Registers a handler to remove temp file on shell exit (normal, or due to exit command/error with set -e, excluding SIGKILL
which cannot be trapped)
- C) Deletes file immediately
- D) Creates file on exit
**Q16.** What is the difference between source script.sh and ./script.sh?
- A) No difference
- B) source runs script in current shell process (variables/functions persist), ./script.sh spawns a subshell/new process
- C) ./script.sh sources, source executes in subshell
- D) source only works for python scripts
**Q17.** What will echo "${array[@]}" vs "${array[*]}" output when inside quotes for array=(a "b c" d)?
- A) Both identical
- B) "${array[@]}" -> 3 words: a, b c, d; "${array[*]}" -> 1 word: a b c d joined by first IFS char
- C) "${array[@]}" -> 1 word, "${array[*]}" -> 3 words
- D) Both produce 4 words
**Q18.** What does exec 3> output.log; echo "hi" >&3 do?
- A) Opens fd 3 for writing to output.log, then redirects echo to fd 3
- B) Syntax error
- C) Opens fd 3 for reading
- D) Redirects stderr to output.log
**Q19.** Analyze: find /var/log -type f -name "*.log" -mtime +7 -print0 | xargs -0 rm -f. Why use -print0 and -0?
- A) For faster execution
- B) To safely handle filenames containing spaces, newlines, or special characters by using NUL delimiter instead of
newline/space
- C) Required for rm -f
- D) It compresses the list
**Q20.** What does set -u (or set -o nounset) cause?
- A) Treats unset variables as error and exits/non-zero (except for special cases like $*, $@)
- B) Unsets all exported variables
- C) Makes all variables uppercase
- D) Enables debugging
Intermediate Answer Key & Explanations
**Q11. Answer: A** – ${x:-10} uses default without assignment, so x stays 5. ${x:=10} would assign only if x unset/null, but
here x=5, so still 5. Hence 5,5,5.
**Q12. Answer: B** – Normally pipeline exit is last command (true=0). With pipefail, pipeline returns last non-zero, or 0 if all
succeed, so it returns 1 from false.

--- PAGE 71 ---
**Q13. Answer: B** – [[ =~ ]] is bash's regex operator using ERE. It sets BASH_REMATCH array for capture groups. [ is
POSIX test, no regex support.
**Q14. Answer: A** – -r stops backslash escapes being removed. IFS= prevents word-splitting trimming. This is the robust
idiom for while IFS= read -r line; do ... loops.
**Q15. Answer: B** – trap on EXIT ensures cleanup runs on shell termination regardless of success/failure. SIGKILL (9)
cannot be trapped or ignored.
**Q16. Answer: B** – Sourcing executes in current shell environment, crucial for loading env vars, functions. Direct execution
forks subprocess, isolating side effects.
**Q17. Answer: B** – This mirrors the $@ vs $* rule but for arrays. [@] preserves elements, [*] joins. Quoting is essential to
preserve spaces in "b c".
**Q18. Answer: A** – exec without a command manipulates file descriptors of current shell. 3> file opens fd 3 to file, >&3
redirects stdout to fd 3.
**Q19. Answer: B** – NUL is the only byte not allowed in Linux filenames. Using NUL delimiter avoids breakage on
spaces/newlines, a production-critical pattern for safe batching.
**Q20. Answer: A** – set -u helps catch typos: referencing unset variable errors out. You must handle optional vars with
${var-} or ${var:-default} idioms.
3. Advanced Level (10 Questions)
**Q21.** What is the output and caveat of: count=0; cat file.txt | while read -r line; do ((count++)); done; echo $count ?
- A) Prints correct line count
- B) Prints 0 because while after pipe runs in subshell (in bash without lastpipe), so count modification is lost
- C) Syntax error
- D) Prints 1
**Q22.** In bash 5.1+, what does declare -n ref=$1; ref="new" do inside a function?

enabling pass-by-reference

**Q23.** Consider set -E; trap 'echo "ERR at $LINENO"' ERR; myfunc() { false; }; myfunc. When does ERR trap inherit?

- B) With set -E (errtrace), ERR trap is inherited into functions, subshells, command substitutions, allowing global error

- C) Only with set -T

- B) First tests if specific associative array element with key 'key' is set (even if empty), second tests something else; -v

- C) -v doesn't work for arrays
- D) They test values, not setness
**Q25.** Analyze: exec {fd}> >(logger -t myscript); echo "log msg" >&$fd. What is happening?

- B) Creates a file named logger
- C) Syntax error in bash < 5

**Q28.** What is the difference between process substitution <(cmd) and command substitution $(cmd) regarding binary data
and large output?
- A) No difference
- B) <(cmd) creates a named pipe or /dev/fd file providing streaming output to another command without waiting for full output
or subshell variable capture; $(cmd) captures all stdout into memory as string, stripping trailing newlines and unsuitable for

- C) $(cmd) streams, <() captures
- D) Both strip binary
**Q29.** Given: getopts ":f:v" opt; case $opt in ... What does leading : in optstring do and how to detect invalid option?
- A) Leading colon enables silent error mode: invalid options set opt to ? normally but with :, sets to ? and OPTARG contains
invalid char? Actually leading : causes getopts to set opt to ? for invalid? No: with :, invalid option sets opt to ?? Correction:
with leading :, invalid option sets opt to ?? Let's recall: Without :, error printed. With leading :, errors suppressed: invalid
option sets opt to ?? Actually with :, invalid sets opt to ? and OPTARG to option char? Wait: POSIX: with leading :, invalid
option sets opt to ?? Actually: invalid option -> ? without :, ? with OPTARG? With : -> ? becomes ?? Let's be precise: with :
optstring, invalid option sets opt to ? and OPTARG holds option char, missing arg sets opt to :.
- B) Leading : suppresses getopts default error messages; invalid option sets opt to ? with OPTARG being invalid char,
missing argument sets opt to : with OPTARG being option requiring arg, allowing custom handling
- C) Leading : makes all options require args
- D) It makes getopts case-insensitive
**Q30.** Write: How would you implement a bash function retry that retries a command up to 3 times with exponential backoff
(1s,2s,4s) and returns last exit code if all fail?
- A) retry() { for i in 1 2 3; do $@ && return 0; sleep $i; done; return 1; }
- B) retry() { local tries=0 max=3 delay=1; local ec=0; while ((tries < max)); do "$@" && return 0; ec=$?; ((tries++)); ((tries <
max)) && sleep $delay && delay=$((delay*2)); done; return $ec; } with proper "$@" handling and exponential backoff
- C) retry() { until $@; do sleep 1; done } infinite loop
- D) retry() { eval $@ ; }
Advanced Answer Key & Explanations
**Q21. Answer: B** – In bash, each pipe segment runs in subshell. Modifications to count inside while are in child process. Fix
via shopt -s lastpipe (only in non-interactive without job control) or process substitution: while ... done < <(cat file).
**Q22. Answer: B** – declare -n is nameref introduced bash 4.3. It allows true pass-by-reference without eval. Must be careful
about circular references. Bash 5.0+ improves resolution.
**Q23. Answer: B** – set -E enables errtrace, causing ERR traps to be inherited. This is critical for library error handling,
otherwise function failures wouldn't trigger global ERR trap. set -T is similar for DEBUG/RETURN traps.
**Q24. Answer: B** – [[ -v var ]] (bash 4.2+) tests if variable/array element is set, even empty string. This avoids unbound
variable errors under set -u unlike [ -z ${var+x} ] tricks, and supports associative array key checks safely.
**Q25. Answer: A** – This advanced pattern combines automatic fd allocation via {fd}> (bash 4.1+) which assigns free fd >10
to var fd, and process substitution feeding to logger. It enables logging without blocking and allows later exec {fd}>&- to close.
**Q26. Answer: A** – extglob enables !(pattern), @(pattern), *(pattern), +(pattern), ?(pattern). !(*.sh|*.md) negates match: all
files NOT matching .sh or .md. Essential for safe cleanup scripts.

--- PAGE 73 ---
**Q27. Answer: B** – flock uses kernel advisory locks. Using fd-based locking (flock -n 200) inside subshell is modern robust
pattern for critical sections. Append alone is atomic for small writes < PIPE_BUF but not for complex operations.
**Q28. Answer: B** – Process substitution is streaming (FIFO /dev/fd/63) suitable for large data, avoids command
substitution's memory capture and NUL truncation issues. Use <( ) when feeding command expecting file argument, e.g., diff
<(sort f1) <(sort f2).
**Q29. Answer: B** – The silent mode is best practice for CLI tools. Without it, getopts prints to stderr "illegal option". With :,
you control error messages and can distinguish missing arg (:) vs invalid option (?) via case handling.
**Q30. Answer: B** – Option A doesn't double delay (uses linear) and doesn't preserve "$@" quoting correctly for some
cases? Actually $@ vs "$@" matters. Option B correctly uses "$@" to preserve args, tracks exit code, implements
exponential backoff delay*=2, and returns last error. Option C is infinite retry without backoff logic.

- A) Aliases are persistent across sessions automatically
- B) gal is an alias for Get-Alias and gci for Get-ChildItem

--- PAGE 75 ---
- D) ls is not a valid alias in PowerShell
**Q9:** How do you stop a running service named Spooler using PowerShell?
- A) Stop-Service -Name Spooler
- B) Kill-Service Spooler
- C) Set-Service -Name Spooler -Status Stopped
- D) Get-Service Spooler | Stop
**Q10:** What is the purpose of $null on the left side like $null -eq $myVar?
- A) It is a syntax error
- B) It prevents accidental assignment and handles cases where $myVar is an array, avoiding unwanted enumeration
- C) It makes the comparison case-sensitive
- D) It improves performance by 10x
Beginner Answer Key & Explanations
**Q1 Answer: B) Get-Help Get-Process -Examples**
The -Examples parameter shows only practical examples. -Full shows detailed info including parameters and examples, but
-Examples is the direct way to request examples.
**Q2 Answer: C) Restricted**
On Windows client, PowerShell's default execution policy is Restricted, blocking script execution. On Windows Server it's
RemoteSigned by default, and on non-Windows platforms it's Unrestricted.
**Q3 Answer: B) The current object being processed**
$_ and its alias $PSItem represent the current pipeline object inside script blocks for ForEach-Object and Where-Object. This
is fundamental to pipeline processing in PowerShell.
**Q4 Answer: B) -like**
While -match uses regex, -like uses wildcard patterns and is case-insensitive by default. For substring check with wildcards
you use "hello world" -like "*world*", whereas -contains checks collection membership.
**Q5 Answer: C) $myVar = 10 (inside function, default is local)**
Variables assigned inside a function default to local scope automatically. While $local: is explicit, simply assigning $myVar
inside function creates a local copy without affecting parent scope.
**Q6 Answer: B) Export-Csv -Path data.csv (NoTypeInformation is now default in PS 7.4+)**
Starting with PowerShell 6.0, -NoTypeInformation became default, so the #TYPE header is no longer emitted by default. In
7.4+ this behavior remains, making explicit -NoTypeInformation redundant but still accepted for compatibility.
**Q7 Answer: B) Execution policies for all scopes: MachinePolicy, UserPolicy, Process, CurrentUser, LocalMachine**
Get-ExecutionPolicy -List enumerates all policy scopes in precedence order. This helps diagnose which Group Policy or local
setting is actually determining effective execution policy.
**Q8 Answer: B) gal is an alias for Get-Alias and gci for Get-ChildItem**
PowerShell includes many familiar aliases for discoverability: gal -> Get-Alias, gci -> Get-ChildItem, ls -> Get-ChildItem, dir ->
Get-ChildItem. They are not persistent unless exported via profile.
**Q9 Answer: A) Stop-Service -Name Spooler**
Stop-Service is the correct cmdlet to stop a service. Option D is invalid syntax; the pipeline form would be Get-Service
Spooler | Stop-Service. Stop verb is approved and discoverable via Get-Verb.
**Q10 Answer: B) It prevents accidental assignment and handles cases where $myVar is an array, avoiding unwanted
enumeration**
Putting $null on left avoids $myVar = $null typo bugs and ensures correct boolean logic when $myVar is an array. If array is
on left side, comparison enumerates and may return an array of true/false instead of single boolean.

**Q13:** Consider this advanced function snippet:

**Q18:** What is the correct way to handle terminating and non-terminating errors to stop execution?

- B) Get-Item NoFile -ErrorAction Continue; catch { }

**Q20:** How do you create a custom PowerShell object with properties Name and Age in PowerShell 7?

- B) [PSCustomObject]@{Name='John';Age=30}

Intermediate Answer Key & Explanations
**Q11 Answer: A) a=3, b=4**
Arrays with += create a new array instance; $b is reassigned to new array, $a still references original 3-element array. For
Arrays, assignment copies reference but += operator clones plus adds, breaking the reference link unlike List<T> .Add()
behavior.
**Q12 Answer: B) -eq is case-insensitive by default, -ceq is case-sensitive**
PowerShell comparison operators are case-insensitive by default ( -eq, -like, -match ). Prefixing with 'c' enforces
case-sensitivity (-ceq, -clike), and 'i' explicitly enforces case-insensitivity (-ieq), which is default behavior.
**Q13 Answer: B) 1 time (array passed as single parameter, process runs once, but $_ behavior differs)**
When array is passed as parameter argument (not via pipeline), process block runs once with $Name bound to entire array,
and PowerShell coerces array to string as "a b" if used as string. Pipeline input @("a","b") | Get-Data would invoke process
twice, highlighting parameter binding vs pipeline binding difference.
**Q14 Answer: B) Get-ChildItem @params**
Splatting uses @ instead of $ to expand hashtable or array into parameters: $params = @{Path='C:\'; Recurse=$true};
Get-ChildItem @params. Using $ would pass the hashtable as single positional argument, causing binding error.
**Q15 Answer: B) SSH remoting uses New-PSSession -HostName and -SSHTransport, works cross-platform without WinRM,
ideal for Linux/macOS**
PowerShell 7 introduced SSH transport for remoting via -HostName, -UserName, -SSHTransport without WinRM
dependencies. This enables cross-platform remoting to Linux/macOS where OpenSSH is configured, using Enter-PSSession
-HostName.
**Q16 Answer: A) 12 (3+4+5)**
Where-Object filters to 3,4,5, Measure-Object calculates Sum=12, and Select-Object -ExpandProperty returns raw value.
This demonstrates pipeline composition: filtering -> measuring -> expanding property to get scalar.
**Q17 Answer: B) Export-Clixml with PSCredential object encrypted via DPAPI (current user only) or use SecretManagement
module vault**
Best practice for 2024-2026 is Microsoft.PowerShell.SecretManagement module with SecretStore or Azure KeyVault, or
DPAPI-encrypted CliXml only for current user/machine. Hardcoding passwords violates credential hygiene and security
baselines like ASD, CIS.
**Q18 Answer: A) Get-Item NoFile -ErrorAction Stop wrapped in try/catch**
Non-terminating errors don't trigger catch by default; -ErrorAction Stop converts them to terminating errors capturable by
try/catch. Setting $ErrorActionPreference to Stop globally achieves same but less targeted; try/catch only catches terminating
errors.
**Q19 Answer: B) The directory where the .psm1 module file resides**

--- PAGE 78 ---
$PSScriptRoot is automatic variable containing the directory of the currently executing script or module file. This is crucial for
relative path resolution inside modules, unlike Get-Location which returns working directory that may differ.
**Q20 Answer: C) Both A and B work, but B preserves property order and is faster**
[PSCustomObject] type accelerator with hashtable literal is idiomatic PS3+ syntax, preserves insertion order and is highly
optimized. New-Object works but doesn't guarantee property order on older PS and is slower due to cmdlet binding overhead.

- B) ForEach-Object -Parallel -ThrottleLimit uses runspaces with isolated runspace per iteration, far more memory-efficient

**Q29:** How do you properly dispose and handle async .NET Tasks in PowerShell 7.4+ to avoid deadlocks?
- A) $task.Result synchronously always safe
- B) Use Await keyword like C#: await $task
- C) Use $task.GetAwaiter().GetResult() may still deadlock in STA; preferred pattern is $task | Wait-Task -? actually: use
while (-not $task.AsyncWaitHandle.WaitOne(200)) {} or leverage ForEach-Object -Parallel or Task.Wait() with timeout, or use
Start-ThreadJob with Receive-Job -Wait -AutoRemoveJob; PowerShell 7.4 adds Task support via .GetAwaiter().GetResult()
but safe pattern is [System.Threading.Tasks.Task]::WaitAll()
- D) Never use .NET tasks in PowerShell
**Q30:** Examine this advanced parameter set definition:
powershell
function Test-Param {
[CmdletBinding(DefaultParameterSetName='ByName')]
param(
[Parameter(Mandatory, ParameterSetName='ByName', Position=0)]
[string]$Name,
[Parameter(Mandatory, ParameterSetName='ById', Position=0)]
[int]$Id,
[Parameter(ParameterSetName='ByName')]
[Parameter(ParameterSetName='ById')]
[switch]$Force
Which call is INVALID?
- A) Test-Param -Name "Foo" -Force
- B) Test-Param -Id 5
- C) Test-Param -Name "Foo" -Id 5
- D) Test-Param "Foo"
Advanced Answer Key & Explanations
**Q21 Answer: B) $PSScriptRoot not accessible at property initialization time; initialize in constructor: MyClass(){ $this.path =
$PSScriptRoot } or use static initialization workaround**
PowerShell classes execute property initializers in a context where automatic variables like $PSScriptRoot are not available.
The fix is assignment inside constructor where runspace context is fully initialized.
**Q22 Answer: A) Begin(1x) -> Process(3x) -> End(1x)**

--- PAGE 80 ---
Begin executes once at start, Process executes once per pipeline object (3 times), End executes once at completion. This is
fundamental to writing pipeline-aware advanced functions that accumulate state in Begin/End and handle per-item logic in
Process.
**Q23 Answer: B) ForEach-Object -Parallel -ThrottleLimit uses runspaces with isolated runspace per iteration, far more
memory-efficient than Start-Job; ThreadJob module is lighter than PSJob**
Start-Job spawns new process (heavy), ThreadJob uses threads (lighter), and ForEach-Object -Parallel (PS7+) uses
runspace pool with ThrottleLimit default 5, offering best balance. Runspaces can share via $using: scope modifier to capture
outside variables.
**Q24 Answer: B) Use class-based DSC resource with [DscResource()] attribute and Tests/Set/Get methods, or prefer DSC
v3 / PSDSCResources / Guest Configuration; MOF compiled via Configuration keyword still supported in Windows
PowerShell 5.1 but not actively developed for PS7.2+**
Microsoft moved DSC focus to DSC v3 (separate DSC executable, YAML/JSON) and class-based resources. Legacy
Configuration compilation is Windows PowerShell 5.1 only; PS 7.1+ removed DSC engine but module PSDscResources
bridges functionality. Modern practice uses Guest Configuration or DSCv3.
**Q25 Answer: B) 0 and 1 (with GetNewClosure, captures current iteration value due to closure snapshot)**
Without GetNewClosure, all scriptblocks share same $i variable reference returning final value 3. GetNewClosure() creates
dynamic module capturing current lexical values, preserving iteration state, which is critical for deferred execution in loops.
**Q26 Answer: B) Select-String -Path log.txt -Pattern "ERROR" -SimpleMatch**
Select-String uses fast internal file streaming and avoids assigning each line to PowerShell object with extra properties.
Option C with [System.IO.File]::ReadLines and .Contains() is also very fast; Get-Content | Where-Object is slowest due to
heavy pipeline object wrapping and overhead of regex matching per line.
**Q27 Answer: B) Use streaming: implement function that uses process {} block and emits one-by-one, or use
IAsyncEnumerable pattern via Write-Output -NoEnumerate, and avoid += array building; use List<T> for accumulation**
Best practice is streaming architecture: produce/consume one object at a time to keep memory constant O(1). Array
concatenation with += is O(N^2) because it copies entire array each iteration; use [System.Collections.Generic.List[string]]
and .Add() for efficient accumulation.
**Q28 Answer: A) ?? is null-coalescing operator, ??= assigns if left is null; e.g. $x ?? "default" and $x ??= "default"**
Introduced in PowerShell 7.0, null-coalescing operators simplify null handling: $value = $maybeNull ?? "fallback". The
assignment variant ??= only assigns if left operand is $null, optimizing default initialization pattern without overwriting existing
values.
**Q29 Answer: C) Use $task.GetAwaiter().GetResult() may still deadlock in STA; preferred pattern is $task | Wait-Task -?
actually: use while (-not $task.AsyncWaitHandle.WaitOne(200)) {} or leverage ForEach-Object -Parallel or Task.Wait() with
timeout, or use Start-ThreadJob with Receive-Job -Wait -AutoRemoveJob; PowerShell 7.4 adds Task support via
.GetAwaiter().GetResult() but safe pattern is [System.Threading.Tasks.Task]::WaitAll()**
PowerShell doesn't have native await keyword like C#. Calling .Result can block thread. Safest approach is using
GetAwaiter().GetResult() which unwraps exceptions, but avoid in STA mode or pipelines; wrap in ThreadJob or use WaitAll
with timeout and handle AggregateException. PowerShell 7.4 improved Task handling but deadlock risk remains.
**Q30 Answer: C) Test-Param -Name "Foo" -Id 5**
Parameter sets are mutually exclusive; -Name belongs to ByName set, -Id to ById set, cannot be combined. Parameter
binder throws "Parameter set cannot be resolved" when arguments match multiple sets ambiguously. -Force belongs to both
sets, so it's allowed with either.

Course File: 13_VMware.md
# VMware & Virtualisation - Comprehensive Exam
**Total Questions: 30 | Beginner: 10, Intermediate: 10, Advanced: 10**
**Valid for: vSphere 8.0 U2/U3, VCF 5.1/5.2, vSAN 8.0 ESA/OSA, NSX, Broadcom Era 2024-2026**
**Passing Score: 70%**
Section 1: Beginner (Questions 1-10)
Q1. What is the primary role of the ESXi hypervisor in a VMware
environment?
A) To provide network routing between VMs
B) To install guest operating systems automatically
C) To abstract physical hardware and run virtual machines directly on it
D) To manage Active Directory authentication
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** ESXi is a Type-1 bare-metal hypervisor that installs directly on physical hardware. It abstracts CPU, memory,
storage, and networking to create and run multiple isolated VMs.
Q2. What does vCenter Server primarily provide?
A) Physical switch management
B) Centralized management for multiple ESXi hosts and VMs
C) Backup for VMs only
D) Guest OS patching
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** vCenter Server is the centralized management plane for vSphere, managing clusters, hosts, VMs, and
features like vMotion and DRS. Since vSphere 7.0+, it is only available as the vCenter Server Appliance (VCSA).
Q3. Which feature allows a running VM to be moved live from one ESXi
host to another with zero downtime?
A) Storage vMotion
B) vMotion
C) vSphere HA
D) Fault Tolerance
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** vMotion performs live migration of a running VM's memory and execution state between hosts. Storage
vMotion moves only the VM's disk files, while vMotion moves compute without downtime if requirements are met.
Q4. In the Broadcom era (2024-2026), how is VMware vSphere primarily
licensed?
A) Perpetual per-CPU with no support
B) Free open-source license
C) Subscription-based per-core bundles: VCF and VMware vSphere Foundation (VVF)
D) Per-VM monthly license only
**Answer: C**

--- PAGE 82 ---
**Explanation:** After Broadcom's acquisition, perpetual licenses were discontinued in favor of subscription per-core bundles.
The two main offerings are VMware Cloud Foundation (VCF) and VMware vSphere Foundation (VVF), with a 16-core
minimum per CPU (72-core minimum per order in 2025 updates).
Q5. What is a datastore in vSphere?
A) A database storing vCenter credentials
B) A logical container that holds VM files, ISOs, and templates on storage
C) A physical hard disk in the server
D) A network share for backups only
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** A datastore is a storage abstraction layer presented to ESXi hosts from VMFS, NFS, or vSAN. It stores VM
configuration, VMDKs, snapshots, ISOs, and templates as files.
Q6. What is the purpose of VMware Tools / open-vm-tools?
A) To provide antivirus in the VM
B) To improve VM performance, enable graceful shutdown, and provide drivers and ballooning
C) To convert VMs to physical servers
D) To license the guest OS
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** VMware Tools provides paravirtualized drivers (VMXNET3, PVSCSI), memory balloon driver, and host-guest
communication. It enables accurate time sync, graceful OS shutdown/reboot, and improved performance metrics.
Q7. What is the difference between vSAN OSA and vSAN ESA in vSphere
8?
A) OSA is faster always
B) ESA (Express Storage Architecture) is the new architecture optimized for NVMe/TLC with single-tier, log-structured file
system; OSA is Original Storage Architecture with cache+capacity tiers
C) ESA only works with HDDs
D) There is no difference
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** vSAN 8 introduced ESA as an optional architecture requiring all-NVMe and redesigned for high performance
and efficiency. OSA retains the traditional two-tier disk group model with caching tier and is broader in hardware compatibility.
Q8. What does DRS (Distributed Resource Scheduler) do?
A) Powers off all VMs at night
B) Automatically load-balances VMs across hosts in a cluster based on resource usage and rules
C) Backs up VMs daily
D) Creates snapshots automatically
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** DRS monitors CPU/memory pressure and uses vMotion to balance workloads. Since vSphere 7.0+, it uses a
workload-centric algorithm and integrates with vSphere Lifecycle Manager and DPM.
Q9. Which virtual switch type is standard in vSphere and managed
per-host?
A) vSphere Distributed Switch (VDS)
B) vSphere Standard Switch (VSS)

--- PAGE 83 ---
C) NSX Logical Switch
D) Cisco Nexus 1000V
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** The Standard Switch (VSS) is configured individually on each ESXi host. The Distributed Switch (VDS) is
centralized via vCenter, provides advanced features like NIOC, port mirroring, and is required for VCF.
Q10. What is a VM snapshot?
A) A full backup copy stored offsite
B) A point-in-time delta file preserving VM state and disk, allowing rollback
C) A clone that uses no extra space
D) A memory dump only
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** A snapshot creates delta VMDKs and optionally memory state, saving the VM's point-in-time. It is not a full
backup and can degrade performance if left long-term; VMware recommends <72 hours.
Section 2: Intermediate (Questions 11-20)
Q11. In vSphere 8.0 U3, what changed for vMotion to improve live
migration of VMs with large memory pages or AI/ML workloads?
A) vMotion was removed
B) Improved vMotion with faster page tracing and up to 6x faster for large VMs using Data Plane Development Kit (DPDK)
improvements and vMotion stun time reduction
C) vMotion now requires VM power-off
D) Only cold migration is supported
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** vSphere 8.0 U3 introduced significant vMotion performance improvements for monster VMs (up to 24Tbps
logical bandwidth). It reduces stun time via adaptive page tracing, critical for vGPU and large-memory SAP/HANA and AI
workloads.
Q12. You have a VCF 5.2 deployment. How much vSAN capacity
entitlement is included per VCF core license?
A) 0 TiB
B) 0.25 TiB per core (VVF level)
C) 1 TiB per core
D) Unlimited
**Answer: C**
**Explanation:** VCF 5.2 entitles 1 TiB of vSAN storage per core license (OSA or ESA or vSAN Max when used as principal
storage). VVF entitles 0.25 TiB per core; excess requires separate vSAN capacity add-on subscription.
Q13. What is vSphere Lifecycle Manager (vLCM) and its key advantage
over Update Manager?
A) It only updates VMware Tools
B) vLCM provides desired-state image-based lifecycle for hosts, clusters, firmware, drivers combined; supports vSAN ESA
and single image for entire cluster
C) It deletes VMs automatically
D) Same as Update Manager with different name

--- PAGE 84 ---
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** vLCM replaces legacy baselines with declarative cluster images (ESXi version, vendor add-on, firmware). In
vSphere 8, it can remediate entire clusters with parallel remediation and integrates with vSAN ESA for non-disruptive
updates.
Q14. An admin wants to enforce VM-to-Host affinity and anti-affinity for
licensing or performance. Which feature should be used?
A) VM Overrides inside DRS rules
B) Storage DRS only
C) HA admission control
D) vMotion without rules
**Answer: A**
**Explanation:** DRS VM-Host rules (Must/Should run on, Should Not) and VM-VM anti-affinity rules control placement. In
2024+, these are managed via the vSphere Client > Cluster > Configure > VM/Host rules and are respected during DRS
balancing and maintenance mode.
Q15. What is vSphere Trust Authority and VM Encryption in vSphere 8?
A) Encrypts only network traffic
B) A key provider architecture where remote attestation clusters attest trusted hosts; enables VM encryption, vMotion
encryption, and vSAN encryption without requiring TPM on all workload hosts
C) Disables Secure Boot
D) Only for vCenter Server
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** vTrust Authority separates key management from attestation using a dedicated trusted cluster. This enables
encrypted vMotion by default in vSphere 8, TPM 2.0 attestation, and KMS-based VM encryption for compliance.
Q16. You need to configure stretched cluster for vSAN ESA in VCF 5.2.
What is newly supported in vSAN 8.0 U3?
A) Stretched clusters are not supported on ESA
B) Stretched cluster support on ESA is now GA in VCF 5.2 with witness and up to 5ms RTT between sites
C) Only 2 nodes supported
D) Requires OSA only
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** vSAN 8.0 U3 added full stretched cluster support for ESA, previously OSA-only. In VCF 5.2, ESA stretched
clusters support site failure tolerance with witness appliance and flexible topologies, enabling active-active data centers.
Q17. What is the purpose of vSphere Distributed Services Engine and
DPUs (Data Processing Units) support added in vSphere 8?
A) To run games faster
B) Offload NSX, vSphere networking, and security services (NSX, vDefend) to DPU, freeing CPU cores and improving

C) To disable NSX
D) Only for storage
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** vSphere 8 U2+ supports DPUs (NVIDIA BlueField, AMD Pensando) via vSphere Distributed Services
Engine. Infrastructure services offload from x86 CPUs, allowing workload consolidation and zero-trust host isolation.

--- PAGE 85 ---
Q18. How does vSphere HA admission control work with the new vSAN
ESA clusters large scale?
A) It is disabled

**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** HA admission control in 8.0 supports Cluster Resource Percentage, Failover Hosts, and Slot Policy (legacy).

Q19. In NSX 4.2 integrated with VCF 5.2, what is the VCF default

**Answer: B**

VLAN-backed port groups; workload segments use GENEVE overlay with T0/T1 gateways for north-south/east-west and

Q20. How does Storage vMotion handle encrypted VMs with vSphere 8.0
U3?
A) It decrypts them automatically
B) Supports live migration with encryption preserved via KMS, with improved performance using fast file cloning APIs
(VAAI/NAS) when storage supports it
C) Cannot migrate encrypted VMs
D) Requires power off
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Encrypted VMs can be Storage vMotioned without decrypting if hosts share KMS trust. vSphere 8 U3

Section 3: Advanced (Questions 21-30)
Q21. You are designing a VCF 5.2.1 private cloud with vSAN ESA, 50 hosts,
2TB RAM per host, and GPU-enabled clusters for AI. What advanced
vSphere 8 features must be considered for AI/ML workloads?
A) Disable DRS and use only standard switches
B) vGPU with Dynamic DirectPath I/O, RDMA over Converged Ethernet v2 (RoCE v2) support, vMotion for vGPU-enabled

C) Only run AI on physical servers
D) AI workloads not supported in vSphere
**Answer: B**

--- PAGE 86 ---
**Explanation:** vSphere 8 U2/U3 added support for vMotion of vGPU VMs (stun time <2s), Device Groups for DPU/GPU
passthrough, and RDMA. For LLMs, use 2MB/1GB pages, VM latency sensitivity High, reservation, and NUMA alignment with
vSphere's virtual NUMA (vNUMA) auto-sizing.
Q22. Explain the difference between vSAN ESA RAID-5/6 erasure coding vs
OSA and impact on capacity and resync in 8.0 U3.
A) Same as OSA
B) ESA uses adaptive RAID-5/6 with dynamic stripe width, per-object optimized parity, and high-performance scalable
snapshots; resync is log-structured and up to 2x faster, with 4x more efficient capacity vs OSA RAID-1 default
C) ESA only supports RAID-1
D) ESA requires 10 nodes minimum for RAID-6
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** ESA implements new log-structured file system (LFS) with adaptive erasure coding that chooses optimal
stripe. It reduces write amplification, enables space-efficient snapshots (up to 100x snapshots per object) and faster resync
due to journal-based resync, improving usable TiB per VCF core entitlement.
Q23. In a VCF 5.2 SDDC Manager lifecycle upgrade from VCF 5.1.1 to 5.2.1,
what is the required upgrade sequence and pre-check?
A) Direct upgrade of ESXi only
B) Use VCF Upgrade Bundle: SDDC Manager -> NSX -> vCenter -> ESXi/vSAN (via vLCM image) after using VCF Async
Patch Tool, VCF Validate, and vSAN HCL pre-checks; Management WLD first, then VI WLDs
C) Reinstall everything manually
D) NSX first then ignore SDDC Manager
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** VCF has strict BOM (Bill of Materials) order enforced by SDDC Manager; skipping breaks interoperability.
Pre-checks include vSAN Skyline Health, NSX upgrade coordinator, VCF Upgrade Precheck, and offline depot for
air-gapped. Management domain must be upgraded before VI workload domains.
Q24. How does vSphere 8.0 U3 handle TPM 2.0, Secure Boot, and virtual
TPM (vTPM) for attestation with Broadcom hardware HSM?
A) vTPM not supported anymore
B) Attestation is integrated: ESXi requires Secure Boot + TPM 2.0 for trusted boot; vTPM 2.0 devices backed by native key
provider or KMS for VM's measured boot, with vCenter as native key provider for small deployments since 7.0 U2
C) TPM causes boot failure always
D) No encryption
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** vSphere Trustchain uses host TPM for measured boot into ESXi, vCenter verifies attestation. vTPM provides
virtual TPM inside VM for Windows 11, measured boot, BitLocker, without physical TPM per host. Native key provider
simplifies vTPM without external KMS but not for full KMIP compliance.
Q25. You have a 4-node vSAN ESA cluster that loses a full disk group
(NVMe failure). What is the failure handling and rebuild process difference
in ESA vs OSA?
A) Same as OSA, full disk group rebuild needed
B) ESA is disk-abstracted: no disk groups; failure domain is disk level + node level, using adaptive resync with individual drive
failure handling, partial rebuilds, and dynamic capacity rebalancing without traditional disk group concept
C) ESA cluster fails completely

--- PAGE 87 ---
D) Requires full cluster reformat
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** ESA removes disk group construct; all disks form single storage pool per host. A single NVMe failure triggers
object repair at extent level, not entire disk group evacuation. This lowers blast radius, reduces resync time, and keeps
storage pool online, improving MTTR and reducing vSAN capacity overhead.
Q26. In advanced troubleshooting, an ESXi 8.0 host shows PURPLE
DIAGONAL SCREEN (PSOD) due to RDMA driver. What log and tool should
you analyze post-reboot, and how to prevent?
A) Ignore PSOD
B) Analyze /var/log/vmkernel.log, vobd.log, vmksummary, and core dump via vsish, esxcli system coredump, check vSAN
ESA RDMA/DPDK compatibility via VCG/HCL; isolate driver with vLCM vendor add-on quarantine or disable RoCE if
unsupported; file Broadcom SR with vm-support bundle
C) Reboot again without logs
D) Only check Windows Event Viewer
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** PSOD data includes backtrace and hardware info; core dump preserved in vmk dump partition. RDMA/RoCE
requires specific firmware/driver combos certified on VCG for vSAN ESA; mismatched MLNX OFED drivers cause PF
exception. Use vmkdump -l and ESXi host client > Monitor > Hardware.
Q27. Design a VCF 5.2 multi-tenancy architecture using NSX Projects, VCF

per tenant, plus GPU Operator via vGPU profiles in namespace
C) Only one tenant supported
D) Use separate vCenters only
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** VCF 5.2 supports enhanced multi-tenancy: NSX 4.2 Projects provide enterprise-grade tenant isolation
without multiple NSX Managers. vSphere IaaS control plane enables self-service K8s namespaces and VM pools with
quotas. VCF Automation orchestrates tenancy with Aria Operations for capacity metering and chargeback, critical for
Broadcom portfolio private cloud.
Q28. You must automate day-2 operations for 1000 VMs across VCF with
Ansible and PowerCLI in 2025+. What is best practice for API
authentication and lifecycle after Broadcom entitlement changes?
A) Use root passwords in scripts
B) Use OAuth 2.0 / API tokens via vCenter SSO with vSphere Automation API, Aria Automation API tokens with rotating

C) Hardcode admin credentials

**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** Broadcom tightened security: vSphere 8 supports token-based auth, vAPI token exchange, and short-lived

Q29. During VCF 5.2.1 to VCF 9.0 readiness assessment (released late 2024

A) No change
B) VCF 9.0 converges VCF and VVF into single unified platform with VCF Operations (Aria) fleet management, integrated
vSphere Kubernetes Service (VKS), vSAN unified data path for ESA + vSAN Max + VMFS, and single SSO with NSX 9

C) Removes vSAN entirely
D) VCF 9.0 is only SaaS
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** VCF 9.0 (announced VMware Explore 2024, GA roadmap 2025) is Broadcom's vision for private cloud: one

Q30. A customer reports 40% performance degradation after enabling
vSAN ESA RAID-6 and enabling virtual machine encryption cluster-wide.
What is root cause analysis and remediation?
A) Encryption always degrades 40% - normal
B) Root cause likely CPU contention from KMIP encryption offload + RAID-6 parity compute overhead + lack of AES-NI
offload or DPU offload; Check: Enable AES-NI, vSAN data-at-rest encryption offload to DPU or CPU optimized, use RAID-5
where FTT=1 suffices, separate KMS latency (<5ms), and enable vSAN compression only if CPU headroom >30%; Use
esxtop - encryption world counters and vSAN performance service
C) Disable vCenter
D) Only reboot solves it
**Answer: B**
**Explanation:** ESA RAID-6 has 2x parity calc vs RAID-5; combining with VM encryption causes double encryption (vSAN +
VM). ESA does not support erasure-coded encryption offload to controller, relies on CPU AES-NI and VMkernel Crypto.
Remediate with data-at-rest only OR VM encryption per sensitivity, tune cluster to RAID-5 adaptive where applicable, ensure
KMS RTT low, and use DPUs for encryption offload.
