Course File: 24_Git.md
Course: Git & GitHub
1. Beginner Level (10 Questions)
**Q1. You just installed Git and want to set your identity for all repositories on this machine. Which commands should you
run?**
A. git config --global user.name "Alice" && git config --global user.email "alice@example.com"
B. git config user.name "Alice"
C. git set --global identity "Alice"
D. git --global config user "Alice"
**Q2. You have modified two files, but you only want to commit app.js and keep config.json unstaged. What should you do?**
A. git commit -a -m "update"
B. git add app.js && git commit -m "update app"
C. git commit app.js
D. git stage app.js && git push
**Q3. You want to see a concise one-line history of commits with graph and all branches. Which command is best?**
A. git log --oneline --graph --all
B. git history
C. git show branch
D. git log --full
**Q4. You accidentally committed with a typo in the message on your last local commit (not pushed). How do you fix it
quickly?**
A. git commit --amend -m "correct message"
B. git reset --hard HEAD~1 and commit again
C. git revert HEAD
D. git fix HEAD
**Q5. On GitHub, you forked a repo octo/original to your account. How do you keep your fork's main branch up to date with
upstream after cloning your fork?**
A. git pull origin main
B. git remote add upstream https://github.com/octo/original.git && git fetch upstream && git merge upstream/main
C. git clone upstream main
D. git sync fork
**Q6. You want to create a new branch feature/login and switch to it in one command on Git 2.36+. What are valid modern
commands?**
A. git checkout -b feature/login and git switch -c feature/login
B. git branch feature/login
C. git new feature/login
D. git create branch feature/login
**Q7. What does .gitignore do, and where should you place a file named secrets.env to never track it?**
A. It deletes files from remote; add secrets.env to .git
B. It prevents Git from tracking matching untracked files; add secrets.env or .env pattern to .gitignore in repo root
C. It encrypts files; add to .gitencrypt
D. It commits files faster; add to .gitinclude
**Q8. You cloned a repo, made a commit, and git push fails with ! [rejected] main -> main (fetch first). Why and what to do?**

--- PAGE 168 ---
A. GitHub is down; wait
B. Remote has commits you don't have locally; run git pull --rebase origin main (or fetch + merge/rebase) then push
C. You need to force push always
D. You forgot git add
**Q9. On GitHub, what is the difference between a Pull Request and an Issue?**
A. They are same
B. Issue tracks bug/task/discussion; Pull Request proposes code changes to be reviewed and merged into a branch
C. Pull Request is for private repos only
D. Issue automatically merges code
**Q10. How do you discard all local modifications to a tracked file index.html and restore it to last committed state?**
A. git restore index.html or git checkout -- index.html
B. git delete index.html
C. git ignore index.html
D. git remove index.html
Beginner Answer Key & Explanations
**Q1 Answer: A - git config --global user.name and user.email**
These commands set the commit author identity globally in ~/.gitconfig for all repos. From late 2023 GitHub also requires
email verification but commit identity is still locally configured.
**Q2 Answer: B - git add app.js && git commit -m "update app"**
Git only commits what is staged in the index. Staging selectively allows you to commit app.js while config.json remains
modified but unstaged.
**Q3 Answer: A - git log --oneline --graph --all**
This combination shows compact history with branch topology across all refs. It's the standard alias many teams use for
understanding branch structure quickly.
**Q4 Answer: A - git commit --amend -m "correct message"**
--amend replaces the last commit with a new one containing fixed message and/or staged changes. It should only be used if
the commit hasn't been pushed to a shared branch to avoid rewriting public history.
**Q5 Answer: B - add upstream remote, fetch, merge**
A fork has origin pointing to your copy. Adding an upstream remote pointing to original repo lets you fetch upstream/main and
integrate updates, or use GitHub's Sync fork button in UI since 2021.
**Q6 Answer: A - git checkout -b and git switch -c**
Both create and switch in one step. git switch -c is the newer, safer command introduced in Git 2.23 to split checkout's many
responsibilities.
**Q7 Answer: B - .gitignore prevents tracking untracked files**
.gitignore patterns tell Git to ignore unmatched files, so they won't show in git status or be added accidentally. It does not
affect already tracked files unless you untrack them with git rm --cached.
**Q8 Answer: B - Remote ahead, need to pull/rebase first**
The error means remote main diverged; pushing would overwrite. Fetching then rebasing keeps linear history, the default
strategy for protected main branches on GitHub in 2024-2026.
**Q9 Answer: B - Issue tracks task, PR proposes code changes**
Issues are for discussion/bugs, while Pull Requests link commits, trigger CI checks, code review, and merge workflows.
GitHub can auto-close issues with keywords like Fixes #123 in PR.
**Q10 Answer: A - git restore index.html**
git restore (since Git 2.23) restores working tree file from index/HEAD, discarding local edits. Older git checkout -- <file> does
same but restore is clearer and less error-prone.

--- PAGE 169 ---
2. Intermediate Level (10 Questions)
**Q11. You need to temporarily save your current changes without committing, then switch branches to hotfix, then return.
Which sequence works and preserves untracked files too?**
A. git stash push -m "wip" --include-untracked, git switch hotfix, ... git switch back, git stash pop
B. git commit -m temp && git checkout hotfix
C. git save stash
D. git branch --stash
**Q12. What is the difference between git merge and git rebase when integrating feature into main, and when should you
avoid rebase?**
A. Merge creates a merge commit preserving history; rebase rewrites feature commits on top of main for linear history. Avoid
rebase on public/shared branches already pushed to others.
B. They are identical
C. Rebase deletes commits
D. Merge rewrites history
**Q13. You want to undo the last commit that is already pushed to origin/main, without rewriting history for teammates. What
should you do?**
A. git reset --hard HEAD~1 && git push -f
B. git revert HEAD && git push origin main
C. git checkout HEAD~1
D. git delete commit
**Q14. Your repo has a large video.mp4 (400MB) accidentally committed. GitHub push fails. How do you remove it from
history and prevent future large files for 2024-2026 best practice?**
A. git rm video.mp4 and push
B. Use git filter-repo or git filter-branch / BFG to rewrite history removing the file, add it to .gitignore, and use Git LFS for large
files going forward, then force push with team coordination
C. Just add to .gitignore
D. Use git large-file
**Q15. Explain git cherry-pick and give a scenario where it's useful.**
A. It copies a specific commit from one branch to current branch. Useful to backport a hotfix from main to release branch
without merging entire branch.
B. It deletes a cherry
C. It merges all branches
D. It picks random commits
**Q16. You have main protected requiring PR reviews and status checks. How do you configure a GitHub branch protection
rule and what happens if you try direct push to main?**
A. Repo -> Settings -> Branches -> Add rule for main, check Require pull request before merging and Require status checks,
disallow bypass. Direct push is rejected with remote error.
B. Enable via git push protection
C. Can't protect main
D. Use .gitprotect file
**Q17. In a GitHub Actions workflow .github/workflows/ci.yml, you want to run tests on push to any branch and on PRs to
main, using Node 20. What trigger and setup is correct for 2024-2026?**
push:
pull_request:
branches: [main]
test:
runs-on: ubuntu-latest

--- PAGE 170 ---
steps:
- uses: actions/checkout@v4
- uses: actions/setup-node@v4
with: { node-version: 20 }
- run: npm ci && npm test
B. on: [manual only]
C. Use Jenkinsfile
D. on: push: branches: none
**Q18. What does git reset --soft HEAD~1, --mixed (default), and --hard do differently?**
A. --soft keeps changes staged in index, --mixed (default) keeps changes unstaged in working tree but unstages index, --hard
discards all changes in index and working tree
B. All delete commits permanently
C. They are same as revert
D. --soft deletes working tree
**Q19. How do you resolve a merge conflict in app.js during git merge feature, and how does Git mark conflicts?**
A. Git marks with <<<<<<< HEAD, =======, >>>>>>> feature sections, edit file to choose/combine, then git add app.js and
git commit to finish merge
B. Git auto deletes conflicted files
C. Run git conflict --resolve
D. Delete app.js
**Q20. What is GitHub's Pull Request workflow with forks and what does gh CLI do to streamline it?**
A. Fork -> clone fork -> branch -> commit -> push to fork -> open PR via GitHub UI or gh pr create, then review, CI, and gh pr
merge or squash merge. gh authenticates and automates PR creation from terminal.
B. Push directly to upstream always
C. Email patches
D. Use FTP
Intermediate Answer Key & Explanations
**Q11 Answer: A - git stash push --include-untracked**
git stash saves dirty working directory and index to a stack, allowing clean switch. --include-untracked also stashes new files,
and pop restores and drops the stash entry.
**Q12 Answer: A - Merge preserves history vs rebase rewrites linear**
Merge is non-destructive and keeps true topology, good for shared branches. Rebase creates cleaner linear log for feature
branches but rewrites SHA, so avoid on public branches to prevent force-push disruption.
**Q13 Answer: B - git revert HEAD && git push**
revert creates a new commit that undoes the changes of a previous commit without deleting history. Reset + force push would
rewrite public history and break collaborators.
**Q14 Answer: B - filter-repo + .gitignore + Git LFS**
git rm only removes from next commit, file remains in history and still blocks push >100MB GitHub limit. History rewriting via
git filter-repo (recommended replacement for BFG/filter-branch since 2023) fully purges it, LFS stores large binaries outside
Git.
**Q15 Answer: A - cherry-pick copies specific commit**
Cherry-pick creates new commit with same diff but new SHA on current branch. It's ideal for selective backports without
pulling whole feature branch, though it can cause duplicate commits if later merged.
**Q16 Answer: A - Settings -> Branches -> protection rule**
Branch protection enforces review, status checks, signed commits, and blocks force push via API. Direct push returns
GH006: Protected branch update failed because GitHub server rejects it.

--- PAGE 171 ---
**Q17 Answer: A - on push + pull_request with checkout@v4 and setup-node@v4**
This is canonical 2024-2026 Actions pattern. actions/checkout@v4 and setup-node@v4 are current stable versions with
Node20 runtime, replacing deprecated Node16 actions.
**Q18 Answer: A - --soft vs --mixed vs --hard scope**
Understanding reset's three trees (HEAD, index, working tree) is key to undoing. Soft is safe for recommiting, mixed for
unstaging, hard is destructive and permanently discards local work.
**Q19 Answer: A - Conflict markers <<<<<<< ======= >>>>>>>**
Git inserts markers around conflicting hunks from both sides. Developer must manually resolve, stage resolved file, and
commit; tools like VS Code or git mergetool help parse markers.
**Q20 Answer: A - Fork-based PR + gh CLI**
Fork workflow maintains security for open-source. gh CLI (GitHub official) lets you gh auth login, gh repo fork, gh pr create
--fill, gh pr view, gh pr checks without browser, adopted widely by 2024.
3. Advanced Level (10 Questions)
**Q21. Your team uses Conventional Commits and wants to auto-generate changelog and semantic versioning. A feature
branch has 30 messy commits that must be cleaned into 3 logical commits before PR merge to main. Detail exact Git +
GitHub strategy using interactive rebase, squash merge options, and protection to avoid losing authorship.**
A. Use git rebase -i main to squash/fixup 30 into 3, keep meaningful commit messages like feat: and fix:, force push to feature
branch with --force-with-lease, open PR, then use Squash or Rebase merge strategy according to repo policy, with protected
main requiring linear history
B. Use git merge --no-ff always
C. Delete commits and recommit
D. Use git reset --hard
**Q22. You need to debug when a bug was introduced between v2.0 and HEAD across 500 commits. What Git bisect
strategy with automation and how does it work internally?**
A. git bisect start; git bisect bad HEAD; git bisect good v2.0; git bisect run npm test (or script returning 0/1), Git does binary
search checking out middle commits, finds first bad commit in ~log2(500) ~9 steps
B. Manually checkout each commit
C. Use git blame only
D. Use git log --grep
**Q23. Explain Git internal objects: blob, tree, commit, tag, and how HEAD, index, reflog, and .git/refs work. How would you
recover a commit deleted by git reset --hard 2 days ago?**
A. Blob stores file content, tree maps names to blobs/trees, commit points to tree + parents + metadata, annotated tag is
object. HEAD is ref to current branch, index is staging area. Recover via git reflog which logs HEAD moves, find SHA, then git
branch recovery <SHA> or git checkout
B. Git stores files directly
C. Can't recover after hard reset
D. Use GitHub recycle bin
**Q24. You manage a monorepo with 10 teams. You need CODEOWNERS, required review from owning team, and prevent
secrets leak. Configure GitHub's code ownership + push protection + secret scanning for 2024-2026.**
A. Add .github/CODEOWNERS with patterns /apps/payment/ @org/payment-team, enable Branch protection -> Require
review from CODEOWNERS, enable Settings -> Security -> Secret scanning + Push protection, enable Private vulnerability
reporting, use secret-scanning.yml and bypass list
B. Use .gitowners file
C. Can't enforce ownership
D. Use manual Slack messages
**Q25. A GitHub Actions workflow needs to deploy to AWS without storing long-lived AWS keys in GitHub Secrets. Also
needs OIDC, environment approvals, and reusable workflows in 2025. Design it.**
A. Configure AWS IAM Identity Provider for GitHub OIDC (token.actions.githubusercontent.com), create IAM role with
StringLike: repo:org/repo:* condition, use aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials@v4 with role-to-assume, use environment:

--- PAGE 172 ---
production with required reviewers, and call reusable workflow via uses: ./.github/workflows/deploy.yml or
org/reusable/.github/workflows/deploy.yml@v1
B. Store AWS_ACCESS_KEY in Secrets always
C. Use root keys
D. Use personal access token
**Q26. You have a large repo (5GB history). git clone is slow for CI. How do you optimize with partial clone, shallow clone,
sparse-checkout, and GitHub's new features in 2024-2026?**
A. Use git clone --depth 1 --filter=blob:none --sparse + git sparse-checkout set --no-cone packages/app, enable GitHub's
sparse index and use actions/checkout@v4 with fetch-depth: 1 and filter: blob:none; for very large use Git LFS or split
monorepo with submodules / subtree or use scalar/git sparse-clone
B. Always full clone
C. Use zip download
D. Use FTP
**Q27. Explain git worktree vs git submodule vs git subtree vs git subrepo pattern. When would you use worktrees for parallel
development in 2024?**
A. git worktree add ../hotfix hotfix-branch creates linked working tree sharing same .git for parallel checkouts without
switching; submodule links another repo at fixed SHA, requires separate commits; subtree merges external repo history into
subdir. Worktrees are ideal for reviewing PR while continuing feature without stash.
B. All same feature
C. Worktree deletes repo
D. Submodule auto updates
**Q28. You accidentally pushed secrets (AWS key AKIA...) to public GitHub repo main history 5 commits ago. GitHub push
protection didn't block because bypass. Detail immediate mitigation steps including history rewrite, credential revocation, and
GitHub security workflow 2024-2026.**
A. Immediately revoke/rotate leaked key in AWS IAM, enable GitHub Secret Scanning alert, use git filter-repo or BFG to
purge file from all history, force push with --force-with-lease after team warning, notify stakeholders, open GitHub Security
advisory, add trufflehog/gitleaks pre-commit hook, enable push protection that blocks even admins
B. Just delete file and push
C. Make repo private temporarily and forget
D. Use git rm only
**Q29. How does Git's 3-way merge algorithm work, what are merge.ours, theirs, union, and recursive vs ort strategy? Give
config to auto-resolve lockfiles.**
A. 3-way uses common ancestor base + ours + theirs to compute changes. ort (Ostensibly Recursive's Twin) is default since
Git 2.33, faster than recursive. Drivers: -X ours keeps our side, -X theirs keeps theirs. For lockfiles you can set echo
'package-lock.json merge=binary' >> .gitattributes or custom driver, but better set merge=ours or union driver; e.g., git config
merge.unity.driver - is anti-pattern, use .gitattributes: pnpm-lock.yaml merge=binary
B. Git uses 2-way
C. Can't configure drivers
D. ort is deprecated
**Q30. You need to enforce Signed Commits, verified by GitHub, and explain GPG vs SSH vs S/MIME signatures in
2024-2026. How do you set up commit signing for team?**
A. Generate SSH key ssh-keygen -t ed25519, add signing key as allowed via GitHub Settings -> SSH and GPG keys ->
Signing key, config git config --global gpg.format ssh, git config --global user.signingkey ~/.ssh/id_ed25519.pub and git config
--global commit.gpgsign true; or GPG gpg --gen-key then git config gpg.format openpgp. GitHub verifies with Verified badge if
email matches. Require via Branch protection -> Require signed commits and via Rulesets in 2024.
B. Signing not supported
C. Use PGP only since 2010
D. Signing encrypts commit
Advanced Answer Key & Explanations

--- PAGE 173 ---
**Q21 Answer: A - Interactive rebase to reword/squash/fixup**
Interactive rebase lets you collapse messy WIP commits into logical atoms preserving authorship with Co-authored-by trailer.
Force-with-lease is safer than -f as it fails if remote changed, preventing accidental overwrites.
**Q22 Answer: A - git bisect with run script binary search**
Bisect automates binary search between good and bad to find regression in O(log n). git bisect run uses exit status to mark
good/bad automatically, essential for large histories and CI reproducible bugs.
**Q23 Answer: A - Objects blob/tree/commit/tag + HEAD/index/reflog**
Git is content-addressable SHA-1/SHA-256 DAG. Reflog keeps HEAD history for ~90 days locally even if branch refs deleted,
allowing recovery after reset unless GC pruned. Understanding objects explains why rewrites change SHA.
**Q24 Answer: A - CODEOWNERS + CODEOWNERS review + secret push protection**
CODEOWNERS enforces team ownership and review routing. GitHub's push protection (GA 2023) blocks secret pushes at
client side and server, secret scanning alerts catch historical leaks, mandatory for enterprise compliance in 2024-2026.
**Q25 Answer: A - GitHub OIDC federation with AWS IAM role, environments, reusable workflows**
OIDC eliminates long-lived secrets via short-lived tokens with aud/sub claims. Environments add manual approvals and
secrets scoping, reusable workflows deduplicate deployment logic following DRY and 2025 best practices.
**Q26 Answer: A - --filter=blob:none, --depth 1, sparse-checkout, scalar**
Partial clone fetches only needed objects, reducing 5GB to <200MB. Sparse-checkout limits working tree. GitHub Actions
checkout supports filter and sparse. Scalar (Microsoft) optimizes huge monorepos with background prefetch and FSMonitor.
**Q27 Answer: A - worktree for parallel working trees vs submodule/subtree for nested repos**
Worktrees share object store and allow multiple branches checked out simultaneously without cloning twice. Submodules pin
external repo at SHA (detached), subtree merges code, each has different update/merge semantics for dependency
management.
**Q28 Answer: A - Revoke immediately, then filter-repo purge, then harden**
Rotation is step 1 because history remains public even after delete. Purge requires rewriting all branches/tags, force push,
and notifying contributors to rebase. GitHub's push protection + pre-commit hooks with gitleaks prevent recurrence.
**Q29 Answer: A - 3-way merge with ort strategy and merge drivers**
Base+ours+theirs enables conflict detection; ort solves performance/memory of recursive for large merges. Merge drivers per
.gitattributes allow custom handling, e.g., keeping generated lockfiles via binary or union to reduce noise.
**Q30 Answer: A - SSH signing (new in 2022) vs GPG vs S/MIME, enforced via Rulesets**
SSH signing is simpler as devs already have SSH keys, supported by GitHub since 2022, verification via key in account. GPG
is legacy but still common. New Rulesets (2024 GA) replace branch protection and enforce signing organization-wide.

# RabbitMQ & Messaging - Certification Exam
**Course Code:** 25_RabbitMQ
**Total Questions:** 30 (10 Beginner, 10 Intermediate, 10 Advanced)
**Pass Mark:** 70% (21/30)
**Time Limit:** 90 minutes
**Version:** 2024-2026 (RabbitMQ 3.12.x / 3.13.x, AMQP 0-9-1, AMQP 1.0 preview, Quorum Queues default)
Part A: Beginner Level (Questions 1-10)
Q1. What is the primary protocol used by RabbitMQ for messaging?
A) HTTP/2
B) AMQP 0-9-1
C) MQTT 5.0 only
D) gRPC
Q2. In RabbitMQ, what is the role of an Exchange?
A) Stores messages persistently on disk
B) Receives messages from producers and routes them to queues based on rules
C) Consumes messages and processes them
D) Authenticates client connections
Q3. Which exchange type routes messages to all bound queues regardless
of routing key?
A) Direct exchange
B) Topic exchange
C) Fanout exchange
D) Headers exchange
Q4. What is a vhost in RabbitMQ?
A) A virtual machine hosting RabbitMQ
B) A logical grouping of exchanges, queues, and permissions providing multi-tenancy
C) A backup of queue data
D) A type of consumer
Q5. Which of the following is the default port for AMQP client connections
to RabbitMQ?
A) 5432
B) 6379
C) 5672
D) 15672
Q6. What happens to a non-persistent message when RabbitMQ restarts?
A) It is recovered from disk

--- PAGE 175 ---
B) It is lost
C) It is moved to a dead-letter exchange
D) It is automatically re-queued with higher priority
Q7. Which RabbitMQ management plugin port serves the HTTP
management UI and REST API by default?
A) 5672
B) 25672
C) 15672
D) 1883
Q8. In RabbitMQ, what does basic.ack do?
A) Publishes a new message
B) Acknowledges that a message was successfully processed by a consumer
C) Creates a new queue
D) Binds a queue to an exchange
Q9. What is the purpose of a binding in RabbitMQ?
A) To encrypt messages
B) To link an exchange to a queue with an optional routing key / pattern
C) To bind a consumer to a vhost
D) To create a user permission
Q10. Which client library is the official RabbitMQ-maintained Java client?
A) rabbitmq-java-client (com.rabbitmq:amqp-client)
B) spring-amqp
C) qpid-jms-client
D) pika
Answer Key - Beginner Level
| Question | Correct Answer | Explanation |
|----------|---------------|-------------|
| Q1 | B | AMQP 0-9-1 is the core protocol. RabbitMQ also supports MQTT, STOMP, AMQP 1.0 via plugins, but primary is
AMQP 0-9-1. |
| Q2 | B | Exchanges are routers. Queues store messages. Producers never write directly to queues in AMQP model. |
| Q3 | C | Fanout ignores routing key and broadcasts to all bound queues. |
| Q4 | B | Vhosts isolate resources and permissions, similar to virtual hosts / namespaces. |
| Q5 | C | 5672 for AMQP, 5671 for AMQP TLS. 15672 is management UI, 25672 clustering, 1883 MQTT. |
| Q6 | B | Non-persistent messages exist only in RAM. Persistent requires both persistent delivery_mode=2 AND durable
queue. |
| Q7 | C | 15672 is management UI. Enabled via rabbitmq-plugins enable rabbitmq_management. |
| Q8 | B | Consumer acknowledgments prevent message loss. Unacked messages are requeued on consumer failure. |
| Q9 | B | Binding defines routing relationship. For direct: exact key match. Topic: pattern match (* and #). |
| Q10 | A | com.rabbitmq:amqp-client is official. Pika is Python, Spring AMQP builds on top of official client. |

Answer Key - Intermediate Level
| Question | Correct Answer | Explanation |
|----------|---------------|-------------|
| Q11 | B | Since 3.8, Quorum Queues using Raft are recommended. Since 3.12, classic queue mirroring (ha-mode) was
removed. Quorum queues are default for new queues in some client wrappers from 2024+. |
| Q12 | B | x-message-ttl is queue-level default TTL. Can be overridden by per-message expiration. Triggers dead-lettering or
discard. |
| Q13 | B | DLX routing occurs on reject/nack requeue=false, TTL expiry, max-length overflow. Requires
x-dead-letter-exchange arg. |
| Q14 | B | QoS prefetch is critical for work queues. Without it one slow consumer could hoard all messages. Recommended
10-50 for throughput. |
| Q15 | A | Topic spec: * substitutes exactly one dot-separated word, # substitutes zero or more words. Example: logs.#
matches logs.error.db |
| Q16 | B | Quorum queues are CP (consistent) via Raft. Need (N/2)+1 replicas. Minority partition cannot elect leader / accept
writes. |
| Q17 | B | Streams (since 3.9) provide append-only log, high throughput, non-destructive consuming with offsets. Ideal for
event sourcing. |
| Q18 | B | Publisher confirms guarantee broker responsibility. Required for reliable publishing. waitForConfirms or async
ConfirmListener. |
| Q19 | B | Mandatory + alternate exchange are two ways to handle unroutable messages. Without either, unroutable
messages are dropped. |
| Q20 | B | Shovel is core for inter-broker replication, much more reliable than federation for point-to-point WAN replication.
Dynamic shovels since 3.12. |

--- PAGE 180 ---
incompatible migration path managed via feature flags
B) Khepri is slower but uses less memory

Answer Key - Advanced Level
| Question | Correct Answer | Explanation |
|----------|---------------|-------------|
| Q21 | B | 3.13 introduced soft_limit for quorum command batching to control memory. Default formula prevents OOM when
publishers outpace Raft log apply. Tunable via rabbitmq.conf. |
| Q22 | B | Super streams partition by key hash; order guaranteed per partition. SAC per partition avoids competing
consumers. 3.12+ super stream client support matured (Java/Go/Python). |
| Q23 | B | Disabling disk alarm risks data loss/corruption. Proper fix: expand disk, monitor, use separate data dirs, set
retention. Memory watermark 0.4-0.6 typical, not >1. |
| Q24 | A | Master locator removed with mirroring removal. For quorum queues: leader balanced via rabbitmq-queues
rebalance. Can also use rabbitmq-queues --vhost ... rebalance. |
| Q25 | A | Core AMQP 1.0 difference: credit flow, link-based. RabbitMQ maps AMQP 1.0 addresses to queues/exchanges.
Important for interoperability in 2024-2026. |
| Q26 | B | Low latency + durability requires async confirms, quorum for HA, prefetch for steady flow, SSD, Raft tuning. Sync
confirms kill throughput. |
| Q27 | B | x-delivery-limit is native poison handling for quorum queues since 3.8.11+. Classic needs custom retry topology
(retry -> wait -> DLX -> main). |
| Q28 | B | OffsetTracking + Consumer Update is 3.12+ feature allowing smart SAC failover, e.g., resume with offset spec or
handle promotions. |
| Q29 | B | True exactly-once impossible in distributed messaging alone. Effectively-once requires idempotency + outbox +
deduplication. This is industry standard pattern for 2024-2026. |
| Q30 | A | Khepri replaces Mnesia. Uses Raft. Solves long-standing Mnesia limitations for large metadata sets. Feature flag
khepri_db introduced 3.13, GA 4.0. |

# Elasticsearch & ELK Stack - Certification Exam
**Course Code:** 26_ELK
**Total Questions:** 30 (10 Beginner, 10 Intermediate, 10 Advanced)
**Passing Score:** 70%
**Time Limit:** 90 minutes
**Version:** 2024-2026 (Elasticsearch 8.x / 8.13+)

| 7 | B | Documents are JSON objects containing fields. |

| 9 | B | Beats (Filebeat, Metricbeat, etc.) are lightweight shippers. |
| 10 | C | POST /<index>/_doc creates document with auto ID; PUT /<index>/_doc/<id> with specified ID. |

Answer Key - Intermediate
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 11 | B | Mapping types deprecated in 6.x and removed in 7.0+. In 8.x, single type, typeless APIs. |
| 12 | B | Grok uses %{PATTERN:field} to extract structured data. Dissect is faster alternative since 7.x. |

| 15 | B | _bulk API uses NDJSON: action line + optional source line. Far more efficient than individual requests. |
| 16 | A | ILM manages hot -> warm -> cold -> frozen -> delete phases. Used with data streams and rollover. |
| 17 | B | dense_vector added kNN since 8.0, GA improved in 8.4+. HNSW graph, int8 quantization in 8.7+, BBF in 8.13+.
Essential for RAG/gen AI. |
| 18 | B | Data View ties Kibana visualizations to one or more indices (e.g., logs-*) and time field. |
| 19 | B | Filebeat modules pre-configure inputs, parsers, pipelines, dashboards: filebeat modules enable nginx. |
| 20 | B | _source is stored original JSON. Can be disabled or filtered but usually retained for reindex, highlighting, fetch. |

Q28. For 2024-2026 observability, which Elastic Stack approach is
recommended to replace legacy Logstash-heavy ingestion?
A) Only Logstash is supported
B) Elastic Agent with Fleet-managed integrations (replaces multiple Beats) with edge processing, plus Logstash only where
advanced transformation needed
C) Disable ingestion
D) Use Kafka alone
Q29. A query using wildcard "*error*" at start is slow. How to optimize?
A) Keep as-is
B) Avoid leading wildcard; use edge n-gram/autocomplete, search_as_you_type, keyword with wildcard field type (2021+)
which uses ngrams for efficient wildcard/regex, or use ESQL LIKE optimizations
C) Increase shards
D) Use SQL
Q30. You are designing ES|QL (Elasticsearch Query Language, GA in 8.15)
query for security analytics. Which ES|QL statement is valid?
A) SELECT * WHERE log.level = "error"
B) FROM logs-* | WHERE @timestamp > NOW() - 1 hour | STATS count=COUNT(*) BY host.name | SORT count DESC |
LIMIT 10
C) GET logs/_search {match_all}
D) db.logs.find({level: "error"})
Answer Key - Advanced
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 21 | B | Data streams are recommended for time-series in 8.x. Rollover prevents oversized shards (ideal 10-50GB).
Searchable snapshots reduce storage cost; hot-warm-cold ILM introduced in 7.x, refined 8.x. |
| 22 | B | Mapping explosion causes heap/memory issues. Mitigate via flattened for dynamic key-value, strict mapping control,
runtime fields (8.x) to avoid indexing unused, ECS normalization. |
| 23 | B | Core concept: text -> inverted index tokens, keyword -> doc_values for sort/agg/exact. Use multi-fields: "field" text +
"field.keyword". |
| 24 | B | In 8.0 security enabled by default. Bootstrap creates certs, enrollment token for Kibana/nodes, enables TLS.
Previously opt-in. |
| 25 | B | CCS = search across clusters (platinum?). CCR = replication (active-passive/follow). Important distinction for global
clusters. CCS is available on all tiers, CCR needs enterprise/platinum. |
| 26 | B | Percentile via tdigest (default) or HDR (more accurate but memory). ES|QL 8.11+ introduces PERCENTILE. For
high-cardinality use sampling or rollups. |
| 27 | B | Architecture design: dedicate master, data hot/warm, coordinating (load balancer), ingest, ML nodes. Coordinating
node smart routing. |
| 28 | B | 2024 strategy: Elastic Agent unified agent (single binary replaces Beats), Fleet central management, prebuilt
integrations (500+). Logstash still valid for heavy transformation. |
| 29 | B | Leading wildcard cannot use index and triggers full scan. wildcard field type uses binary doc values + ngram index to
accelerate contains/regex. Edge n-grams better for autocomplete. |
| 30 | B | ES|QL is piped language: FROM source | WHERE | STATS | SORT | LIMIT. Introduced tech preview 8.11, GA
8.13-8.15. Becoming default in Discover. Correct piped syntax is option B. |
Exam Summary

# Course Title: Prometheus & Grafana - Monitoring and Observability Deep Dive
Section 1: Beginner (Questions 1-10)
**1. What is the primary data model used by Prometheus for storing time-series data?**
A) Documents with JSON fields
B) Multi-dimensional time series identified by metric name and key/value labels
C) Relational tables with timestamp columns
D) Graph nodes and edges
**2. By default, on which port does the Prometheus server listen?**
A) 3000
B) 9090
C) 8080
D) 9093
**3. In Prometheus, what is an Exporter?**
A) A Grafana plugin to export dashboards to PDF
B) A piece of software that exposes existing metrics from third-party systems in Prometheus format
C) A component that exports data from Prometheus to long-term storage
D) A tool to convert PromQL to SQL
**4. Which HTTP endpoint do Prometheus exporters typically use to expose metrics?**
A) /api/v1/metrics
B) /metrics
C) /prom/metrics
D) /stats
**5. What is Grafana's primary role in a Prometheus stack?**
A) To scrape and store metrics
B) To trigger alerts via Alertmanager
C) To visualize metrics, build dashboards, and explore data from multiple data sources
D) To manage service discovery
**6. What are the four official Prometheus metric types?**
A) Count, Sum, Average, Percentile
B) Counter, Gauge, Histogram, Summary
C) Integer, Float, String, Boolean
D) Rate, Increase, Delta, Irate
**7. Which PromQL function calculates the per-second average rate of increase for a Counter?**
A) increase()
B) delta()
C) rate()
D) avg_over_time()
**8. In Grafana, what is a Data Source?**
A) A folder where dashboards are stored
B) A connection configuration to a backend system like Prometheus, Loki, or MySQL where Grafana queries data
C) A user role for viewing panels
D) An alert notification channel
**9. What file does Prometheus use for its main configuration?**

--- PAGE 189 ---
A) prometheus.yaml
B) prometheus.yml
C) prometheus.json
D) prom.conf
**10. What is the purpose of Alertmanager in the Prometheus ecosystem?**
A) To visualize firing alerts on Grafana dashboards
B) To handle alerts sent by Prometheus server, including deduplicating, grouping, silencing, and routing them to receivers like
Slack, PagerDuty, or email
C) To automatically fix the underlying issue that caused the alert
D) To scrape alerting rules from targets
Answer Key - Beginner
| Question | Correct Answer | Explanation |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | B | Prometheus stores data as time series identified by metric name + label set. |
| 2 | B | Prometheus server runs on 9090 by default; Grafana on 3000; Alertmanager on 9093. |
| 3 | B | Exporters (e.g., node_exporter, blackbox_exporter) translate system metrics to Prometheus exposition format. |
| 4 | B | /metrics is the standard exposition endpoint. |
| 5 | C | Grafana is the visualization and dashboarding layer, not storage or scraping. |
| 6 | B | The four core types are Counter, Gauge, Histogram, and Summary. |
| 7 | C | rate() calculates per-second rate; increase() gives total increase over range. |
| 8 | B | Data Source defines where Grafana gets data from. |
| 9 | B | Main config file is prometheus.yml. |
| 10 | B | Alertmanager manages the alert lifecycle after Prometheus evaluates rules. |

**20. What is Recording Rule in Prometheus?**

C) A rule to record alerts as audio files

Answer Key - Intermediate
| Question | Correct Answer | Explanation |
|---|---|---|
| 11 | C | sum by (code) (rate(...)) correctly calculates per-second rate then aggregates by code label. A is invalid syntax, B
misses aggregation, D uses avg incorrectly. |
| 12 | B | Relabeling (relabel_configs and metric_relabel_configs) mutates target labels to control scraping. |

--- PAGE 191 ---
| 13 | B | Histogram: server-side quantile via histogram_quantile() and aggregatable. Summary: client-side quantiles, not
aggregatable. Also Native Histograms (SPARSE histograms) stable as of 2024+. |
| 14 | B | Prometheus 2.50+ added stable Native Histograms, OTEL UTF-8 names, and Prometheus 3.0 (Nov 2024)
introduced new UI, improved query performance, and better compression. |
| 15 | B | $__rate_interval = max($__interval + scrape_interval, 4*scrape_interval) prevents aliasing. Recommended since
Grafana 9. |
| 16 | B | High cardinality kills TSDB. Never use unbounded IDs as labels. Drop or refactor to logs/traces. |
| 17 | B | promtool is essential for validation and TSDB debugging. |
| 18 | C | Unified Alerting evaluates inside Grafana, supports many data sources, routes via contact points or external AM. |
| 19 | B | changes() counts value changes in range vector; alternative: last_over_time comparison. |
| 20 | B | Recording rules create new series like job:http_requests:rate5m for efficiency. |
Section 3: Advanced (Questions 21-30)
**21. In Prometheus TSDB, what is the purpose of the Head block vs. persisted blocks, and how does WAL work?**
A) Head block is for alerts, persisted blocks are for metrics; WAL is Write-Ahead Log for Grafana
B) Head block is mutable in-memory block for recent samples (2h default); WAL ensures durability on crash by logging
samples; every 2h Head is compacted to immutable persisted block on disk with compaction and retention
C) Head block stores config, persisted blocks store logs
D) WAL means Web Alert Logging
**22. You are querying histogram_quantile(0.99, sum by (le) (rate(http_request_duration_seconds_bucket[5m]))) and getting
high 99th percentile. With Prometheus Native Histograms (2024+), what is the modern equivalent?**
A) No equivalent exists
B) histogram_quantile(0.99, sum by (le) (...)) still required
C) histogram_quantile(0.99, sum(rate(http_request_duration_seconds[5m]))) where the metric uses new native histogram
type with histogram_quantile directly on sparse histogram without le buckets
D) quantile(0.99, http_request_duration_seconds)
**23. In Grafana 10-11 (2024-2026), what are Correlations and Scenes, and how are they used?**
A) Correlations link data sources (e.g., from Prometheus metrics to Loki logs/traceID); Scenes is the new dashboarding
architecture powering Dashboards and Explore
B) They are legacy features removed in 2024
C) Correlations are alert rules, Scenes are panel types
D) They only work with MySQL
**24. How does Prometheus handle staleness and the lookback-delta logic as of Prometheus 3.x?**
A) Stale markers are not used
B) If a target stops exposing a time series, Prometheus inserts a stale marker; query with 5-minute lookback by default
(--query.lookback-delta default 5m) will not return value older than that unless specified; as of 2024, created timestamp and
stale handling improvements reduce false extrapolation
C) Prometheus keeps series forever
D) Staleness is handled by Grafana only
**25. You need to reduce Prometheus memory usage with 1M active series. Which combination is effective in 2025-2026?**
A) Increase scrape_interval to 30s/60s, drop unused labels via metric_relabel_configs, use recording rules to drop raw series
after aggregation, enable --storage.tsdb.min-block-duration tuning, and use Agent Mode + remote write to central
Thanos/Mimir/Cortex for long-term storage
B) Set retention to infinite
C) Add more exporters
D) Only use increase() function
**26. For Grafana multi-tenant setup, how do you isolate data sources and dashboards for 50 teams efficiently in Grafana
10+?**
A) Create 50 separate Grafana servers

--- PAGE 192 ---
B) Use Grafana Organizations or Teams + Folder + RBAC + Data source permissions (Enterprise) + provisioning via

C) Share admin password with all teams
D) Use one dashboard for all
**27. Explain Exemplars in Prometheus & Grafana and how to enable them.**
A) Exemplars are random sample values
B) Exemplars store Trace ID as reference alongside metrics (e.g., from OpenTelemetry instrumentation). Enable in
Prometheus via --enable-feature=exemplar-storage and exemplars: {max_exemplars: ...} in config (enabled by default in
Prometheus 2.40+, 3.x); In Grafana, enable Exemplars toggle in Prometheus data source and show as dots on graph linked
to Tempo/Jaeger
C) Exemplars are Grafana Enterprise licenses
D) Exemplars are log files
**28. Consider PromQL: sum by (job) (rate(errors[5m])) / sum by (job) (rate(requests[5m])) > 0.05. What advanced concern
exists?**
A) No concern, query is perfect
B) Division by zero and partial series matching can fire incorrectly; should use: ((sum by (job) (rate(errors[5m])) / sum by (job)
(rate(requests[5m])) > 0.05) and (sum by (job) (rate(requests[5m])) > threshold)) or handle absent/error via >0 filter, and
ensure label matching alignment. Also use group_left if needed and consider absent() handling
C) Query needs avg()
D) Query needs SQL
**29. In Thanos / Cortex / Mimir architecture built on Prometheus remote-write (2024-2026 standard), what does the Query
Frontend and Compactor do?**
A) Nothing, they are deprecated
B) Query Frontend: caches, splits queries by time, retries, rate-limits in Mimir/Grafana Cloud; Compactor: compacts blocks
from object storage, downsamples (in Thanos), handles retention and deduplication for HA Prometheus pairs
C) They only generate PDF reports
D) They replace Grafana
**30. You are debugging slow Grafana dashboard with 20 panels querying Prometheus with rate(metric[5m]) over last 7 days
at 10s step. How do you optimize per Grafana & Prometheus best practices 2025?**
A) Add more panels
B) Use recording rules to pre-aggregate, increase min step / $__rate_interval or use 1m step auto via $__interval, enable
Grafana query caching (2024+ built-in), use query-frontend in Mimir, reduce cardinality, use exemplar sampling, and set
query lookback limits
C) Set interval to 1ms
D) Disable Prometheus
Answer Key - Advanced
| Question | Correct Answer | Explanation |
|---|---|---|
| 21 | B | Head = recent 2h mutable block, WAL for crash recovery, compaction creates 2h-> etc blocks. |
| 22 | C | Native Histograms (stable Prometheus 1.0+ spec 2023, widely adopted 2024-2026) don't use le buckets. Query is
histogram_quantile(q, sum(rate(metric[range]))). Uses sparse/dense representation. |
| 23 | A | Correlations (2023 GA) link metrics->logs->traces. Scenes (Grafana 10+) is React framework replacing old
dashboard render. |
| 24 | B | Stale markers from target removal + 5m lookback prevents stale data graphing. Prometheus 2.42+ / 3.x improved
created timestamps handling (--enable-feature=created-timestamp-zero-ingestion). |
| 25 | A | Valid cost optimization stack: fewer scrapes, relabel drop, recording rules, agent mode, remote-write to
Mimir/Thanos with downsampling. |
| 26 | B | Best practice is multi-tenancy via Orgs/RBAC/Folders + IaC provisioning (grafana provider for Terraform). Enterprise
adds fine-grained data source perms. |

--- PAGE 193 ---
| 27 | B | Exemplars link metrics to traces, crucial for observability correlation. Enabled by default now but Grafana data
source needs Exemplars=true. |
| 28 | B | Division can yield NaN if denominator 0; vector matching can cause missing alerts; add min request volume guard
and use group_left/group_right as needed. Also consider or vector(0) handling. |
| 29 | B | In modern long-term storage, Frontend handles query splitting/caching, Compactor deduplicates and compacts. |
| 30 | B | Optimize by recording rules, bigger step, caching, query-frontend, cardinality reduction. Querying 7d at 10s step =
60k points per series - heavy. |

# SRE & Observability - Comprehensive Examination
**Course Code:** 28_SRE
**Total Questions:** 30
**Passing Score:** 70%
**Time Limit:** 90 minutes
**Valid For:** 2024-2026 syllabus covering SLI/SLO, MTTR/MTBF, OpenTelemetry, Prometheus, Grafana, incident
management, and chaos engineering
Part A: Beginner Level (Questions 1-10)
Q1. What does SRE stand for in the context of modern software
engineering?
A) System Reliability Engineering
B) Site Reliability Engineering
C) Software Resilience Engineering
D) Service Recovery Engineering
Q2. Which of the "Four Golden Signals" defined by Google SRE focuses
on the rate of requests that fail?
A) Latency
B) Traffic
C) Errors
D) Saturation
Q3. What is an SLI (Service Level Indicator)?
A) A business agreement with customers about uptime
B) A quantitative measure of some aspect of service quality, e.g., request latency or success rate
C) A post-incident report
D) A tool for log aggregation
Q4. In observability, which pillar answers the question "HOW LONG did
something take?"
A) Logs
B) Metrics
C) Traces
D) Profiling
Q5. What does MTTR stand for?
A) Mean Time To Recovery / Repair
B) Maximum Time To Response
C) Minimum Time To Restore
D) Mean Ticket Tracking Rate

--- PAGE 195 ---
Q6. Which tool is primarily used for metrics collection and alerting with a
pull-based model and PromQL?
A) Elasticsearch
B) Jaeger
C) Prometheus
D) Fluentd
Q7. What is the purpose of an error budget?
A) To allocate money for fixing bugs
B) To quantify the acceptable amount of unreliability before freezing features and focusing on reliability
C) To count the number of errors in logs
D) To budget for on-call compensation
Q8. An on-call rotation is best described as:
A) A database replication strategy
B) A schedule that distributes incident response responsibility among team members to ensure 24/7 coverage
C) A load balancing algorithm
D) A deployment strategy that rotates servers
Q9. What is a runbook in SRE?
A) A log of all production deployments
B) A compiled list of source code execution steps
C) A documented set of procedures for routine operational tasks and incident response
D) A financial ledger for cloud costs
Q10. Which open-source standard (now CNCF graduated) provides
vendor-neutral APIs/SDKs for traces, metrics, and logs?
A) OpenTracing
B) OpenCensus
C) OpenTelemetry (OTel)
D) StatsD
Answer Key - Beginner Level
**Q1. Answer: B) Site Reliability Engineering**
Explanation: Coined by Ben Treynor at Google in 2003. SRE applies software engineering principles to operations and
infrastructure problems.
**Q2. Answer: C) Errors**
Explanation: Four Golden Signals are Latency, Traffic, Errors, Saturation. Errors measures rate of failed requests (HTTP 5xx,
etc.).
**Q3. Answer: B) A quantitative measure of some aspect of service quality, e.g., request latency or success rate**
Explanation: SLI is the actual measured metric. SLO is the target range for that SLI. SLA is the contractual agreement.
**Q4. Answer: B) Metrics**
Explanation: Metrics are numeric aggregations over time (latency p95, QPS). Logs provide context (WHAT happened),
Traces show causation and flow (WHERE and WHY), Metrics show trends.

--- PAGE 196 ---
**Q5. Answer: A) Mean Time To Recovery / Repair**
Explanation: MTTR measures average time from failure detection to service restoration. Key reliability metric alongside MTBF
(Mean Time Between Failures).
**Q6. Answer: C) Prometheus**
Explanation: Prometheus (CNCF graduated, 2012) uses pull model, time-series DB, and PromQL. Became de-facto standard
for Kubernetes monitoring.
**Q7. Answer: B) To quantify the acceptable amount of unreliability before freezing features and focusing on reliability**
Explanation: Error Budget = 100% - SLO. If SLO is 99.9% availability, error budget is 0.1% downtime (~43m/month).
Exhaustion triggers policy to prioritize reliability.
**Q8. Answer: B) A schedule that distributes incident response responsibility among team members to ensure 24/7
coverage**
Explanation: Prevents burnout, ensures clear ownership. Modern tools: PagerDuty, Opsgenie, Grafana OnCall.
**Q9. Answer: C) A documented set of procedures for routine operational tasks and incident response**
Explanation: Runbooks reduce MTTR, enable less-experienced on-callers, and are essential for incident management
maturity.
**Q10. Answer: C) OpenTelemetry (OTel)**
Explanation: OTel merged OpenTracing + OpenCensus, graduated CNCF in 2024. Provides unified collection for traces,
metrics, logs, with wide language and vendor support.
Part B: Intermediate Level (Questions 11-20)

Q20. What is chaos engineering, popularized by Netflix's Chaos Monkey
and now CNCF Chaos Mesh / LitmusChaos?
A) Randomly shutting down data centers without approval

--- PAGE 198 ---
B) The disciplined practice of injecting controlled failures into production-like environments to build confidence in resilience
before outages occur
C) A method for debugging code by adding random errors
D) A security penetration testing technique only
Answer Key - Intermediate Level
**Q11. Answer: B) ~40.3 minutes**
Explanation: 28 days = 40320 minutes. 0.1% of that = 40.32 minutes. For 30-day month, ~43.2m (common interview
shortcut). 28-day rolling is Google-recommended to avoid month-length variation. Calculation: 28*24*60*0.001 = 40.32.
**Q12. Answer: B) histogram_quantile(0.95, sum(rate(http_request_duration_seconds_bucket[5m])) by (le, service))**
Explanation: Prometheus histogram quantiles require _bucket metric, rate(), aggregation preserving le label, and
histogram_quantile() function. Option A averages, C/D are invalid syntax.
**Q13. Answer: B) To link spans across service boundaries by passing trace-id, parent-id, and sampling flags**
Explanation: W3C Trace Context (traceparent: 00-{trace-id}-{parent-id}-{flags}) is the 2020+ standard for propagation.
Enables distributed tracing across polyglot services without vendor lock-in. Adopted by OTel default.
**Q14. Answer: A) Symptom-based alerts on user-visible impact (SLO burn), cause-based on internal states (CPU high);
symptom alerts should page humans**
Explanation: Google SRE book (Chapter 5): Page on symptoms directly impacting users/SLOs. Cause-based (CPU 80%)
should be ticket or info, not paging, because they may not affect users.
**Q15. Answer: B) Implement multi-window, multi-burn-rate SLO alerting, eliminate noisy alerts, and page only on actionable,
user-impacting conditions**
Explanation: Multi-burn-rate (Google SRE Workbook 2016, still standard in 2024-26) uses fast burn (1h) + slow burn (6h) to
reduce false positives. Actionable, no toil, tuned thresholds.
**Q16. Answer: B) Receivers, Processors, Exporters**
Explanation: OTel Collector pipeline: Receiver gets data (OTLP, Prometheus etc.), Processor mutates/batches/filters (batch,
memory_limiter, attributes), Exporter sends to backend (Jaeger, Prometheus, OTLP).
**Q17. Answer: B) Setting SLO at 100% availability**
Explanation: 100% SLO is impossible and removes error budget, halts innovation, creates impossible on-call burden. Perfect
availability violates CAP and real-world constraints. SLO should reflect user happiness (99.9, 99.95, etc.)
**Q18. Answer: B) Toil - manual, repetitive, automatable operational work; SRE teams should keep toil < 50% of time**
Explanation: Toil definition from Google: Manual, repetitive, automatable, tactical, no enduring value, scales linearly. Google
SRE mandates <50% toil to allow engineering work to reduce toil.
**Q19. Answer: B) Loki indexes only metadata/labels and compresses chunks, making it cheaper; Elasticsearch indexes full
text for fast full-text search**
Explanation: Loki design (like Prometheus but for logs) trades full-text indexing speed for cost efficiency. Ideal for Kubernetes
where labels are abundant. Uses LogQL. Huge adoption 2024-26 due to cost.
**Q20. Answer: B) The disciplined practice of injecting controlled failures into production-like environments to build
confidence in resilience before outages occur**
Explanation: Principles of Chaos (2022+): Build hypothesis, blast radius control, abort, run in prod. Tools: Chaos Mesh,
LitmusChaos (CNCF incubating), Gremlin, AWS FIS (Fault Injection Simulator).

processor; best practice hybrid: head 1% + tail keep all errors >1s
B) Tail-based is always cheaper
C) No sampling needed in 2025, store everything

A) Ignore alert
B) Automated: freeze canary/progressive rollouts via CI gate checking error budget, auto-rollback if recent deployment
correlates; Procedural: IC assesses severity, halt feature pushes if burn > 10% in hour, route excessive alert volume to

C) Immediately delete service
D) Increase SLO threshold to 100% to avoid burn
Answer Key - Advanced Level
**Q21. Answer: A) If burn continues, entire 28-day budget will be consumed in ~2 days (28/14.4 ≈ 1.94 days)**
Explanation: Burn rate 1 = budget consumes exactly in SLO window. Burn 14.4x means budget exhausted in 28/14.4 days =
~1.94 days. Alert logic from SRE Workbook: page when burn 14x for 1h AND burn 6x for 6h. 14.4 often used for 2% budget in
1h for 99.9% SLO.
**Q22. Answer: A) Span is unit of work with start/end, SpanEvent is timestamped annotation within span, Span Link connects
causally related but not parent-child traces (e.g., batch job fan-out)**
Explanation: OTel data model 2024: Span = operation, Event = something happening at timepoint (e.g., exception stacktrace,
cache miss). Link: for async patterns where multiple upstream traces trigger one downstream (e.g., Kafka batch consumer
links to many producer traces). Critical for event-driven architectures.
**Q23. Answer: B) Remove high-cardinality label from metric; move user-specific detail to traces/logs with exemplars linking
metrics to traces**

--- PAGE 201 ---
Explanation: Prometheus cardinality best practice: keep labels bounded <1000 values per metric. user_id causes TSDB
explosion (M * cardinality). Solution: use exemplars (Prometheus 2.25+ feature, stable 2024) to link histogram bucket to trace
ID containing user_id. Traces handle high-cardinality.
**Q24. Answer: B) Progressive delivery: canary + automated analysis (Kayenta/Flagger/Argo Rollouts) + automatic rollback
on SLO violations via error-budget-aware gates**
Explanation: 2024-2026 industry standard: Argo Rollouts / Flagger with metrics analysis (p95, error rate vs baseline).
Integrates with error budget - if budget <25% remaining, block deploys automatically. Google's SRE prod pipeline uses this.
**Q25. Answer: B) Ratio of successful stream messages delivered / requested, plus stream establishment success +
freshness/throughput SLI for data lag**
Explanation: Streaming SLI is complex: availability = successful handshakes + messages within lag threshold. Also need
freshness SLI (how old is latest data). Inspired by Google streaming SLI examples in Implementing Service Level Objectives
(2020) and gRPC OTel observability 2024.
**Q26. Answer: B) Incident Commander (decision maker), Communications Lead, Operations Lead/Ops, Subject Matter
Experts; blameless structure, defined by IMS**
Explanation: IMS/Incident Management System + Google IRM: IC has final decision, not technical fixer. Comms handles
stakeholders. Ops drives mitigation. Blameless postmortem mandatory. Anti-pattern: IC also debugging.
**Q27. Answer: B) Use push via OTel Collector or Prometheus Pushgateway/OTLP gateway with ephemeral job handling, or
use metrics from OTel SDK pushing to Collector, since pull will likely miss short-lived targets; also expose via OpenMetrics
with kube-state-metrics**
Explanation: Prometheus pull scrapes 15s default, misses < scrape interval jobs. Pushgateway (or better OTel Collector with
Prometheus remote write) is solution for batch. Modern 2025 recommendation: OTel SDK pushes to Collector via OTLP,
Collector exports via remote-write to Mimir/Thanos. kube-job metrics via kube-state-metrics for completion tracking.
**Q28. Answer: A) RED: Rate, Errors, Duration - for request-driven services (user-facing). USE: Utilization, Saturation, Errors
- for resources (CPU, disk, DB). Apply RED to services, USE to infrastructure**
Explanation: RED by Tom Wilkie (Weaveworks) for microservices - perfect for SLI mapping. USE by Brendan Gregg for
infrastructure saturation. Mature SRE 2024-26 uses both: dashboards show RED (top) + USE (bottom). Alerts on RED
(symptoms).
**Q29. Answer: A) Head-based decides sampling decision at trace start (cheaper, may miss interesting errors); Tail-based
collects all spans initially, samples after observing entire trace (can keep all errors/latent traces) - requires OTel Collector's
tail_sampling processor; best practice hybrid: head 1% + tail keep all errors >1s**
Explanation: Tail sampling allows intelligent retention (keep 100% errors, slow traces, high-value tenants). Tradeoff: needs
buffering (memory). OTel Collector tail_sampling with composite policy: status_code=ERROR, latency >1s, probabilistic 10%.
Major cost optimization for observability 2024-2026.
**Q30. Answer: B) Automated: freeze canary/progressive rollouts via CI gate checking error budget, auto-rollback if recent
deployment correlates; Procedural: IC assesses severity, halt feature pushes if burn > 10% in hour, route excessive alert
volume to error-budget-aware SLO dashboard, initiate freeze per policy, post incident channel, prioritize reliability tasks until
budget replenishes (28d rolling)**
Explanation: This is the full Error Budget Policy contract (Google SRE book chap 34, 2024 adaptations). Key actions: Enforce
freeze, not blame. 72x burn = 5% in 30 min => will exhaust full budget in ~10 hours if sustained (100/5 *0.5h=10h). Requires
immediate human escalation and automated guardrails. Blameless but accountable.
**End of Exam - SRE & Observability**

Answer Key - Beginner Level
| Q | Correct Answer | Explanation |
|---| :--- | :--- |
| 1 | B | Indexer is the core component that licenses/parses/indexes data and serves search requests. Forwarders (A) send,
Search Head (C) serves UI. |
| 2 | C | props.conf handles parsing attributes: TIME_FORMAT, LINE_BREAKER, SHOULD_LINEMERGE etc. indexes.conf
defines indexes, inputs.conf defines monitoring. |
| 3 | D | search is used early and is fast (searches index), where uses eval expressions and filters after field extraction. Best
practice: use search first. |
| 4 | C | 9997 is default receiving port (inputs.conf on indexer: [splunktcp://9997]). 8000 is web UI, 8089 is management port. |
| 5 | B | SPL = Search Processing Language. The pipe | chains commands, e.g., ... | stats count by host. |
| 6 | B | A bucket (hot/warm/cold) contains rawdata/journal.gz (compressed raw events), plus tsidx files, bloomfilters, and
metadata. |

--- PAGE 204 ---
| 7 | C | Knowledge Objects are user-created: Lookups, Event Types, Tags, Field Aliases, Macros, Workflow Actions, Data
Models, etc. |
| 8 | B | stats is a transforming/distributable streaming command. It reduces data at indexers before sending to search head,
improving performance. |
| 9 | A | As of 2024+, Splunk Cloud uses IDAC (Indexer Discovery + Cluster Manager combined) and Federated Search for
Amazon Security Lake / remote Splunk Platform is GA and critical for hybrid search. |
| 10 | B | stats count by user is correct transforming syntax. table just lists fields without aggregation. |

Answer Key - Intermediate Level
| Q | Correct Answer | Explanation |
|---| :--- | :--- |
| 11 | B | Routing to nullQueue is standard index-time filtering to discard noise (e.g., debug logs). REGEX=. matches all; more
specific regex filters selectively. |

--- PAGE 206 ---
| 12 | B | Lifecycle: hot (writable) -> warm (rolled) -> cold (low-speed storage) -> frozen (archived/deleted). Thawed is for
restoring frozen data. Managed by indexes.conf maxHotBuckets, maxWarmDBCount etc. |
| 13 | B | tstats directly queries tsidx files, bypassing rawdata, ~10-100x faster. Requires accelerated data model or enabled
summary. metasearch is deprecated/fast but limited. |
| 14 | B | SmartStore (since 7.2, standard in Splunk Cloud 9.x) decouples compute/storage. CacheManager manages local
cache. Improves scaling, resilience, and cost on S3. |
| 15 | A | Correct syntax: [monitor://path] with lowercase. sourcetype is valid attribute. Universal Forwarder needs outputs.conf
to send to indexer. |
| 16 | B | CIM provides normalized data models (Authentication, Network Traffic, Web etc.) to make content portable across
customers/vendors. Core to ES and SOC operations. |
| 17 | B | eval creates new field with if() condition. where filters. You can also do | where status>=400. case() is better for
multiple conditions. |
| 18 | B | SHC (deployer + 3+ members + captain) for HA for users/search artifacts. Indexer Cluster (Cluster Manager + peers)
for data replication and High Availability via RF/SF. |
| 19 | B | Data Models are knowledge objects enabling Pivot and tstats Acceleration (indexes.conf [datamodel]
acceleration=true). Used heavily in ES. |
| 20 | A | transaction groups events into transactions based on field, useful for sessionization, but very expensive - requires all
events in memory on search head. Prefer stats with values(), earliest()/_time. |

Q21. You have Splunk Enterprise 9.3 with TLS certificates for indexer
cluster. Splunk's new default in 9.x is to enforce certificate verification.
What must you configure to maintain secure Splunk-to-Splunk
communication (S2S) without disruption?
A) Nothing, 9.x disables TLS by default
B) Configure server.conf [sslConfig] sslVerifyServerCert = true and provide valid certs via caCertFile, plus in outputs.conf for
forwarders set sslCommonNameToCheck or update to new sslVerifyServerCert
C) Only enable SSL on port 8000
D) Use third-party certs only on Search Head
Q22. In a large-scale deployment with 50+ Universal Forwarders, how do
you optimize limits.conf and ingestion pipeline to handle parsing queue
blocking?
A) Increase maxKBps in limits.conf to 0
B) Investigate indexing pipeline - parsingQueue full via Monitoring Console; tune props.conf SEDCMD, reduce expensive
regex, use Ingest Actions, increase queueSize in server.conf [queue], or scale to Heavy Forwarder / Edge Processor for
pre-parsing
C) Delete indexes.conf
D) Restart forwarders only
Q23. Which search best implements a behavioral anomaly using SPL with
eventstats and streamstats?
A) index=auth | eventstats avg(duration) as avg stdev(duration) as stdev by user | where duration > avg+3*stdev
B) index=auth | stats count
C) index=auth | transaction user | where duration > 100
D) index=auth | eval avg=100

--- PAGE 207 ---
Q24. What is the role of Splunk Edge Processor (GA 2024) and Ingest
Processor in Splunk Cloud Platform?
A) Same as Universal Forwarder
B) Edge Processor is next-gen stateless data processing at edge/cloud for filtering/masking/routing before indexing,
replacing/augmenting Heavy Forwarder use cases with Pipeline UI and SPL2. Ingest Processor is its Cloud-managed service
for S3-based pipelines.
C) A replacement for Search Head
D) Only for on-prem SmartStore
Q25. Explain workload management in Splunk 9.x.
A) Deprecated since 8.0
B) Workload Management (wlm.conf) allows creating workload pools/rules to allocate CPU/memory to search vs. ingest vs.
ad-hoc roles, preventing runaway searches. Critical for Splunk Cloud Victoria Experience.
C) Only for free license
D) Manages forwarder load
Q26. A customer using Splunk Cloud Platform notes SVC (Splunk Virtual
Compute) license model introduced 2023-2024. What does it meter?
A) Daily indexing volume only
B) Search, Ingest, and Storage virtual compute units - based on actual resource consumption across workloads, replacing
pure ingest-based licensing, with allowances for SVCs
C) Number of users only
D) Number of forwarders
Q27. You need to accelerate a data model for ES. What are acceleration
requirements and trade-offs?
A) datamodels.conf acceleration=true, acceleration.earliest_time, max_concurrent_optimizes plus private data model +
summary range; trade-off is storage overhead (~30% extra) and optimization job load on indexers
B) Only need to enable on search head
C) Acceleration has no cost
D) Acceleration works on all indexes automatically
Q28. How do you securely configure props.conf + transforms.conf for
indexed field extraction vs. search-time?
A) Always use INDEXED_EXTRACTIONS = JSON for all sources
B) Search-time extraction (REPORT- in props.conf) is preferred for flexibility and upgradability; Indexed extraction
(EXTRACT- or INDEXED_EXTRACTIONS, or TRANSFORM with WRITE_META) is for performance or for fields needed for
tstats, but increases license usage and cannot be changed retroactively without re-indexing. Use fields.conf [field]
INDEXED=true for indexed fields.
C) Both are same
D) Search-time not supported in 9.3
Q29. Which SPL2 / federated search scenario is valid for Amazon Security
Lake (ASL) integration in Splunk Cloud 2024-2026?
A) FROM federated:security_lake | where ... via Federated Search for Amazon Security Lake with automatic OCSF mapping
to CIM

B) index=security_lake only
C) | tstats cannot be used with federated
D) Security Lake requires Heavy Forwarder only
Q30. Troubleshooting: Search index=firewall returns incomplete results for
2 of 5 indexers in an indexer cluster. Monitoring Console shows search
factor not met. What steps to diagnose?
A) Reinstall Splunk
B) Check splunkd.log, cluster_info_searchable rest: | rest /services/cluster/master/peers, verify replication factor met, check
bucket fixup tasks, ensure all peers up, splunk show cluster-status, check disk space and indexes.conf ensure replication

Answer Key - Advanced Level
| Q | Correct Answer | Explanation |
|---| :--- | :--- |
| 21 | B | Splunk 9.x hardens TLS by default (TLS 1.2+). For S2S you need sslConfig certs, sslRootCAPath/caCertFile, verify.
Failure to update certs post 9.0 upgrade is common cause of broken forwarder-to-indexer comms. Use splunk btool server list
sslConfig --debug. |
| 22 | B | ParsingQueue blocking often from expensive regex in props/transforms. Use Edge Processor/Ingest Actions to
offload. Check metrics.log. Don't set maxKBps=0 blindly (throttles). |
| 23 | A | eventstats calculates avg/stdev per user without transforming away events, allowing per-event anomaly filter. Classic
UBA pattern. streamstats useful for trending over time. |
| 24 | B | Edge Processor (2024+ GA) & Ingest Processor are strategic for data optimization-at-edge, PII masking, filtering.
Replaces many HF use cases. Configured via UI pipelines with SPL2. Important for cost optimization in SVC licensing. |
| 25 | B | Workload Management replaces old Search Scheduler limits, enables rule-based admission control: e.g., limit
concurrent historically, reserve capacity for ingestion. Configured via wlm.conf + Monitoring Console WLM dashboard. |
| 26 | B | SVC model (2023+) meters Search Compute, Ingest Compute, Storage - aligns to actual compute, not just GB/day.
Encourages search optimization and data optimization with Ingest Actions/Edge Processor. Workload-based pricing. |
| 27 | A | Acceleration creates tsidx summaries on indexers. Requires datamodels.conf settings, private app with data model.
Cost: storage, CPU for summarization, needs careful backfill_time and acceleration.cron_sq. Use Monitoring Console -> Data
Models. |
| 28 | B | Best practice 2024-2026: prefer search-time. Indexed fields increase index size and license count (if extraction
creates extra length) and are immutable. Use for high-performance correlation like ES data model fields. |
| 29 | A | Splunk's native FS for Amazon Security Lake (FSASL) uses federated provider, automatically converts OCSF to
Splunk CIM, allows SPL/SPL2 searches across S3 without ingesting all logs, saving SVC. FedRAMP compliant. |
| 30 | B | Search Factor not met = not enough searchable copies. Diagnose via Cluster Manager REST endpoints, bucket
health, fixup tasks. Common root causes: disk full, indexer down, network partition, manual bucket freeze. |
*End of Exam - Generated for 2024-2026 Splunk Core Certified Power User / Admin / Architect track. Covers Splunk
Enterprise 9.1-9.3, Splunk Cloud Platform Victoria Experience, SmartStore, Edge Processor, SVC licensing, and Federated
Search.*

# Active Directory & LDAP - Comprehensive Certification Exam
Version: 2024-2026 Aligned | Total Questions: 30 | Format: MCQ (4
Options)
Coverage: AD DS, Azure AD / Entra ID, LDAP v3, LDAPS, Kerberos, Group
Policy, Replication, Security Hardening
SECTION A: BEGINNER LEVEL (Questions 1-10)
**Focus:** Core concepts, objects, protocols, and basic administration.
**Q1. What is the primary protocol used by clients to query and modify objects in Active Directory?**
A) SMB
B) LDAP
C) Kerberos
D) DNS
**Q2. In Active Directory, what does the acronym DN stand for?**
A) Domain Namespace
B) Distinguished Name
C) Directory Node
D) Data Network
**Q3. Which TCP port does LDAP use in clear text by default?**
B) 389
C) 636
D) 3268
**Q4. What is the smallest administrative unit in Active Directory that can have Group Policy linked to it?**
A) Site
B) Domain
C) Organizational Unit (OU)
D) Forest
**Q5. Which of the following is NOT a partition in Active Directory?**
A) Domain partition
B) Configuration partition
C) Schema partition
D) Security partition
**Q6. What tool replaced Active Directory Users and Computers (ADUC) as the recommended modern management tool in
Windows Server 2022/2025 and for hybrid environments?**
A) Windows Admin Center (WAC) and Active Directory Administrative Center (ADAC)
B) netsh
C) Server Manager only
D) Ldp.exe
**Q7. In LDAP, what is the root of an LDAP search called?**
A) Relative Distinguished Name (RDN)
B) Base DN
C) Global Catalog

--- PAGE 210 ---
D) Common Name (CN)
**Q8. What authentication protocol does Active Directory use by default for domain authentication within the domain?**
A) NTLMv2
B) OAuth 2.0
C) Kerberos
D) PAP
**Q9. Which object class in Active Directory represents a security principal that can be assigned permissions?**
A) OrganizationalUnit
B) Container
C) User
D) Schema
**Q10. What does LDAPS provide that LDAP does not?**
A) Faster query speed
B) Encryption via TLS/SSL
C) Anonymous binding by default
D) Support for more attributes
Answer Key - Beginner Level
| Q | Correct Answer | Explanation |
|---|----------------|-------------|
| 1 | B) LDAP | LDAP is the core access protocol for AD DS - Lightweight Directory Access Protocol. |
| 2 | B) Distinguished Name | DN uniquely identifies an entry, e.g., CN=John Doe,OU=Users,DC=corp,DC=com |
| 3 | B) 389 | Port 389 for LDAP, 636 for LDAPS, 3268/3269 for Global Catalog. |
| 4 | C) Organizational Unit (OU) | GPOs can be linked to Site, Domain, OU - OU is most granular. |
| 5 | D) Security partition | Real partitions are Domain, Configuration, Schema, Application, and Global Catalog partial RO. |
| 6 | A) Windows Admin Center (WAC) and ADAC | Microsoft pushes ADAC and WAC for modern admin; ADUC is legacy but
still present in RSAT. |
| 7 | B) Base DN | Base DN is starting point for search, e.g., DC=corp,DC=com. |
| 8 | C) Kerberos | Kerberos is default since Windows 2000; NTLM is fallback. |
| 9 | C) User | User, Computer, Group are security principals with SIDs. |
| 10 | B) Encryption via TLS/SSL | LDAPS wraps LDAP in TLS on port 636, required by Microsoft hardening guidance
post-2024. |

Answer Key - Intermediate Level

--- PAGE 212 ---
| Q | Correct Answer | Explanation |
|---|----------------|-------------|
| 11 | B) Push replication triggered by change notification | Intra-site: source DC notifies partners within 15 sec, then pushes.
Inter-site uses pull on schedule (default 180 min). |
| 12 | B) Ldp.exe | Ldp.exe is built-in low-level LDAP client for bind/search/modify. |
| 13 | A) Stores full copy of own domain + partial read-only forest attributes; Port 3268 | GC required for forest-wide searches
and universal group login. 3268 = GC LDAP, 3269 = GC LDAPS. |
| 14 | A) Domain Controller: LDAP Server channel binding token Requirements + LDAP server signing requirements |
Hardening advisory ADV190023 (2020 enforced 2024+). Set to 'When supported' or 'Required'. |
| 15 | A) (&(objectClass=user)(userAccountControl:1.2.840.113556.1.4.803:=2)) | Uses LDAP_MATCHING_RULE_BIT_AND
to check bit flag 2 (ACCOUNTDISABLE). |
| 16 | C) Schema Master and Domain Naming Master for forest-level operations | Adding DC touches forest partitions;
Schema Master + Domain Naming Master must be reachable. RID Master needed for new DC's RID pool. |
| 17 | A) MSAL with Kerberos Cloud Trust for Windows Hello for Business | Modern Entra ID uses MSAL library,
OAuth2/OIDC, Cloud Kerberos Trust eliminates need for line-of-sight to DC in 2024-2026 best practices. |
| 18 | B) It is a constructed / backlink attribute | memberOf is backlink of member; maintained by system, not directly written.
Also not replicated as stored value. |
| 19 | A) Last writer wins based on version number and timestamp | Attribute-level conflict: highest version wins; if tie, latest
timestamp; if still tie, higher DC GUID. |
| 20 | B) DNS SRV records (_ldap._tcp.dc._msdcs.<domain>) | DC Locator uses DNS SRV records plus site-specific records
_ldap._tcp.<sitename>._sites... |

Answer Key - Advanced Level
| Q | Correct Answer | Explanation |
|---|----------------|-------------|
| 21 | A) DS-Replication-Get-Changes and DS-Replication-Get-Changes-All | DCSync requires those extended rights; should
be audited, only Domain Controllers group should have them. Detect via 4662 events. |
| 22 | C) TLS 1.2 | Microsoft security baseline 2024+ deprecates TLS 1.0/1.1. TLS 1.2 minimum required for LDAPS, AD DS,
Entra ID. TLS 1.3 supported in Server 2022/2025. |
| 23 | B) Use LDAP paged search control (RFC 2696) with page size 1000 and DirSync | Best practice: never raise
MaxPageSize (default 1000/5000). Use paged results, VLV, or range retrieval. DirSync / USNChanged for large syncs. |
| 24 | A) Shadow Credentials / Whisker attack | msDS-KeyCredentialLink used for Windows Hello / Key Trust; unprivileged
write allows persistence via PKINIT. Must audit via DS ACL. |

--- PAGE 214 ---
| 25 | A) To allow JIT admin via time-bound membership of shadow principals in privileged groups | PAM in AD uses MIM +
Shadow Principals (objectClass=shadowPrincipal) for time-bound JIT membership with TTL, reducing standing privileged
access. |
| 26 | A) Enforce LDAP Signing + Channel Binding + Extended Protection + Disable NTLM + FAST | CISA/Micro 2025:
Require signing & channel binding, EPA, LDAP over TLS, disable NTLMv1, enforce Kerberos AES, FAST armoring. |
| 27 | A) repadmin /removelingeringobjects + Enable Strict Replication Consistency | Lingering objects removed per DC;
enable Strict Consistency (registry 1) to block replication of lingerers. Tombstone lifetime 180d in new forests since 2003
SP1. |
| 28 | A) (&(objectCategory=person)(objectClass=user)(adminCount=1)(memberOf=CN=Domain
Admins,CN=Users,DC=corp,DC=com)) | adminCount=1 indicates AdminSDHolder protection; combine with memberOf for
DA. Note adminCount alone not sufficient, check ACL inheritance disabled. |
| 29 | A) It stores a GUID derived from objectGUID that is immutable and used as sourceAnchor for immutableID |
mS-DS-ConsistencyGuid used as immutableID anchor prevents soft-match errors, especially during forest migration or Entra
Cloud Sync (2024 best practice uses objectGUID->ConsistencyGuid). |
| 30 | A) DsHeuristics bit 7: Set 0000002 and dSHeuristics 7th char to 2 + disallow anonymous | CIS: Set DsHeuristics fChar
7=2 to block anonymous listing of objects, and ensure no null session access. Also audit via dSHeuristics and LDAP policies:
AllowAnonymous is deprecated. RootDSE anonymous remains per RFC. |

# Cybersecurity - Final Examination
**Course Code: 31_Cybersecurity | Total Questions: 30 | Year: 2024-2026 Aligned | Format: Multiple Choice (4 Options
Each)**
This exam covers foundational principles, modern threats (2024-2026), NIST CSF 2.0, OWASP Top 10 2023, Zero Trust,
Cloud Security, and Advanced Incident Response.
Section A: Beginner Level (Questions 1-10)
**1. What does the CIA Triad in cybersecurity stand for?**
A) Central Intelligence Agency Triad
B) Confidentiality, Integrity, Availability
C) Cyber Intelligence Analysis
D) Control, Inspect, Authenticate
**2. Which of the following is the MOST effective defense against phishing in 2024-2026?**
A) Only using antivirus
B) Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) with phishing-resistant methods like FIDO2/Passkeys
C) Changing wallpapers frequently
D) Disabling firewalls
**3. What type of attack involves tricking a user into revealing credentials via a fake login page?**
A) DDoS
B) Phishing / Credential Harvesting
C) SQL Injection
D) Cross-Site Scripting
**4. What does VPN stand for and what is its primary purpose?**
A) Virtual Private Network - creates an encrypted tunnel over public networks
B) Visual Protocol Node - speeds up graphics
C) Verified Personal Number - verifies identity
D) Virtual Public Network - makes traffic public
**5. Which of the following is an example of a strong password policy aligned with NIST SP 800-63B (2024 update)?**
A) Password: 123456
B) Require 8-char minimum, check against breached password lists, allow long passphrases, no mandatory 90-day expiration
C) Require special characters only
D) Use same password everywhere
**6. What is malware that encrypts victim data and demands payment for decryption called?**
A) Spyware
B) Adware
C) Ransomware
D) Rootkit
**7. What is the purpose of a firewall?**
A) To cool down computers
B) To monitor and control incoming and outgoing network traffic based on security rules
C) To increase internet speed
D) To store backups
**8. Two-Factor Authentication (2FA) is important because:**
A) It makes login slower

--- PAGE 216 ---
B) It adds a second layer of verification beyond password, mitigating password theft
C) It replaces antivirus
D) It only works on iPhones
**9. Which law/regulation enforced from 2024 heavily focuses on data breach notification and protection in the EU?**
A) HIPAA
B) GDPR with 2024 NIS2 Directive requirements for critical sectors
C) DMCA
D) SOX only
**10. What is social engineering?**
A) Building social media apps
B) Manipulating people into breaking security procedures and divulging confidential information
C) Engineering network cables
D) Creating social networks
Answer Key - Beginner Level
1. B - Confidentiality, Integrity, Availability
2. B - Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) with phishing-resistant methods like FIDO2/Passkeys - SMS codes are now
phishable, FIDO2 is gold standard in 2024-26
3. B - Phishing / Credential Harvesting
4. A - Virtual Private Network - creates an encrypted tunnel over public networks
5. B - Require 8-char minimum, check against breached password lists, allow long passphrases, no mandatory 90-day
expiration
6. C - Ransomware - e.g., LockBit, BlackCat/ALPHV dominant in 2024
7. B - To monitor and control incoming and outgoing network traffic based on security rules
8. B - It adds a second layer of verification beyond password, mitigating password theft
9. B - GDPR with 2024 NIS2 Directive requirements for critical sectors
10. B - Manipulating people into breaking security procedures and divulging confidential information

**21. In 2024, a new critical vulnerability CVE-2024-3400 (CVSS 10.0) affected which vendor's firewall OS, exploited as a

A) Palo Alto Networks PAN-OS GlobalProtect
B) Microsoft Windows 11
C) Cisco IOS
D) Fortinet FortiGate only

25. B - It hooks into kernel via eBPF to provide real-time syscall observability, container-aware detection, and prevents race
