# HashiCorp Vault Certified Associate & Professional - Complete Exam
**Course Code: 32_Vault | Updated: 2024-2026 (Vault 1.15 - 1.18 / HCP Vault)**
Part 1: Beginner Level (10 Questions)
Q1. What is the primary purpose of HashiCorp Vault?
A) Container orchestration and scheduling
B) Secrets management, encryption as a service, and identity-based access control
C) Monitoring infrastructure logs and metrics
D) Version control for Terraform code
Q2. In Vault, what is the sealed state?
A) Vault is running but all data is encrypted and inaccessible; master key shards are required to unseal
B) Vault is stopped and needs systemctl start
C) Vault is in read-only mode after a network partition
D) Vault license has expired
Q3. By default, which storage backend is used when you run vault server
-dev?
A) Consul
B) Raft integrated storage
C) In-memory storage
D) PostgreSQL
Q4. What is the difference between KV Secrets Engine v1 and v2?
A) v1 supports MFA, v2 does not
B) v1 is for certificates, v2 is for passwords only
C) v2 supports versioning, check-and-set, undelete, and soft-delete; v1 does not
D) There is no difference, they are aliases
Q5. Which command creates or updates a Vault policy?
A) vault policy write my-policy policy.hcl
B) vault write policy my-policy @policy.hcl
C) vault auth enable policy
D) vault policy create my-policy
Q6. What is a Vault token?
A) A Kubernetes manifest
B) An authentication token that carries identity and policy membership; used for all API requests via X-Vault-Token header
C) A static API key that never expires
D) A TLS certificate issued by PKI engine

--- PAGE 221 ---
Q7. Which default auth method is enabled automatically on a fresh Vault
dev server?
A) AppRole
B) Kubernetes
C) Token
D) GitHub
Q8. What does vault status return when Vault is sealed?
A) Sealed: true, HA Mode, and seal type (shamir, awskms, etc.)
B) Error 500
C) List of all secrets
D) Active node count
Q9. Which path is the default for KV v2 secrets?
A) kv/
B) secret/ (in dev mode) or kv mount configured with type kv-v2
C) sys/
D) auth/
Q10. What is the purpose of VAULT_ADDR environment variable?
A) To set where the Vault CLI/API client should connect to the Vault server
B) To set the storage address
C) To configure auto-unseal
D) To define the audit log path
**Answer Key - Beginner:**
1. B - Vault is a secrets management solution providing secure storage, dynamic secrets, data encryption, and identity-based
access.
2. A - When sealed, Vault knows where data is but cannot decrypt it. Requires unseal keys or auto-unseal recovery.
3. C - Dev mode uses in-memory only, non-persistent, root token = 'root'.
4. C - KV v2 (added in 0.10) provides versioned secrets with metadata, soft delete. KV v1 is unversioned.
5. A - Correct syntax is vault policy write <name> <file.hcl>. File contains path {} blocks.
6. B - Tokens are core client auth mechanism. Service vs batch tokens (1.15+).
7. C - Token auth method is always enabled and cannot be disabled. Dev server also enables token only by default in newer
versions; older dev docs showed userpass enabled but current default is token only.
8. A - vault status shows sealed/unsealed, seal type, storage type, HA mode, cluster name, version.
9. B - Dev server mounts kv-v2 at secret/. In production you typically mount vault secrets enable -path=kv kv-v2.
10. A - Example: export VAULT_ADDR='https://vault.example.com:8200'
Part 2: Intermediate Level (10 Questions)
Q11. In AppRole auth method, what is the security model?
A) role_id is like username (non-secret) and secret_id is like password (secret); best practice is different delivery mechanisms
B) Both role_id and secret_id are public
C) AppRole is only for human users
D) AppRole requires LDAP to function

--- PAGE 222 ---
Q12. How does Vault's lease and renewal system work for dynamic
secrets?
A) All secrets are static forever
B) Secrets issued have a lease ID, TTL, and max TTL; clients must renew via vault lease renew or API before TTL expiry,
otherwise Vault revokes them
C) Leases are only for KV secrets
D) Vault automatically renews forever without client action
Q13. What is Response Wrapping?
A) Compressing the Vault binary
B) A method where Vault stores a single-use response in its cubbyhole and returns a wrapping token; recipient unwraps to
get real secret, protecting against eavesdropping
C) Wrapping audit logs in JSON
D) Encrypting Raft snapshots
Q14. You have a policy: path "secret/data/prod/*" { capabilities = ["read"] }.
A user tries vault kv put secret/prod/api key=123. Why does it fail?
A) Policy only grants read, not create/update. KV v2 requires create, update capabilities on secret/data/prod/* and often read
on secret/metadata/*
B) Vault doesn't support wildcard
C) User must be root
D) Path should be secret/prod/* without data
Q15. What does the Transit secrets engine provide?
A) Database credential rotation
B) Encryption as a Service (EaaS) - encrypt/decrypt data without storing it in Vault; key management, rotation,
bring-your-own-key
C) SSH key signing
D) Kubernetes authentication
Q16. Which statement about Vault Agent is true for Vault 1.15+?
A) Vault Agent is deprecated
B) Agent can handle auto-auth, caching, templating (consul-template syntax via agent templates), and API proxy; reduces
token sprawl and is recommended for machines
C) Agent only works with AWS
D) Agent stores secrets in plaintext on disk by default
Q17. What is the Cubbyhole secrets engine?
A) A global secret store
B) A per-token isolated secret store for the token's lifetime; no one else including root can read it (except via wrapping). Used
for response wrapping and secure introductions
C) Same as KV v2
D) An audit device
Q18. When configuring Kubernetes auth method, what does Vault
validate?

--- PAGE 223 ---
A) The Kubernetes ServiceAccount JWT by calling TokenReview API against K8s API server using configured service
account token
B) Vault checks Docker Hub credentials
C) Vault checks Consul ACL
D) Only checks username/password
Q19. What is the correct way to enable audit logging in production?
A) vault audit enable file file_path=/var/log/vault-audit.log - audit devices must be enabled; at least one file or syslog device is
required for compliance, and logs are raw but can be hashed with log_raw=false
B) Audit is enabled by default
C) Audit logs are stored in Raft automatically
D) vault secrets enable audit
Q20. For PKI secrets engine, what happens when you vault write
pki/roles/web-server allow_any_name=true max_ttl=72h?
A) It creates a role that can issue certificates for any CN/SAN with max TTL 72 hours; leaf certs still constrained by ttl
parameter in request
B) It deletes the PKI engine
C) It generates a root CA immediately
D) It only allows web-server hostnames
**Answer Key - Intermediate:**
11. A - role_id is semi-public identifier, secret_id is secret credential. Pull model requires secure delivery of secret_id via
separate trusted orchestrator.
12. B - Lease lifecycle is core to dynamic secrets (DB, AWS, PKI). KV v2 can also have optional TTL but not leased.
13. B - vault kv get -wrap-ttl=60s secret/... returns wrapping token. Unwrap: vault unwrap <token> or vault unwrap -field= .
Prevents clear text over wire.
14. A - KV v2 API split: data path is secret/data/..., metadata is secret/metadata/.... Writing needs create+update.
15. B - Transit: vault write -f transit/keys/my-key, then transit/encrypt/my-key, transit/decrypt/my-key. Never stores data, only
keys. Supports key rotation, rewrap, convergent encryption.
16. B - Since Vault 1.11+, agent is mature with auto_auth { method { type=approle/kubernetes... } }, cache {
use_auto_auth_token=true }, template {} blocks.
17. B - Cubbyhole path is cubbyhole/. Tokens see only their own cubbyhole. Data dies when token expires.
18. A - Config: vault write auth/kubernetes/config kubernetes_host=... token_reviewer_jwt=.... Role binds service_account
names/namespaces.
19. A - Audit devices log every request/response (excluding sensitive HMAC'd values). Multiple audit devices allowed. Best
practice: enable file audit before production traffic.
20. A - PKI roles define constraints: allow_subdomains, allowed_domains, allow_any_name, key_type, key_bits,
enforce_hostnames. pki/issue/<role> issues leaf.

Q21. You are migrating from Shamir seal to AWS KMS Auto-Unseal with

A) Stop all nodes, edit config, restart
B) Use seal migration: add new seal stanza with disabled=false, old with disabled=false, set api_addr and run vault operator
unseal -migrate following docs; or for 1.16+, vault operator seal migration status workflow. Requires one unseal with old and
new mechanism

C) Auto-unseal migration is impossible
D) Just change config, Vault auto-detects
Q22. What is the difference between Disaster Recovery (DR) Replication
and Performance Replication (Enterprise 2024)?
A) DR replicates static data for failover (standby cluster, limited API), Performance replicates for scaling reads/writes; since
1.14+, Performance secondary can be promoted to primary via vault write -f sys/replication/performance/primary/promote
B) No difference
C) DR is OSS, Performance is Enterprise
D) DR replication replicates only dynamic secrets
Q23. In Vault Enterprise Namespaces (2024-2026), which statement is
accurate?
A) Namespaces provide isolated environments with their own policies, auth methods, and secret engines; root namespace
admin can create child namespaces. Path becomes ns1/ns2/secret/... and tokens are namespace-bound. Since 1.14, API
X-Vault-Namespace header.
B) Namespaces share all policies globally

Q24. High Availability with Raft Integrated Storage in Vault 1.18: A 3-node
cluster loses 1 node. What happens and what is quorum?
A) Cluster continues to serve, Raft tolerates (n-1)/2 failures; 3 nodes needs quorum 2. Leader election via Raft. vault operator
raft list-peers shows peers. Performance standbys forward writes to active
B) Cluster stops immediately
C) All nodes become active
D) Raft requires 5 nodes minimum
Q25. Explain Identity Entities and Groups vs direct token policies. Why use

A) Identity ties multiple auth method logins (e.g. GitHub + LDAP same user) to single Entity with merged policies via Entity +

B) Identity is legacy
C) Entities are same as tokens
D) Identity only works with Userpass
Q26. A compliance requirement: Production database credentials rotated
every 24h, apps never restarted manually. Which architecture (2024 best

A) Static KV secrets
B) Dynamic Database Secrets Engine with rotation vault write database/config/mydb ... rotation_period=24h + static roles via

C) Hardcode credentials
D) Use AppRole with infinite TTL

Q27. Vault Agent template vs vault proxy template behavior and Sentinel
EGP/RGP (Enterprise) - select correct advanced security feature:
A) Agent templates use consul-template syntax {{ with secret "secret/data/..." }}{{ .Data.data.key }}{{ end }} to render files;
Sentinel policies (RGP/EGP) allow advanced logic: main = rule { ... } to restrict CIDR, MFA enforcement, allowed paths; EGP
runs on every request, RGP after ACLs. Since Vault 1.16, MFA with mfa login enforcement
B) Templates are not supported
C) Sentinel is OSS
D) Sentinel replaces HCL policies
Q28. You suspect a Vault node is running out of memory due to large audit
logs and large lease counts. Which advanced tuning would you apply in
Vault 1.17-1.18?
A) Increase max_lease_ttl, default_lease_ttl to infinite, disable audit
B) Use max_request_duration, max_request_size, disable_mlock only
C) Enable batch tokens for high-volume ephemeral workloads (non-renewable, non-persistent, 32k byte limited), configure
cache_size for auth cache, tune audit { file { ... } } with log_raw=false and hmac_accessor=true, reduce lease count via
sys/leases/revoke-force and shorten TTLs, enable disable_cache=false for agent, monitor sys/metrics Prometheus
D) Restart Vault daily
Q29. Zero-Trust deployment: Kubernetes pods need Vault secrets without
long-lived tokens, across 2 datacenters. Choose correct advanced pattern
for 2025-2026.
A) Hardcoded root token in ENV
B) JWT auth with bound service accounts plus Vault Agent Injector or Secrets Store CSI Driver, Short-lived tokens (TTL 5m),
Performance Replication for second DC with local mount for auth/kubernetes, use token_bound_cidrs, token_explicit_max_ttl,
response wrapping for Agent auto-auth
C) Use UI token only
D) Share same token for all pods
Q30. Vault 1.15+ introduced support for Managed PKI and Improved UI,
plus what major change in licensing and storage?
A) Vault moved to BSL license in 2023; Raft is now recommended production storage over Consul; HCP Vault Dedicated now
supports tiered namespaces, managed audit, and automated upgrades. Vault 1.15+ removed support for mlock on Windows,
added PKCS#11 auto-unseal improvements and OpenAPI 3.x spec
B) Vault is now MIT only
C) Consul is mandatory again
D) Vault no longer supports Kubernetes
**Answer Key - Advanced:**
21. B - Seal migration workflow documented: https://developer.hashicorp.com/vault/docs/concepts/seal#seal-migration .
Requires both seals defined, run migration command. Since 1.16 supports vault operator unseal -migrate.
22. A - DR: disaster recovery copy, cannot serve reads/writes until promoted (limited /sys endpoints). Performance: active on
secondaries for reads, can have local mounts (auth). Enterprise only.
23. A - Namespaces isolation: each has own sys/. Root token in child cannot escape. Path API: X-Vault-Namespace header
or namespace in path for API. Available only Enterprise/HCP.
24. A - Raft quorum = floor(n/2)+1. 3 nodes tolerates 1 failure, 5 tolerates 2. vault operator raft autopilot state. Performance
standby nodes serve reads but forward writes.

--- PAGE 226 ---
25. A - Entity model: unique identity, aliases per auth mount. Identity groups internal vs external. External maps from auth
method claims (groups from OIDC). Entity alias count visible in identity/entity/id/.
26. B - Database engine database/roles/* dynamic, database/static-roles/* auto rotation. Vault Agent template renders env file
and can trigger reload via command. CSI driver syncs K8s secret.
27. A - Agent template rendering + Prometheus metrics. Sentinel: requires license, written in Sentinel language, RGP
enforced at policy check, EGP enforced pre-policy. Example: import "sockaddr"; main = rule {
sockaddr.is_contained("10.0.0.0/8", http.remote_address) }
28. C - Batch tokens: lightweight, encrypted blob, no storage. Service tokens persisted. Also need disable_mlock false in
prod, tune api_addr, cluster_addr, limit max_lease_ttl=768h. Metrics: vault.token.count.by..., vault.audit.log.request.
29. B - Modern zero-trust: Kubernetes auth, short-lived, Agent Injector via annotations vault.hashicorp.com/agent-inject, CSI
driver, replication. bound_service_account_names, bound_service_account_namespaces. No static secrets.
30. A - Key context 2024-2026: License BSL (HashiCorp changed 2023), Raft integrated storage is default production
backend since 1.11, HCP Vault Dedicated is SaaS evolution, Plugin ecosystem, removal of old storage backends. Vault 1.15
added ACME support for PKI, 1.16 Secrets Sync to AWS/GCP/Azure etc, 1.17+ WIF and improved events system.

# Course Title: Playwright & Cypress - End-to-End Testing Mastery (2024-2026)
Exam Overview
- Total Questions: 30
- Format: Multiple Choice (4 options each)
- Levels: Beginner (Q1-10), Intermediate (Q11-20), Advanced (Q21-30)
- Stack: Playwright 1.40+ (2024-2026 features), Cypress 13.x/14.x
- Time: 90 minutes | Passing: 70%
Part 1: Beginner Level (10 Questions)
**Q1. What is the primary architectural difference between Playwright and Cypress regarding browser execution?**

B) Cypress runs inside the browser's event loop (same run-loop), Playwright controls browsers via WebSocket/CDP
out-of-process
C) Both run outside the browser using Selenium protocol
D) Playwright only supports Chromium, Cypress supports all browsers
**Q2. In Playwright, which command initializes a new project with official test runner?**

D) Gets data from localStorage

D) page.locator('#submitBtn')
**Q5. How do you handle auto-waiting in Cypress?**
A) You must manually add page.waitForTimeout(5000) before every action
B) Cypress automatically retries commands and assertions before failing

D) Auto-waiting is not supported in Cypress
**Q6. What is the default test runner file pattern for Playwright?**
A) **/*.cy.js
B) **/*.e2e.js
C) **/*.spec.js or **/*.test.js and searched via playwright.config.ts testDir
D) cypress/e2e/**/*.js only
**Q7. In Cypress, how do you intercept and mock a network request in Cypress 13+?**
A) cy.route()
B) cy.intercept('GET', '/api/users', { fixture: 'users.json' })

--- PAGE 228 ---
C) page.route() from Playwright
D) cy.mock()
**Q8. What does test.only do in Playwright Test?**
A) Skips all tests
B) Runs only the marked test(s) - useful for focused debugging
C) Marks test as flaky
D) Deletes other tests
**Q9. Which assertion library is built into Playwright Test runner by default?**

D) Should.js
**Q10. In Cypress, where is baseUrl configured?**

B) In cypress.config.js / cypress.config.ts under e2e.baseUrl
C) In .env file only

Answer Key - Beginner
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| Q1 | B | Cypress runs IN browser; Playwright uses out-of-process automation via CDP/BiDi/WebSocket giving multi-tab,
multi-origin, multi-browser context support. |
| Q2 | B | npm init playwright@latest is official initializer (also npm init playwright@latest -- --g pattern). Creates config,
example tests, GitHub workflow. |
| Q3 | B | Cypress commands are async, retried, DOM-querying. It automatically retries get until timeout (default 4000ms). |
| Q4 | C | Playwright best practice 2024-2026: Use getByRole, getByLabel, getByText, getByTestId for accessibility-first,
resilient locators. |
| Q5 | B | Core Cypress feature: automatic retry-ability for commands and assertions. No manual waits needed for most
cases. |
| Q6 | C | Playwright default testDir is ./tests or ./e2e and matches *.spec.ts/js etc, configurable in playwright.config. |
| Q7 | B | cy.route() was v6 and deprecated; cy.intercept() is standard since Cypress 6+ and current in 13/14. |
| Q8 | B | test.only focuses execution. In CI it will fail if test.only left behind if forbidOnly is set. |
| Q9 | B | Playwright Test bundles expect with web-first assertions that auto-retry (e.g., await expect(locator).toBeVisible()). |
| Q10 | B | Configured in cypress.config.js/ts: e2e: { baseUrl: 'http://localhost:3000' } |

Answer Key - Intermediate
| Q | Answer | Explanation |

Q7. Which of the following is the correct way to define a function that takes
an i32 and returns an i32?
A) fn add(x: i32) -> i32 { x + 1 }
B) func add(x: i32): i32 { return x + 1; }
C) fn add(x: i32) => i32 { x + 1 }
D) def add(x: i32) -> i32 { x + 1 }

A) The GC
B) The variable that holds it; only one owner at a time
C) The function that created it forever
D) All references share ownership equally

A) Array [T; N]
B) Vec<T>
C) Slice &[T]
D) Tuple
Q10. What does & mean in let r = &x;?
A) Creates a mutable copy
B) Creates an immutable reference (borrows)
C) Dereferences x
D) Moves x
Answer Key - Beginner
1. A) i32 - Rust defaults to i32 for integers
2. A) mut - e.g., let mut x = 5;
3. B) Compile-time error: use of moved value - Ownership rule prevents use after move
4. B) println! - print! does not add newline
5. A) let s = "hello".to_string(); - Also String::from("hello") is valid; option C is incorrect syntax
6. B) Checks code for errors without producing an executable - Faster than cargo build
7. A) fn add(x: i32) -> i32 { x + 1 } - Correct Rust function syntax
8. B) The variable that holds it; only one owner at a time - Core ownership principle
9. B) Vec<T> - Growable heap-allocated vector
10. B) Creates an immutable reference (borrows) - & creates a borrow, &mut for mutable borrow

Q18. What does #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)] do?

Q19. Consider: let x = vec![1,2,3].into_iter().filter(|n| n % 2 ==
0).collect::<Vec<_>>(); What does collect need?
A) Type annotation or turbofish to know target collection
B) Nothing, it always returns Vec

Q20. What is the purpose of Arc<Mutex<T>>?
A) Single-threaded mutable sharing
B) Thread-safe shared ownership with interior mutability across threads
C) To make T Copy
D) To avoid borrowing
Answer Key - Intermediate
11. B) String is owned, growable heap data; &str is borrowed slice view
12. B) Because you cannot have mutable and immutable borrows at same time - Borrow checker rule: either one mutable OR
any number of immutable
13. B) Send - Sync is for references that can be shared between threads
14. B) A type that may be Some(T) or None, for explicit null handling - Rust has no null
15. A) ? unwraps Ok or returns Err early from function returning Result - Also works with Option returning None early
16. B) To describe how long references are valid so borrow checker can verify them - Lifetimes are compile-time only
17. A) Native async fn in traits stabilized in Rust 1.75 (Dec 2023) and RPITIT improvements in 1.79+ - Part of 2024-2025
stabilization: async fn in traits without async_trait macro
18. B) Auto-generates implementations for those traits via procedural macro
19. A) Type annotation or turbofish to know target collection - collect is generic over FromIterator
20. B) Thread-safe shared ownership with interior mutability across threads - Arc = Atomic Reference Counted, Mutex for
mutability

Answer Key - Advanced
21. A) Box<dyn Trait> is dynamic dispatch (vtable at runtime), impl Trait is static dispatch (monomorphized) - dyn = dynamic,
impl = static, affects binary size and performance
22. A) Error::source() has always existed; in 2024+ the #[diagnostic] and improved Error reporting in
#[feature(error_generic_member_access)] for provide() - And #[expect(lint)] and detailed error diagnostics stabilized
23. A) Pin prevents moving a value in memory after it is pinned, required for intrusive futures - Self-referential futures need
stable address
24. A) F must work for any lifetime 'a, not just one specific lifetime - HRTB means higher-ranked, works for all lifetimes
25. A) It became the default edition with RPITIT capturing precise lifetimes, gen blocks as replacement for gen keyword
evolution, and let chains improvements - 2024 Edition released Feb 2025 with Rust 1.85; let chains stabilized in 1.64, but
2024 edition refines tail handling and RPITIT lifetime capture rules
26. A) To safely handle uninitialized memory without UB, replacing deprecated mem::uninitialized() - MaybeUninit is
union-like safe wrapper
27. B) UB: writing to immutable bytes &T is undefined behavior even via raw pointer if original was immutable - Cannot
mutate through raw pointer derived from shared ref to immutable binding; violates aliasing rules
28. A) Clone on Write - can hold either borrowed or owned data to avoid unnecessary clone - Cow::Borrowed(&str) or
Cow::Owned(String)
29. B) Rust std only provides async syntax and poll model; external executors like Tokio are needed to run futures, with Tokio
being dominant in 2024-2026 - No runtime in stdlib by design
30. B) const fn capabilities have expanded significantly (e.g., const traits, mutable refs in const fn stabilized in 1.83+, let
patterns, &mut in const) but still have restrictions; const generics support complex expressions via generic_const_exprs still
incomplete but const eval much more powerful - Shows 2024-2026 progression
End of Exam

# Course Title: SQL & Databases - Comprehensive Examination
**Course Code:** SQL-DB-36

SECTION A: BEGINNER LEVEL (Questions 1-10)
**Instructions:** Choose the correct answer from 4 options.
**Q1. Which SQL statement is used to retrieve data from a database?**
A) GET
B) SELECT
C) FETCH
D) RETRIEVE
**Q2. Which of the following is a primary key characteristic?**
A) Can contain NULL values
B) Can have duplicate values
C) Uniquely identifies each row and cannot be NULL
D) Is optional for every table
**Q3. What does SQL stand for?**
A) Structured Query Language
B) Standard Query Logic
C) System Query Language
D) Sequential Query Language
**Q4. Which clause is used to filter results in a SELECT statement?**
A) HAVING
B) WHERE
C) FILTER
D) CONDITION
**Q5. Which data type is used to store variable-length strings in most modern RDBMS (PostgreSQL, MySQL, SQL Server)?**
A) CHAR
B) TEXT ONLY
C) VARCHAR
D) STRING
**Q6. Which command is used to add a new row to a table?**
A) ADD ROW
B) INSERT INTO
C) APPEND
D) ADD RECORD
**Q7. What is the default sorting order of ORDER BY?**
A) DESC
B) ASC (Ascending)

--- PAGE 247 ---
C) RANDOM
D) No default, must specify
**Q8. Which join returns all rows from the left table and matched rows from the right table?**
A) INNER JOIN
B) LEFT JOIN (LEFT OUTER JOIN)
C) RIGHT JOIN
D) FULL JOIN
**Q9. Which function is used to count the number of rows in SQL?**
A) COUNT()
B) TOTAL()
C) NUMBER()
D) SUM()
**Q10. Which constraint ensures that all values in a column satisfy a specific condition?**
A) UNIQUE
B) NOT NULL
C) CHECK
D) DEFAULT
Answer Key - Beginner Level
| Question | Correct Answer | Explanation |
|----------|---|---|
| Q1 | B) SELECT | SELECT is the standard DQL command to retrieve data. |
| Q2 | C) Uniquely identifies each row and cannot be NULL | Primary Key must be UNIQUE and NOT NULL. |
| Q3 | A) Structured Query Language | Official full form. |
| Q4 | B) WHERE | WHERE filters rows before grouping; HAVING filters after GROUP BY. |
| Q5 | C) VARCHAR | VARCHAR(n) stores variable-length strings; CHAR is fixed-length. |
| Q6 | B) INSERT INTO | Syntax: INSERT INTO table_name VALUES (...). |
| Q7 | B) ASC (Ascending) | ORDER BY defaults to ASC if not specified. |
| Q8 | B) LEFT JOIN | LEFT JOIN returns all left rows + matching right rows, NULLs for non-matches. |
| Q9 | A) COUNT() | COUNT(*) counts rows including NULLs. |
| Q10 | C) CHECK | CHECK constraint validates condition like CHECK (age >= 18). |

B) WHERE filters groups, HAVING filters rows
C) WHERE filters individual rows before GROUP BY, HAVING filters groups after GROUP BY

**Q13. Which statement about indexes is TRUE as of 2024-2026 best practices?**
A) Indexes always slow down SELECT queries
B) Indexes speed up reads but can slow down INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE and use extra storage
C) You should create an index on every column
D) Primary keys are never indexed automatically
**Q14. Consider transaction isolation. Which anomaly does REPEATABLE READ prevent that READ COMMITTED does
not?**
A) Dirty Read
B) Non-repeatable read
C) Phantom read is fully prevented in all DBs
D) Both prevent same anomalies
**Q15. What will this query do?
SELECT department, AVG(salary) FROM employees GROUP BY department HAVING AVG(salary) > 60000;

B) Shows departments where average salary exceeds 60000

**Q16. Which is correct about NULL handling?**
A) NULL = NULL returns TRUE
B) WHERE column = NULL correctly finds NULLs
C) WHERE column IS NULL correctly finds NULLs, and COUNT(column) ignores NULLs while COUNT(*) does not

**Q17. What does normalization (3NF) primarily aim to achieve?**
A) Increase data redundancy
B) Reduce data redundancy and improve data integrity by removing transitive dependencies
C) Speed up all queries regardless of cost
D) Merge tables into one large table
**Q18. What is the result of this query in PostgreSQL / MySQL 8+?
SELECT * FROM employees ORDER BY salary DESC LIMIT 5;
A) 5 lowest paid employees
B) 5 highest paid employees
C) All employees sorted, limited to 5 random
D) Syntax error, should use TOP
**Q19. Which is true about FOREIGN KEY?**
A) It must reference PRIMARY KEY or UNIQUE key in another (or same) table and enforces referential integrity
B) Foreign key can contain any value
C) A table can have no foreign keys but only one is ever allowed
D) Foreign keys automatically create duplicate data
**Q20. You have a table orders (id, customer_id, order_date, amount). How to find customers who placed more than 3 orders
in 2024?**
A) SELECT customer_id FROM orders WHERE COUNT(*) > 3 AND YEAR(order_date)=2024
B) SELECT customer_id FROM orders WHERE order_date BETWEEN '2024-01-01' AND '2024-12-31' GROUP BY
customer_id HAVING COUNT(*) > 3
C) SELECT customer_id FROM orders GROUP BY customer_id WHERE COUNT > 3
D) SELECT customer_id FROM orders HAVING COUNT(*) > 3

--- PAGE 249 ---
Answer Key - Intermediate Level
| Question | Correct Answer | Explanation |
|----------|---|---|
| Q11 | B) All departments with employee count, including 0 for departments with no employees | LEFT JOIN preserves all
Departments. COUNT(e.id) will be 0 where no match. |
| Q12 | C) WHERE filters individual rows before GROUP BY, HAVING filters groups after GROUP BY | Standard SQL
execution order: FROM -> WHERE -> GROUP BY -> HAVING -> SELECT. |
| Q13 | B) Indexes speed up reads but can slow down INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE and use extra storage | Indexes are B-Tree
structures; trade-off is write overhead. |
| Q14 | B) Non-repeatable read | REPEATABLE READ prevents dirty and non-repeatable reads. Phantom read prevention
varies (PostgreSQL/MySQL InnoDB prevent phantom via MVCC/next-key locking). |
| Q15 | B) Shows departments where average salary exceeds 60000 | GROUP BY + HAVING filters aggregated groups. |
| Q16 | C) WHERE column IS NULL correctly finds NULLs, and COUNT(column) ignores NULLs while COUNT(*) does not |
NULL is unknown; use IS NULL. COUNT(col) skips NULLs by SQL standard. |
| Q17 | B) Reduce data redundancy and improve data integrity by removing transitive dependencies | 3NF removes columns
not dependent on key (transitive). |
| Q18 | B) 5 highest paid employees | ORDER BY DESC sorts high to low; LIMIT 5 takes top 5. TOP is SQL Server syntax. |
| Q19 | A) It must reference PRIMARY KEY or UNIQUE key in another (or same) table and enforces referential integrity | FK
ensures value exists in referenced table. |
| Q20 | B) SELECT customer_id FROM orders WHERE order_date BETWEEN '2024-01-01' AND '2024-12-31' GROUP BY
customer_id HAVING COUNT(*) > 3 | Correct pattern: filter rows by date in WHERE, then group and filter groups with
HAVING. |

**Q25. For a large table with 100M rows, column status has only 3 distinct values (low cardinality). Which indexing strategy is
generally BAD for OLTP performance in 2024-2026?**
A) Creating a B-Tree index on status alone for filtering WHERE status='X' when selectivity is low (~33% rows)
B) Creating a composite index (status, created_at) where query filters on both
C) Creating a partial/filtered index WHERE status = 'PENDING' in PostgreSQL / SQL Server filtered index
D) Using BRIN index in PostgreSQL for naturally ordered data
**Q26. Explain EXPLAIN ANALYZE in PostgreSQL. What does it do?**
A) Only shows estimated plan without execution
B) Executes the query and shows actual execution plan with real timings and row counts (EXPLAIN alone shows estimates)

D) Deletes the query plan cache
**Q27. Consider deadlock scenario. Transaction A locks row 1 then wants row 2, Transaction B locks row 2 then wants row 1.
How do modern DBMS (PostgreSQL, MySQL InnoDB, SQL Server) handle this in 2024?**
A) Both transactions wait forever
B) Deadlock detector aborts one transaction with deadlock error code, requiring app retry
C) Database merges both transactions
D) Rows are automatically unlocked after 1 second
**Q28. What is the purpose of COALESCE() and how is it different from ISNULL() / IFNULL() in 2024 standards?**
A) No difference
B) COALESCE() is ANSI standard, accepts multiple arguments returning first non-NULL; ISNULL() is SQL Server specific (2
args), IFNULL() is MySQL specific
C) COALESCE only works with numbers
D) ISNULL is ANSI standard
**Q29. You have to design for write-heavy time-series IoT data (2024-2026 context). Which is most appropriate?**
A) Highly normalized RDBMS with many foreign keys and secondary indexes on every column
B) Use TimescaleDB extension for PostgreSQL or partitioned tables with BRIN index, minimal secondary indexes,
compression and retention policies
C) Use SELECT * with no WHERE clause always
D) Store all data in single VARCHAR column
**Q30. Given:
WITH RECURSIVE org AS (
SELECT id, manager_id, name FROM employees WHERE manager_id IS NULL
UNION ALL
SELECT e.id, e.manager_id, e.name FROM employees e JOIN org o ON e.manager_id = o.id
) SELECT * FROM org;
What does this query demonstrate?**
A) A syntax error - recursion not allowed in SQL
B) Recursive CTE to traverse hierarchical/org chart data
C) Simple aggregation
D) Cross join producing cartesian product
Answer Key - Advanced Level
| Question | Correct Answer | Explanation |
|----------|---|---|
| Q21 | B) SELECT dept, name, DENSE_RANK() OVER (PARTITION BY dept ORDER BY salary DESC) | DENSE_RANK()
gives no gaps after tie (1,2,2,3). RANK() would give gaps (1,2,2,4). PARTITION BY needed per dept. ROW_NUMBER would
not handle ties. |

--- PAGE 251 ---
| Q22 | B) They improve readability, allow recursion, and can be materialized or inlined by optimizer | PostgreSQL 12+ allows
MATERIALIZED/NOT MATERIALIZED hint. Major benefit is readability/maintainability + recursion. |
| Q23 | B) UPDATE tbl SET counter = counter + 1 (atomic) or using SELECT ... FOR UPDATE in a transaction, or optimistic
locking with version column | Atomic UPDATE is safe at database level. Pessimistic locking (FOR UPDATE) or optimistic
locking prevents lost update anomaly. |
| Q24 | B) Equivalent to department IN ('Engineering','Sales') | ANY with ARRAY is PostgreSQL idiom equivalent to IN list. |
| Q25 | A) Creating a B-Tree index on status alone for filtering WHERE status='X' when selectivity is low (~33% rows) | Low
selectivity index (33% of rows) leads to full scan anyway; index overhead wasted. Partial/composite indexes are better.
Modern optimizers often ignore such low-selectivity indexes. |
| Q26 | B) Executes the query and shows actual execution plan with real timings and row counts | EXPLAIN shows estimated
cost; EXPLAIN ANALYZE actually runs query (or EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS) in modern PG). |
| Q27 | B) Deadlock detector aborts one transaction with deadlock error code, requiring app retry | All major RDBMS have
deadlock detection; they choose a victim (usually lowest cost) to abort with error 1213 (MySQL) or 40P01 (Postgres). |
| Q28 | B) COALESCE() is ANSI standard, accepts multiple arguments returning first non-NULL; ISNULL() is SQL Server
specific (2 args), IFNULL() is MySQL specific | COALESCE is portable and variadic per SQL:2003+. |
| Q29 | B) Use TimescaleDB extension for PostgreSQL or partitioned tables with BRIN index, minimal secondary indexes,
compression and retention policies | Time-series best practice 2024-2026: hypertables, partitioning by time, compression, few
indexes. |
| Q30 | B) Recursive CTE to traverse hierarchical/org chart data | WITH RECURSIVE is standard (SQL:1999) for hierarchies,
org charts, bill-of-materials. |
